### What the change does

`converge_grant_subtree` resolved the parent scope through `resolve_scope_current`, which answers a superseded verdict with the pointer consult and returns the re-pointed ref. The mint and the tail rebuilt the ref from `parent.identity` instead, so the production promote and interior re-seal ran under a name the parked scope source does not answer to and refused with `Rejected` after the convergence pass had already published.

A re-point makes the whole `ParentScopePlan` stale, not only its label, because the tail re-seals the parent root from the plan's own seeds, commitment, ledger and child index. The command now refuses with the new `CreateGrantError::ParentScopeSuperseded` as soon as the proved ref differs from the planned one — ahead of the resume probe, the convergence pass, the pointer vouch and every publish. The proved ref travels in `ConvergedSubtree`, `PromotedSubtree` and `InteriorBounds::source`, so the three rebuilds from `parent.identity` are gone. No existing rejection is relaxed.

Test `a_re_pointed_parent_refuses_the_grant_before_any_publish` asserts the verdict tag and that nothing is published, nothing is vouched for, no interior node moves and no share pointer is delivered. The grant test fake now keys its scope-resolve, promote and interior-re-seal seams on the parent name, as the production net does. I confirmed the test is red against the pre-fix shape: it reports `publish-failed`, which is the false `Rejected` the issue describes.

### Body and title edits

None. Body check 2026-09-15: issue #1844 states the code facts correctly at `d7434bbe2`, so no edit was needed.

### Follow-ups

None filed and none folded. The whole acceptance list is met in this PR.

### Inline review passes

**Simplify.** The three `ChildScopeRef::new(parent.identity...)` rebuilds are gone; one construction stays, in `converge_grant_subtree`. The proved ref lives in `InteriorBounds.source`, beside `source_read_epoch`, which the same resolve produced — this kept `hand_over_granted_folder` at its old arity and removed a parameter from `reseal_granted_interior`. No new trait, generic or seam; the new error is a unit variant, the shape `ResumeNotThisGrant` already uses. Doc comments are three or four lines each, with no absence-justifying prose and no tracker reference.

**Security.** The change moves a refusal earlier and removes none. A stale plan now costs no publish, no vouch and no convergence write. The check refuses on inequality, so it cannot admit an input the old code refused — the old code reached the same input at `promote_scope_root` and answered `Rejected`. Every interior read and re-seal now runs under the scope ref the resolve proved, so no leg reads under a label no resolve gated. No parsing, no external input, no new allocation bound. `left_partly_made` classifies the new variant as pre-publish, which matches where it is raised.

**Crypto and privacy.** No primitive, KDF edge, wire format or KAT is touched; `crates/core` is unchanged. No key material enters or leaves the new path, and the new variant carries no payload, so no name, seed or pseudonym reaches an error a host may log. The parent re-seal reads `ParentScopePlan::seeds`, which were read from one scope root record; publishing them over a root a re-point superseded would sign a record under material that no longer describes the live scope, and the refusal is what stops that. The test asserts a verdict tag and two empty collections, with no secret material in an assertion.

**PR:** #1853 — `fix/926-1302-queue-memo-and-renewal-cell` — head `ec66ef6f5b4f9cf38c20c4f9fcdc6126915b5c14`. Draft, no CodeRabbit request made.

**CI:** every check passed. `Rust / Fmt + clippy (Linux)`, `Rust / Workspace tests (Linux)`, `Rust / Engine simulation tests`, `Rust / Core KATs (native + WASM)`, `Rust / Adapter tests (macOS and Windows)`, `Rust / FUSE Op Core`, `Web / Client Browser Suite`, `Web Engine WASM`, `Web E2E Smoke`, `Repo / Lint`, `Repo / Tracker Refs`, `Contract Suite`, `lint-pr-title`, `zizmor static audit`. `API`, `Desktop` and the two Web typecheck legs were skipped by change detection. CodeRabbit reports "Review skipped: draft pull request", as intended.

**Issues closed by the PR:** #926 and #1302. Both closing references are registered on the PR.

**What the two commits do**

`perf: consult the queue memo before enumerating the durable op queue` — the scan memo keyed on the queue's own contents, so every read off the queue enumerated the whole durable op queue first. It now keys on `{owner_tag, generation}` and reads the generation before the queue. The decoded queue is cleared at logout beside the render.

`refactor: hold the session renewal set in one map` — `HeldKey` became an enum with a variant per plane. The vault settings and bin index cells fold into `held_records`, and `drop_superseded_pointers` generalises to `drop_superseded` over every plane the resolve tick does not refresh.

**Body and title edits**

- #926: appended a dated "Body check 2026-09-15" section. The proposed seam-level `{high_water, len}` fingerprint was stale — `QueueGenerationStore` already supplies a constant-size generation, so the slice changed no seam trait, no host implementation, and carried no wasm risk. The recon report and the lane brief both predicted a seam-trait change; that prediction was wrong.
- #1302: appended a dated body check. `HeldKey` had already landed, and the parallel cell was no longer one — `Engine::bin_index_record` had joined `Engine::settings_record`, which is exactly what the body predicted would happen. Both are folded in this PR, so the issue closes whole rather than half.

**Follow-ups:** none filed. No defect was found that this PR does not fix.

**Review passes (from `/tmp/w19/lane-F4/review.md`)**

Simplify: the change is a net deletion — two parallel cells, the `live_account_record` helper and the `RecordPlane` type all go. `observed_at` and `hold_if_unchanged` replace six copies of the same compare-before-write block, with the rationale stated once at its home. The `QueueGeneration` trait is not speculative: the tick loop reads the store through `LiveSeam`, which must forward the generation.

Security: the memo key is `owner_tag` plus the generation, read before the queue, so a mutation that lands mid-load costs an extra decode and can never serve a queue that no longer exists. `owner_tag` stays in the key, so one identity never reads another's decoded intent. A failed cold start now clears the whole held set, which carries every name's signer — stronger than the two cells it replaces. `drop_superseded` applies the pointer plane's compare-before-drop to the settings and bin index planes, so the `live_settings_record` guarantee survives the move.

Crypto-privacy: no `crates/core` change, no primitive, no KDF edge, no wire format. Moving `HeldRecord` from two `Option` cells into the existing session map does not widen the signer's reach — that map is already session memory, never persisted, cleared at teardown. No secret material appears in a test assertion.

**Branch:** `refactor/1421-open-truncate-in-op-core` · head `0c13266c858948dc8b5667f357fecc9c86bdec14` · DRAFT, no CodeRabbit request made.

**The change.** `OperationCore::open` now takes `truncate: bool` and owns the open, the truncate and the rollback. It calls `truncate_handle` directly, so an opening truncate no longer repeats the render and the node and kind checks the open just made. `open_handle` is deleted from `crates/fuse/src/adapters/fuse.rs`. The WinFsp adapter passes `false` at its one `core.open` call.

**Recon correction, important for the report-D row and for lane 1792.** The report says WinFsp re-derives the rollback. It does not. WinFsp signals an overwrite through its own `overwrite` callback on a context the open already handed back, so `crates/fuse/src/adapters/windows.rs` never had a compensation to delete. Its diff is one argument and a two-line comment.

**Body and title edits.** The title lost the stale phrase "before the second adapter lands". The body now names `adapters/fuse.rs` instead of `adapters/linux.rs` and carries a dated "Body check 2026-09-15" section that records the WinFsp finding and the refusal the new test drives. The rendered body was read back and is correct.

**Test.** `a_refused_opening_truncate_leaves_no_handle_and_no_pinned_stream` in the `FUSE Op Core` suite drives `O_TRUNC` on an open that cannot write, asserts the `BadHandle` refusal, asserts the handle number the open took is not open, and repeats the refusal past the engine stream ceiling before proving the file still reads. It was checked red first: with the release removed from the rollback it fails on "the refused open left its handle open". `two_handles_on_one_node_seal_under_different_keys` now opens with the truncate flag instead of simulating it with a separate `truncate` call.

**Review passes** (`/tmp/w19/lane-X1/review.md`):
- *simplify* — the hoist deletes an adapter helper and its duplicated rationale; the doc lives once on `OperationCore::open`; the core drops a redundant second render; one test states what it simulates; no new type and no new abstraction.
- *security* — `open` is kernel facing, and a repeated `O_RDONLY|O_TRUNC` can no longer grow the handle table from any adapter. The caller still receives the truncate refusal unchanged. No false ack is possible: the only refusal a zero-length truncate makes runs before any pending state exists, and `release` closes the handle even when its commit fails. No clock, no entropy, no new seam.
- *crypto-privacy* — not required and not run. The diff touches no `crates/core` primitive, no key or seal material and no trust-boundary read. FUSE handles are session tokens, not key material.

<result>## Resolve-review report — PR #1852

**Head sha:** `18f6bdf34680c6fd5ac5c8ca91057997bea9aba0` — unchanged. No push was needed: no item required a code change.

### The finding

CodeRabbit posted one actionable comment, "Fence the final parent publication to the resolved parent reference" (`crates/engine/src/grants/create.rs` line 856, minor, data integrity). Its claim: `hand_over_granted_folder` publishes `parent_record` without passing the captured `parent_ref`, `OwnerRotationNet::publish_scope_root` resolves by the record's own name through `GatedRoots`, and `RootPublish::check_publishable` checks only epoch and cut floors, so a re-point that lands after the new check can still publish the stale `ParentScopePlan` at the old name. It asked me to pass `parent_ref` into the publish and consult the pointer there.

### Verdict: rejected, with evidence, no code change

I verified the mechanism in `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs` before deciding.

The publish is already fenced — by the durable floor mirror, not by a pointer consult. `check_publishable` runs on every scope-root publish, including the tail's parent record, and refuses with `RotationPublishError::Rejected` when the record's read epoch, write epoch or commitment cut epoch sits below the scope's matching durable floor. The checks are release-active, and `floor::acquire_write_epoch_lease` holds the floor read valid through the signature, so the floor cannot rise between the check and the signed bytes.

A re-point is a rotation cut, so it raises that scope's read epoch, write epoch, or both. The floors are monotonic and durable per device, and a focus-window tick that observes another device's re-point raises them on this device as well. A superseded plan therefore carries an epoch below the live floor and never reaches signed bytes. Four named tests already cover this, and the first states this exact case in its own comment — a concurrent tick observes another device's re-point, raises the monotonic write floor while a parked root is held, and both sides of the rise are refused:

- `a_write_floor_rise_before_the_root_republish_refuses_the_seal`
- `a_read_floor_rise_before_the_republish_refuses_the_record`
- `a_cut_floor_rise_inside_the_owner_publish_window_refuses_the_re_seal`
- `a_read_floor_rise_inside_the_mint_publish_window_refuses_the_re_seal`

The suggested change would also not remove the window it names: a pointer consult inside `publish_scope_root` is one round trip and the publish is another, so the same gap stays open, and it would add a network read to every rotation caller of the `ScopeRootPublisher` seam.

### Nitpick and other dispositions

The review body carries no "Nitpick" section and no "Outside diff range" section. The only other dispositionable item is the failed pre-merge check, **Docstring Coverage 66.67 percent against an 80 percent threshold** — also rejected. The uncovered functions in this diff are trait-impl methods on the grant test fake (`resolve_scope`, `consult_pointer`, `promote_scope_root`, `reseal_interior_node`) plus one test function. Each implements a trait documented at its definition, and `AGENTS.md` requires rationale stated once at its home rather than restated on every implementation. Every production item this PR adds does carry a doc comment.

CodeRabbit's four other pre-merge checks passed, including "Linked Issues check" and "Out of Scope Changes check".

### Posted and verified

- Inline thread reply: https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1852#discussion_r4014072481 — read back through `gh api`, body matches the intended text exactly.
- Thread `PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM6idG1P` resolved; the mutation returned `isResolved: true`.
- Disposition comment: https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1852#issuecomment-5677878508 — read back, body matches.

A note for other lanes: `gh api --input &lt;file&gt;` failed on this box with "unexpected end of JSON input" for both the REST reply endpoint and the `addPullRequestReviewThreadReply` mutation. The form that worked is a script that reads the file into a variable and passes `-f "body=$BODY"`.

### CI

No push, so the run is the same one: 31 check runs, 27 pass, 4 skipped by the path filter (`API`, `Desktop`, `Web / Shared packages typecheck + tests`, `Web / Web host typecheck + tests`), 0 fail, 0 pending. The PR is still a draft, no new CodeRabbit review was requested, and no timers are running.</result>

<summary>Agent "Lane R3: #1716 #1574" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>The CodeRabbit item is resolved.

**The item.** One "Outside diff range" comment, Minor, `crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs`: "Do not re-arm the full-tree walk for terminal trust rejections." There were no inline threads and no nitpick section, so this was the only item to disposition.

**Accepted, and it was a genuine defect in my #1574 latch.** I verified the mechanism rather than the wording. A write grantee authors its own scope root's `directChildScopeIndex`, so it chooses which entries the walk resolves. One entry whose record the descendant edge refuses returns `ResolveFailure::Rejected` from `direct_child_index`, and my `Err(_) =&gt; complete = false` arm treated that as a reason to walk again. The latch would then stay open forever and re-walk the whole owned tree every hour — exactly the unbounded, grantee-steered cost #1574 exists to remove, restored by the one input a grantee fully controls.

**What I changed.** The walk now classifies on rule 6's axis instead of collapsing every error. `ResolveFailure::Unavailable` re-arms the walk, because a transport or host stall converges on a retry. `ResolveFailure::ConflictingChildLabel` re-arms it too — the re-point wave repairs both parent indexes, which is the axis `SweepResolveFailure::is_retryable` already states for that class, so I followed the existing precedent rather than inventing one. `ResolveFailure::Rejected` does not re-arm: a rejection is a fail-closed trust verdict, and re-reading the same record reaches the same verdict. The same split applies to the consult: `PointerConsultError::Unavailable` re-arms, `PointerConsultError::Rejected` does not, since a pointer that does not authenticate or vouches a rolled-back write epoch is refused again on every later read.

I did not apply the suggestion literally. It proposed error matching only; I also stated the rule once in a comment at the `complete` declaration and added `a_rejected_descendant_does_not_re_arm_the_walk`, which stages a descendant the gate refuses and proves the second pass spends no further fan-out on that name. I confirmed the test fails when the rejection arm re-arms the walk. No item was rejected, deferred, or turned into a follow-up issue.

**Disposition comment:** posted as &lt;https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1848#issuecomment-5679419356&gt;, built from a shell variable with `-f "body=$BODY"` and read back with `gh api`. The read-back matches the source exactly, carries no literal `/tmp/` path and no tool attribution.

## Lane R4 final report

**PR:** #1858 — `refactor/1439-one-same-epoch-reseal` — head `6cbe085ce91ce49e367be7952f00d4fc8b1b11b7`. Draft, no CodeRabbit request, not merged.

Skipped by path filter: API, Desktop, Web shared-packages and web-host typecheck.

**Issues closed:** #1439 (verified in `closingIssuesReferences`).

**What landed.** `reseal_at_current_epoch` plus `ResealSite` in `crates/engine/src/rotation/reseal.rs`. The two `CascadeTarget`-shaped sites — the descendant re-key loop in `grants/create.rs` and `publish_converted_set` in `facade.rs` — now build no `ScopeRootIdentity` and no `ResealSeeds` literal. The helper hard-wires `prev: None` and `WriteHistory::Carried` and takes a non-optional `owner_enc_secret`, so a same-epoch site can neither mint a history link nor skip the ledger tag-binding proof. A new `rotation::reseal` test proves the helper and the explicit form emit byte-identical sections under one seeded entropy.

**`repair_child_scope_index` stays as it is.** It sources from the sweep's parked `SweptScopeSource`, which carries no `v`, no owner encryption public key, no pseudonym signer and no pointer read key; a shim would have to build a whole owned `CascadeTarget` and clone the commitment, the ledger and the history links on every repair. Recorded in the PR body and the issue body.

**Defect found and fixed — `main` was red.** At `fc6a18651` the engine lib test target did not compile: the parked enrolment assertions in `net/rotation.rs` call `HeldKey::scope_pointer`, but the enum variant is `HeldKey::ScopePointer`. `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine` failed with two `E0599` errors on any checkout of `main`. This PR's first commit repairs it. The orchestrator should know that the "Rust / Engine simulation tests" gate was failing on `main` until this branch.

**Body edits.** #1439 body gained a dated "Body check 2026-09-15" section: the facade method is `publish_converted_set`, not `publish_converted_claim`; only two of the four listed sites are `CascadeTarget`-shaped, because the parent index update reads a `ParentScopePlan`; and the reason `repair_child_scope_index` stays. Rendered body read back and verified.

**Follow-ups:** none filed. The one defect found is fixed in this PR, per the filing bar.

**Review passes** (`/tmp/w19/lane-R4/review.md`):

- *simplify* — four arguments, five fields, nothing optional "for later"; `prev` and the write plane are hard-wired rather than defaulted parameters; each call site holds one literal instead of three; one folded finding, the "existing seed, current epoch, prev = None" rationale was restated at the create.rs loop and in the facade doc and is now stated once at the helper; the rest of the import churn is rustfmt reflow.
- *security* — no trust boundary moves; the helper narrows what a same-epoch site can express; the non-optional `owner_enc_secret` closes the skip that `ScopeRootIdentity`'s `Option` allows; `ascent` and `owes_ascent_link` stay caller-supplied so the two fail-closed ascent errors see the values they saw before; entropy stays injected and no bound is relaxed.
- *crypto-privacy* — no primitive, KDF edge, wire format or KAT is touched; seeds enter by borrow from the target's `Zeroizing` fields and the helper zeroes nothing it does not own; no secret material in any assertion; determinism proved by byte equality rather than assumed; the encode-side fail-closed checks still run before any seal.

**Left undone:** nothing in scope. Attribution grep printed 0 before the single push.</result>

### CI — 31 checks, 0 failures, 0 re-runs needed

SUCCESS: Detect Changes, zizmor static audit, lint-pr-title, Repo / Lint, Repo / Tracker Refs, Repo / Updater Key, Repo / Mock Routing Store, Rust / Fmt + clippy (Linux), Rust / Workspace tests (Linux), Rust / Engine simulation tests, Rust / FUSE Op Core, Rust / Core KATs (native + WASM), Rust / Adapter tests (macOS), Rust / Adapter tests (Windows), Web Engine WASM, Contract Suite, Web / Web Bundle, Web / Client Browser Suite, Web E2E Smoke, and all seven Result gates.

### The move

Seven files. Each production file changes by exactly **one line** — `mod tests {` becomes `mod tests;`. The block moves to a sibling child-module file in the layout `rotation/sweep/sim.rs` already set.

| file | impl before | impl after | test lines moved | tests before | tests after |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| reseal.rs | 3241 | 1141 | 2099 | 43 | 43 |
| rotate_write.rs | 2329 | 855 | 1473 | 27 | 27 |
| cascade.rs | 2169 | 877 | 1291 | 31 | 31 |
| trigger.rs | 1806 | 793 | 1012 | 39 | 39 |
| sweep.rs | 1782 | 870 | 911 | 50 | 50 |
| rotate.rs | 845 | 354 | 490 | 8 | 8 |
| eager_set.rs | 581 | 287 | 293 | 16 | 16 |

The strongest proof of purity: `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine --lib -- --list` returns the **same 1804 test names before and after, byte for byte**. Module paths do not shift, because a child module in a sibling file keeps the path of the inline module it replaces. A whitespace-insensitive comparison of every moved block against its source found exactly **two token differences in the whole change** — trailing commas that `cargo fmt` drops when it rejoins a signature that no longer needs to wrap after the four-column dedent. No visibility was widened; no `pub(super)` was needed.

`cargo fmt --all --check` clean. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean (this also proves no parent-file import became unused). `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine` green. - `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml` exports the six `APPLE_*` variables only when the repository holds every one of them. An absent secret expands to an empty string, which the bundler reads as a signing identity, so the set is all-or-nothing and a credential-free release still produces today's ad hoc signed bundle.
- `.github/workflows/ci-desktop.yml` runs a real credential-free bundle on macOS and asserts the app executable exists and `Signature=adhoc`. The updater artifact is off in that run because it needs the minisign key, which no pull-request job holds. This was found by running the bundle locally first, where the default `createUpdaterArtifacts: true` failed for the missing key.
- `blueprint/deploy.md` records the rule under "Desktop release artifacts".

## Final report

**PR:** #1862 — `feat/1094-file-version-history-engine` — head `f9526a8ed2faf39369e56b6fb791674081b044ce` — DRAFT, no review requested.

**CI:** all 37 checks terminal. 36 SUCCESS, 1 SKIPPED (`Desktop / Desktop Build`, dispatch-gated). `CodeRabbit` carries no conclusion because no review was requested. Nothing was re-run; no flake appeared.

**Issues closed:** #1094 (`closingIssuesReferences` verified as `[1094]`).

**Body edits:** #1094 got a dated "Body check 2026-09-15" section, appended, original scope intact. It records that the 2026-08-18 correction still holds, that its file sizes are stale, and three facts the scope depends on: the `Prune` op is complete but no `Command` stages it, `RetentionPolicy` defaulted to `KeepAll` with nothing applying it automatically, and the sealed `Version` already carries every field a per-version entry needs. Rendered body read back and checked.

**Follow-ups:** none filed. Every finding of the three gates was implemented in this PR.

### v1 behaviour table

| Question | v1 | v2 in this PR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Versions kept | `maxVersionsPerFile` setting, default 10, clamp 0..100 | `keepLatestVersions` setting, default 10 |
| Per entry | `cid`, `fileKeyEncrypted`, `fileIv`, `size`, `timestamp`, `encryptionMode`, all from the sealed record | `contentCid`, `size`, `modifiedAt`, all from the sealed read-body; the key never crosses |
| Naming | array index, no id | `contentCid` — an index moves under a concurrent write |
| Restore | current becomes newest entry, restored entry leaves the list, bytes re-referenced, history keeps its length | the same |
| Delete | entry leaves, current untouched, republish, unpin the CID | the same, through the retire ledger |
| Delete the current version | no — it has no row and no SDK call | refused, at the command and again at the drain |
| Order | newest first, current not listed | the same |
| Actions | download, restore, delete | download, restore, delete |

Forced differences, all stated in the PR body: no version cooldown (v1's 15-minute window needs a clock, which retention replay determinism forbids); a delete is final because each version is fresh-keyed; retire instead of best-effort unpin; an explicit `0` is not representable because `KeepLatest` takes a `NonZeroU64`.

### Review gates

**`/simplify`** — one `shorten_history` and one `journal_retire_debt` now serve both the automatic retention and `publish_prune`; one `open_file_record` serves both new drain paths; a control-flag parameter became `version_position` returning an index. A helper for the shared `publish_node` tail was considered and rejected: four call sites already carry that argument list, so it would need seven parameters and reach into untouched code.

**`/security-review`** — one real finding, fixed. The automatic retention could return `Halt::Permanent(PayloadRefused)` on a history whose `contentCid` repeats; since a version list is authored by anyone holding the scope's write seed, a co-writer could have parked every later write to that file. A history this build refuses to shorten now publishes whole, covered by a new test. Also verified: shortening acts only on a proven member choice, so a failed settings load never destroys history; a version the record no longer names dead-letters rather than publishing a guess; both new reads run the gated child pipeline; no clock enters retention, restore or delete.

**`/crypto-privacy-review`** — `VersionEntry` carries no key material and none crosses the WASM boundary. A restore rotates the list in place, so no content key is moved out of its owning `Vec`; a delete drops the entry and zeroizes its `SecretBytes`. A version download wipes the plaintext at the boundary like `download`. Fresh-keying is preserved: a restore reuses the version's own key and re-encrypts nothing. Every key rides in the one read-body, so a lazy-wave re-seal carries the whole history across a key-regression epoch — covered by a test. No test asserts on key material.

### Left undone, and why

- **The web dialog (#1095) was not started.** The lane brief caps this as an L lane at the engine half plus the binding, and the diff is 1126 lines across 21 files.
- **One item needs an owner eye, flagged in the PR body.** `blueprint/engine.md` previously read "Retention default: keep all versions within quota, with an explicit user-initiated prune op." The lane brief instructed me to state the rule there, so I replaced that line with the count-based rule. This writes down the decision the 2026-08-20 comment already took rather than taking a new one, so I did not draft a decision entry — but it is a blueprint change, and the repo's normal path for one runs through the decision corpus.
- **No `crates/core` change and no KAT regeneration**, so the "stop and report before regenerating" clause never triggered.</result>

**PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box-next/pull/76 (draft, single file)
**Entry:** `decisions/0019-file-version-retention-is-count-based-keep-latest-n.md`, status Proposed, branch `decision/version-retention-keep-latest-n`.

What the entry contains, matching the 0017/0018 precedent exactly: `# ADR 0019 — …` title, the four-item bold metadata block (Status / Date / Relates to / Implemented by), `## Context` with subheads, `## Decision` as numbered **D1**–**D8** bold clauses, `## Alternatives rejected`, `## Consequences` (numbered), `## Residuals` as **E1**–**E4**, `## Gate`, and the closing archive note ("The blueprint and glossary are maintained in the `FSM1/cipher-box` repository…") that both 0017 and the corpus tail carry. Alternatives cover keep-all-within-quota-with-prune, age-based, hybrid, plus a reference-count variant and v1's representable `0`. Attribution check printed 0 before push; PR body read back clean.

Things I was unsure about, or found different from the brief:

1. **There is no index or README listing the entries.** `decisions/` has no README, and the repo `README.md` only points at the wayfinder map in Issues. The previous entry (0018) was added by commit `72dd444` as a single new file with no other change, so I did the same — nothing to update. Ordering is by filename number alone.
2. **Status value.** The corpus uses bare `Proposed` at filing, then rewrites the line to `Accepted — &lt;what landed&gt; in FSM1/cipher-box#NNNN (merged)` in a separate follow-up commit. I filed as `Proposed`; acceptance is a later edit, not part of this PR.
3. **Not all entries have every section.** `Residuals` and `Gate` appear in 0017 and 0018 but not in the earlier ones; I followed the two most recent as instructed.
4. **Reference-link style is mixed** in the corpus — some references are full markdown links to the issue URL, some are bare `FSM1/cipher-box#NNNN`. I used full links in the metadata block (as 0017/0018 do for the primary issues) and bare refs in the prose.
5. **The blueprint line's home.** It is in the "Chunking and retention" paragraph of `blueprint/engine.md` at `FSM1/cipher-box` (line 1188), not in this repo's archived copy, which per the corpus convention this ADR does not edit. I recorded the exact quoted sentence in the metadata and in Consequences 1.
6. PR #1862's body currently states the blueprint reword is "recorded here rather than through a decision entry" — this ADR supersedes that note, so that paragraph on #1862 will need a small edit when this entry is accepted. I did not touch #1862.</result>

## Lane N3 — final report

**PR:** [#1861](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1861) — draft, not marked ready, no CodeRabbit review requested.
**Branch:** `chore/1398-1397-perf-baselines-and-frozen-constants`

**Attribution check:** `git log --format=%B origin/main..HEAD | grep -ci 'co-authored\|generated with'` printed `0` before every push.

### CI status

37 of 37 checks pass. `Desktop / Desktop Build` is `skipping` on its path filter. The new gate `Repo / Perf Tooling` passes in 24 s. `zizmor static audit` passes; I also ran zizmor 1.25.2 locally over `.github/workflows/` and `.github/actions/` with no finding.

### Issues

- **Closes #1398.** The closing reference registers exactly `[1398]`, verified through GraphQL `closingIssuesReferences`.
- **Part of #1397.** Not closed. The reason is below.
- **Part of #658.** Not closed, because its child #1397 stays open.

### What landed

Two commits, one per issue.

1. `scripts/baseline-benchmark.sh` is deleted and replaced by `tools/perf`, a new workspace package. The v2 API surface is already measured by `cipherbox-load`, which drives the engine's own API client over the desktop `Http` seam, so the port is a runner over that harness and not a second HTTP client. `blueprint/deploy.md`'s inventory row names the successor.
2. `Perf Benches` gains a `job` input with two new jobs. `ranged-fetch` runs a new `ranged_profile` engine example and the framing bench. `cross-client-latency` summarizes a `Desktop E2E Tests` run's wait samples, which avoids about 150 lines of duplicated workflow. `poll` now reports every settled wait to an observer and the cross-client orchestrator installs a JSONL sink when given a path.

### Recorded measurements, all real

- **Local baseline**: all five scenarios against the docker stack, no breach, nothing throttled or failed.
- **Staging baseline**: `mixed`, `content-ingest`, `name-wave` and `byo-advisory`, from four real `load-test.yml` dispatches against `api-staging.cipherbox.cc`. The 2-vCPU ceiling and the round-trip floor are stated so a staging row is never read as a regression.
- **Ranged fetch**: the deterministic over-fetch table of the frozen framing.

### What is not done, and why

The cross-client convergence latency is not recorded. It needs a mounted desktop host beside a browser host on one vault, which this lane cannot stand up to a trustworthy number locally. The job to take it is landed and must be dispatched on the mounted matrix. **All five `SyncTimingProfile::PRODUCTION` placeholders therefore keep their values** — `escalation_window`, `focus_horizon`, `pointer_consult_interval`, `sweep_cadence`, `migration_window` — since each is bounded by that latency. Their doc comment now names the job that settles them and the file where a value lands. No KAT changed; no profile constant moved.

`gateway-read` on staging needs the `IPFS_GATEWAY_TOKEN` secret, which no workflow sets. The read accelerator sits behind `forward_auth` and the dispatch refuses the scenario without it. **This is a decision for the owner:** provide the secret, or accept that the staging read-accelerator row stays unrecorded.

### Body edits

- **#1397**: appended a dated "Body check 2026-09-15" section. It records that `ContentProfile::PRODUCTION.chunk_size` is no longer a placeholder (wire-frozen, KAT-pinned) and that the timing placeholder set is now five fields, not two. I read the rendered body back and confirmed it.
- **#1398**: unchanged. Its body states the current code facts, and its `blocked_by: #1390` does not gate.

### Follow-ups

**None filed.** One real defect found during the work was fixed in this PR instead: `cipherbox-load`'s local gateway default named `localhost:8080`, and Kubo serves `localhost` as a subdomain gateway, answering every path request with a 301 — `gateway-read --target local` failed 100 of 100 reads. The default now names the address, matching what every sibling workflow already passes.

### Review passes (`/tmp/w19/lane-N3/review.md`)

**simplify** — one finding folded in: `report.ts` parsed four fields nothing read (`target`, `clients`, `opsPerClient`, `wallMs`); dropped. No duplicated HTTP client, percentile arithmetic or credential handling was added. The latency job reuses an existing run rather than re-declaring a mounted stack. The `ranged_profile` example replaced an earlier sentinel-based range table with a plain list. Every export has a caller and a test.

**security** — no injection surface: the runner spawns an argument vector, never a shell string, and a unit test asserts no credential reaches the command line. Fails closed on a missing secret, an unknown target, an unknown scenario, and a report missing a percentile. Workflow permissions are least-privilege (`contents: read`, plus `actions: read` only for the artifact read); `crossClientRunId` never reaches a `run:` body. <result>`/resolve-pr-reviews` on PR #1857 is complete.

**The finding** — `apps/web/src/auth/coreKit.ts`, 1 actionable comment, Major, "Retry a deletion whose commit failed". The review body carried no "Nitpick" and no "Outside diff range" section, so that one item is the whole list.

**Verified against the installed SDK, and accepted.** The mechanism is real. `TssModule.deleteFactorPub` calls `metadata.updateTSSData` with the remaining factor public keys before it does any network work, so `getTssFactorPub()` stops listing the factor immediately. `commitChanges` is the only call that writes that metadata, through `syncLocalMetadataTransitions`. A sync that rejected therefore left the stored account still carrying a factor the local list no longer named, and my idempotence guard read that state as a resolved delete — so no later call could ever finish the removal.

**The suggested repair was corrected, not applied literally.** A second `deleteFactor` call reaches `deleteFactorPub`, which starts with `found.length === 0` on the local list and throws `could not find factorPub to delete`. The cut and the re-share of the remaining factors have already run; only the sync is left to retry. So the session now records the identifier of a cut whose commit did not land, and a later call for that identifier retries `commitChanges` alone, clearing the record only after the sync resolves. That reasoning and the evidence for it are in the inline reply.

**Regression test** — `re-syncs a cut whose commit did not land, rather than reading it as gone` in `apps/web/src/auth/coreKit.test.ts`. The first commit fails, the second call resolves, and it asserts the factor was cut exactly once, which is what proves the retry is the sync and nothing else. Without the fix the second call returns at the guard and the commit count assertion fails.

**Dispositions** — one item, accepted with a corrected repair. Replied in the inline thread (`#discussion_r4018696049`) and resolved it (`PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM6ioUqS`, `isResolved: true`). Posted one disposition comment (`#issuecomment-5685438129`). Both bodies were read back with `gh api`: no literal scratchpad path, no tool attribution, intended text.

<result>Cycle 2 of `/resolve-pr-reviews` on PR #1857 is complete.

**The finding** — `apps/web/src/auth/coreKit.ts` line 398, 1 actionable comment, Major, "Preserve failed approval-factor cleanup for a later retry". The review body again carried no "Nitpick" and no "Outside diff range" section, so that one item was the whole list.

**Accepted, and the repair turned out to be a simplification rather than the "heavy lift" it was labelled.** CodeRabbit's reachability point was correct and it was a real defect in my cycle-1 fix: the retry branch was keyed on the factor identifier, and no caller ever names an earlier factor again — each failed approval mints its own factor and drops that one. The branch was dead code.

The remedy is smaller than the one proposed, and I verified why against the SDK source. `TKey.syncLocalMetadataTransitions` writes the whole queued transition list in one `setMetadataStream` and clears the queue only when that write resolves. So one `commitChanges` lands every cut the session has queued, and a failed one leaves them all queued for the next call. The session therefore does not need to remember *which* factor is outstanding, and the approval lifecycle does not need the new replay entry point CodeRabbit asked for. It needs only to know *that* a cut still waits for a sync, so the idempotence guard does not short-circuit before running one.

`deleteApprovalFactor` now reads the local list once, cuts the named factor when the account still lists it, and runs the sync whenever it cut something or a previous sync is outstanding. A call that finds nothing to cut and nothing outstanding still returns without work, so the method stays idempotent. The identifier-keyed state is gone, replaced by one boolean.

**Tests** — the fake SDK now models the stored account separately from the local metadata, so both tests assert what the account carries rather than a call count: `re-syncs a cut whose commit did not land, rather than reading it as gone`, and the new `lands an earlier cut on the next delete, without naming that factor again` (first cut's sync fails, a second approval drops its own factor, one sync lands both). I ran a mutation check: removing the pending condition from the guard fails the first test.

**Push SHA** — `6170286d79b8b219aa5ee5b8a20f039558af8cec`. One push, rebased on current `origin/main` first, trailer grep printed 0 both before the commit and after the rebase.

**Verification before the push** — `apps/web` vitest 64 files / 711 tests passing, `pnpm -r typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm lint:tracker-refs` clean.

Both bodies read back with `gh api`: no literal scratchpad path, no tool attribution, intended text. Both review threads on the PR are now resolved.

<result>Cycle 3 of `/resolve-pr-reviews` on PR #1857 is complete. The two items were **different findings**, not the same one, and both are accepted.

**Item 1 — actionable, Major, `apps/web/src/auth/coreKit.ts` line 389: "Clean up a factor when its initial sync fails". Accepted and fixed.**

This is the same defect class the PR exists to close, reached through the mint instead of the seal. `createFactor` adds the factor to local metadata and queues the transition. When `commitChanges` then rejects, `mintApprovalFactor` rejects *before it returns*, so `ApprovalPrompt.step` never assigns `mintedFactor.current` and the cleanup in `answer` has nothing to name. The creation stays queued, and since one `syncLocalMetadataTransitions` writes the whole queue, the next successful sync carries a factor onto the account whose key the approver never received — one more per retry, which is exactly the behaviour #1854 set out to remove.

The mint now derives the identifier before the commit and, on a rejecting commit, cuts the factor back out through `deleteApprovalFactor` before rethrowing the original sync failure. The cleanup is best effort, because the sync that failed is what the member must read. A cleanup whose own sync also fails needed no extra handling: the pending flag from cycle 2 already holds the cut for the next sync. Regression test `cuts a factor back out when its own sync failed, so no later sync carries it` asserts that neither the local metadata nor the stored account carries the factor after the mint rejects.

**Item 2 — outside the diff, Minor, `ApprovalPrompt.tsx` 92-122: "Add an online-recovery back-off test". Accepted and added.**

Correct and genuinely uncovered. `useOnlineStatus` feeds the same `polling` gate as `useVisibility`, so both resets run through one effect, but only the visibility reset had a test — a change dropping the network gate from that expression would have passed. Added `returns to the floor when the network comes back`, with assertions equivalent to the visibility test. I mutation-checked it: removing `online` from `polling` makes it fail.

**Push SHA** — `c0ddf14757648beaaebe5ce5f8919eb036eb4bcc`. One push, rebased on current `origin/main` first; trailer grep printed 0 before the commit and again after the rebase.

**Verification** — `apps/web` vitest 66 files / 733 tests passing, plus `pnpm -r typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm lint:tracker-refs` clean. I re-ran the full suite and typecheck *after* the rebase as well, since main had moved. One correction to my earlier reports: the suite is 66/733, not 64/711 — the cycle-2 rebase brought two test files in from main and I had measured before that rebase.

**Thread handling** — replied inline on the actionable item (`#discussion_r4020754133`) and resolved thread `PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM6it76D`. All three review threads on the PR are now resolved. <result>Cycle 4 of `/resolve-pr-reviews` on PR #1857 is complete.

**The finding** — `apps/web/src/auth/coreKit.ts` lines 402-414, 1 actionable comment, Major: "Retain approval-factor cleanup IDs when `deleteFactor` rejects". No Nitpick or Outside-diff section this pass.

**Accepted, with one correction to the stated consequence.** The gap is real: `deleteApprovalFactor` recorded nothing *before* the cut, so a rejected `deleteFactor` left the factor listed and left no state any later call would act on. The cut is itself a network call — `deleteFactorPub` re-shares the remaining factors — so it fails in the same conditions that fail a sync, making the refused cut the likely case rather than an exotic one.

The correction: CodeRabbit said a later commit "can persist the still-listed factor", which is only true on one of the two call paths. From `ApprovalPrompt.answer` the mint already synced, so the factor is already on the account and a refused cut simply leaves an orphan — nothing new is persisted. The dangerous path is the mint's own cleanup, where the creation is still *queued*: a refused cut there leaves a queued creation that the next successful `commitChanges` carries onto the account. That is the case worth closing, and it is closed.

Rather than add a second piece of retry state beside the cycle-2 pending flag, the two collapse into one: the session holds a set of factors it owes a removal for. An entry is added before the cut is attempted and cleared only after a sync carries it, so neither a refused cut nor a failed sync drops it. A drain cuts every owed factor, skips any already cut, and syncs once. Every delete drains it, and the mint drains it before creating anything — a drain that still fails fails the mint, which is the right direction.

**Tests** — `retries a cut the SDK refused, on the next approval this session drops`, and `drains an owed removal before it mints, so its own sync cannot carry one`. Both mutation-checked: dropping an entry at the cut fails the first, removing the pre-mint drain fails the second.

**Push SHA** — `260875a0dd29ee96d1d97ab01fa3d3390c3ad290`. One push, rebased on current `origin/main`; trailer grep printed 0 before the commit and after the rebase. Full suite (66 files / 735 tests), typecheck and lint run both before the commit and again after the rebase.

**CI** — settled after 4 polls. 31 checks: 29 SUCCESS, 2 SKIPPED by design. PR is `MERGEABLE` / `CLEAN`, still draft, no review requested. All four review threads resolved; disposition comment `#issuecomment-5690301724` read back clean.

**Is the lifecycle now complete?** Walking every failure point: **create refused** — nothing was added, nothing to clean. **create succeeded, its sync refused** — the factor is cut back out and the sync failure is reported; if that cut is also refused, the removal is held and retried. **seal refused** — the minted factor is deleted by its identifier. **send refused, before the request goes out** — same delete. **at or after the send** — the factor is deliberately kept, because a refusal and a lost acknowledgement are indistinguishable and deleting would strand an admitted device. **any cut or sync that did not carry** — retried by the next delete and by the next mint, and the mint refuses to create while a removal is owed. Within one tab session I believe that is closed, and the two mutation checks show the tests hold it closed. The one case genuinely outside it is **a tab that closes while a removal is owed**: `owedRemovals` is in-memory, so the orphan survives until some other session cuts it. Fixing that means persisting approval-factor state at rest, which trades against keeping no approval state on disk, so it is a design decision rather than a patch — that is the case a fifth pass could legitimately raise, and I have stated it in the disposition comment rather than implementing it unilaterally.</result>

## What landed

## Body edits and follow-ups

- #876 needed no further body edit. Part 1 had already appended the dated body-check section enumerating all eighteen sites and the part 1 / part 2 split, and this PR covers exactly the ten assigned to part 2.
- **#1864 filed** — `engine: sweep the three by-value check surfaces the sweep measure did not reach`. ## Review gates

- **Simplify** — the reject family types, the generator writer and the freeze harness were each duplicated in shape across two corpora and moved to one home. `AuthorError` was deliberately not re-prefixed, so no host-facing string and no existing test churned. Ledger and owner_entry each dropped a second vector that carried an identical check and class, leaving one vector per check on every plane.
- **Security** — one finding folded. `CreateGrantError::class` labelled `Resume`, `DescendantResolve`, `InteriorResolve` and every publish arm `availability`, but each wraps a type whose own verdict can be `trust`: a gate rejection, or a publish the network refused. A host that read those as stalls would retry a fail-closed refusal for ever. Every wrapping arm now delegates to the wrapped type's `class()`. The corpus carries only a fixture name, a check and a class. Every driven call takes injected entropy, an explicit time and an in-memory floor store.
- **Crypto and privacy** — the families mint real grant rows and seal a real device factor under fixed test scalars, and none of that material reaches a committed file. Class labels were audited for the one hazard that matters, a trust verdict labelled `availability`; after the create fix none remains.

The full pass summaries are in `/tmp/w19/lane-M2b/review.md`.

## `/resolve-pr-reviews` on PR #1861 — report

**Push SHA:** `fe38a433f598936ef3c1c7771f47ef5052fc4beb` (one push, force-with-lease after a clean rebase onto current `main`, which had advanced by #1838, #1860 and #1865).
**PR state:** still a draft, still open. No new CodeRabbit review requested.
**Attribution check:** `grep -ci 'co-authored\|generated with'` printed `0` before the push.

### Finding 1 — `tools/perf/src/baseline.ts`, "Clear the warm-up report before the measured run" (Major) — **valid, fixed**

I reproduced the mechanism before touching anything. `cipherbox-load` writes its report only after a run finishes, so a run that ends early leaves nothing on disk:

```
$ LOAD_TEST_API_URL=http://localhost:39999 ... cipherbox-load --scenario mixed --report-dir /tmp/probe
cipherbox-load: no virtual client could authenticate against http://localhost:39999
$ echo $?  → 1      $ ls /tmp/probe  → No such file or directory
```

Both phases write one path, and my runner accepted a non-zero status deliberately (a threshold breach exits 1 and still writes real numbers). So a measured run that never reached the API left the warm-up report to be read as the measurement — precisely the failure the warm-up exists to prevent.

I did not apply the suggestion literally. The measured phase now starts from a cleared path **and** an absent report at the end is a refusal with a named error — that second half also catches a report left behind by an earlier session, which the suggested fresh-subdirectory approach would not. The measure step moved into `tools/perf/src/measure.ts` so the unit suite drives it with an injected spawn (no module mocking).

### Finding 2 — `tools/perf/src/waits.ts`, "Reject invalid wait measurements" (Minor) — **valid, fixed**

`1e400` is a valid JSON literal, `JSON.parse` yields `Infinity`, and `typeof` calls that a number, so it reached a rendered latency row intact; negative elapsed times and fractional read counts passed the same way. `parseSamples` now requires a finite non-negative `elapsedMs` and a non-negative integer `attempts`. This also removes an inconsistency with its sibling `report.ts`, which already used `Number.isFinite` for the same class of value.

### Both guards proven non-vacuous

I mutated each fix out and confirmed the matching tests fail, then restored:

- removing the measure guard → 2 failures (`refuses the warm-up numbers…`, `refuses a report left behind by an earlier session`)
- reverting the wait checks to `typeof` → 2 failures (`refuses an elapsed time no wait can produce`, `refuses a read count that is fractional or negative`)

Suite grew from 24 to 34 tests across 4 files. I also added two tests pinning what must stay **accepted** — a zero-elapsed first-read settle, and a fractional elapsed time, which a real clock does produce.

### Other review items dispositioned

The review body carried **no "Nitpick" section and no "Outside diff range" section**; I checked both explicitly. The remaining items:

- **Docstring Coverage pre-merge warning (46.15% vs 80%) — rejected with reason.** It is a reviewer setting that contradicts this repo's written standard: AGENTS.md says comments explain why not what, and that a comment restating what the next lines do "rots out of sync and misleads". The counted functions are small named helpers (`fixed`, `human`, `isDuration`, `isCount`, `asString`). The non-obvious rationale *is* documented, once, at its home.
- **Finishing touches (generate docstrings / unit tests / stacked PR) — declined.** Same objection for docstrings; the generated tests are unnecessary given 34 tests plus the mutation proof.
- **CodeRabbit resource warning: it could not clone the repo, so the review may be incomplete — noted**, no action available from my side. Worth flagging to you, since it means this review pass may have missed surface.

## Findings

CodeRabbit posted **2 actionable comments** and **no** "Nitpick" or "Outside diff range" items. Both are on `scripts/check-cloudflare-ranges.mjs`.

### 1. Add a deadline to each fetch attempt — ACCEPTED, fixed

I verified the mechanism before acting, and CodeRabbit's framing needed one correction. Node *does* bound the connect phase: an unreachable host rejects after 10 s with `ConnectTimeoutError`, which I had already seen earlier in this lane. It does **not** bound the response body. I stood up a local server that answers `200`, writes a partial JSON body and never ends it:

- unguarded `fetch` + `response.json()` — still pending after 6 s
- same call with `AbortSignal.timeout(2000)` — rejected at 2007 ms with `TimeoutError`

So the stall is real, and it is the body read rather than the connect. The job's `timeout-minutes: 5` means it is not literally unbounded, but the retry loop never runs and the operator sees a killed job instead of the readable `Could not read &lt;source&gt;` message.

I did not apply the suggestion literally. The budget is **15 s** per attempt, not CodeRabbit's 30 s, so three attempts plus the two 5 s delays reach 55 s worst case and stay well inside the job's own 5-minute cap. One signal per attempt covers `response.json()` as well as the request, which the experiment confirms.

### 2. Reject duplicate CIDRs before set comparison — REJECTED, with evidence

The observation is factually correct but the resulting failure is not actionable. I ran three cases through the real script against a local server:

- **A** exact 22 ranges → exit 0
- **B** 22 ranges plus one duplicate → exit 0, confirming CodeRabbit's claim
- **C** a duplicate **and** one range removed → exit 1, still naming `- 173.245.48.0/20 (trusted, no longer published)`

Case C is the load-bearing one: set comparison loses the repeat count, never the membership. A duplicate can only hide *itself*. So the proposed check would fail on exactly one new condition — a repeat in an otherwise unchanged set — which no commit in this repo can resolve, because `docker/Caddyfile` encodes `trusted_proxies static` as a set and the set is identical either way. The nightly would open a tracking issue for a cosmetic upstream quirk, which is the false alarm this job exists to prevent.

I kept the other malformed-source guards and said why in the reply: a non-200, a `success` other than `true`, and an absent or empty `ipv4_cidrs`/`ipv6_cidrs` each leave the trusted set *unknown*, not merely printed oddly.

## Nitpick dispositions

None to disposition — the review body carried no "Nitpick" and no "Outside diff range" section. I checked the body text directly rather than inferring from the thread count. The disposition comment states this explicitly and still tables both actionable items with their outcome.

## PR

- **PR #1866** — `feat: render file version history in the details dialog`
- Branch `feat/1095-web-version-history`, head sha `fbedfb7a9093c963a4418ac0cdd79ddcd1219964`
- State: DRAFT. Closing reference verified through GraphQL: it closes #1095. The rendered body carries no tool attribution, and the branch commit carries no `Co-Authored-By` trailer (grep printed 0 before the push).

## CI status

Every check on the head sha is COMPLETED SUCCESS. Nothing failed and nothing was re-run: API image, API integration tests, migration drift, OpenAPI freshness, API typecheck, API unit tests, API Result, Contract Suite (+Result), Desktop frontend typecheck + tests, Desktop Result, Detect Changes, Repo Lint, Mock Routing Store, Perf Tooling, Tracker Refs, Repo Result, Web Client Browser Suite, Web shared packages typecheck + tests, Web Bundle, Web host typecheck + tests, Web E2E Smoke (+Result), Web Engine WASM, Web Result, lint-pr-title, zizmor static audit. Desktop Build and Rust are SKIPPED by path filter, and both roll up SUCCESS.

## What landed

- `useFileVersions` reads `facade.fileVersions(node)` on dialog open and again after an accepted restore or delete, with a generation guard on out-of-order reads.
- `VersionHistory` renders one row per `VersionEntryDescriptor` — clamped content root CID, modified date, size — with a download, a restore, and a delete per row. No client-computed version metadata. A file with no prior version renders no section.
- Restore and delete confirm in the repository `ConfirmDangerDialog`; a refusal holds the confirmation up with the engine's own message.
- A version command in flight blocks every dismissal route on the details dialog, and an unanswered confirmation holds the outer dialog so one Escape cannot dismiss both.
- The v1 `version-download-guard.ts` was not ported, per the brief.
- Supporting: `lib/saveBlob.ts` now holds the buffered save path shared with `useFileDownload`; `utils/format.ts` gained `clampId`, shared with `shortAccountId`; `packages/client` now exports `VersionEntryDescriptor`, which was defined but absent from the barrel.
- Tests: 10 new cases in `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/details/versions.test.tsx`, proven red against unfixed code. `details.test.tsx` now mounts inside an engine provider. Local runs: `pnpm -r typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm lint:tracker-refs`, web suite 745 pass, client suite 723 pass.

## Issue edits and follow-ups

- #1095 body edited with a dated "Body check 2026-09-16" section: the body assumed versions ride on the snapshot and are named by an index, but #1862 landed a separate `fileVersions` read keyed by `contentCid` and dropped the v1 `fileIv`, `fileKeyEncrypted`, and `encryptionMode` fields. The original scope was kept.
- No new issues filed. Both review-pass findings were implemented in this PR.

## Review passes (from `/tmp/w19/lane-W5b/review.md`)

**simplify** — one shared `clampId` replaces a second both-ends clamp; the buffered save path moved to `lib/saveBlob.ts` so the version download and the file download share one implementation; the two writes are one `write(command, contentCid)` call; no guard port and no client-computed field; comments carry only the non-obvious rationale.

**security** — the dialog renders the engine answer verbatim; a refused write keeps the confirmation up and re-reads nothing; a command in flight blocks dismissal so no outcome reports into a replaced dialog, while a plain read does not block it so a slow read cannot trap the member; the version download uses an opaque blob type and revokes the object URL after the save and on unmount; reads carry a generation guard and the list length is the engine's.

**crypto-privacy** — not run. The diff touches no `crates/core` primitive, no key or seal material, and no trust-boundary read. Content root CIDs are content addresses, not secrets.

## Left undone

Puppeteer verification was not driven: the dialog needs a logged-in engine session and a file with a real write history, which the browser MCP cannot reach alone. Six manual steps are listed in the PR body instead.</result>

The dialog now holds its own `confirming` flag across the whole promise. The reviewer's extra claim that the parent modal also unlocks is rejected: `dismissible={pending === null}` already held it.
- `useFileVersions.ts` entries not cleared on node change — TAKEN. The effect clears `entries` on every node change and advances the read generation for a `null` node. The committable suggestion was not applied literally, because `+generation.current;` is a unary plus that never advances the counter; the landed code uses `generation.current += 1`.

## Report

**Head SHA:** `ee7bcd6008f7b552c3d30d648e912158bc28c284` (branch `feat/1095-web-version-history`, PR #1866, still a draft)

**Finding and disposition (1 item):** "Bind version operations to the active node" on `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/DetailsDialog.tsx` — **accepted and fixed**, not applied as written; the root cause is that the dialog's state names one node while the node arrives as a prop the parent can swap in place, so the fix enforces that rule in both owners instead of duplicating the node into `PendingWrite`.

I verified both halves of the mechanism on the PR branch before fixing:
- `DetailsDialog` keeps `pending` and `confirming` across a prop swap, so a confirmation raised for node A is answered against node B's hook with A's `contentCid`.
- An accepted write for A holds A in its closure, and its post-write `reload(A)` lands A's list on B. The hook's generation counter does **not** stop this, because `reload` advances the counter itself, so the late read always wins.

The fix: `DetailsDialog` holds a ref of the node it shows, retires `pending`/`confirming` in the render that changes the node, and ignores the outcome of a write whose node it has left; `useFileVersions.write` re-reads only while that write's node is still on screen. Two new tests were added, and I confirmed both fail on the pre-fix code by stashing the source changes.

**Review body sections:** the 20:09:40Z review carried neither an "Outside diff range" nor a "Nitpick comments" section — it reported one actionable comment and nothing else. The disposition comment states that explicitly.

**CI:** fully green on `ee7bcd600` — all Web, API, Repo, Contract Suite, Desktop, and E2E Smoke checks pass; Rust and Desktop Build were skipped by path filtering. Locally `pnpm -F @cipherbox/web typecheck`, `test` (750 tests), `pnpm lint`, and `pnpm lint:tracker-refs` all pass. The attribution-trailer grep printed 0 before the single push.

**Disposition comment:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1866#issuecomment-5703940107 — inline reply at https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1866#discussion_r4030393193, thread `PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM6jGTjj` resolved. Both bodies were read back and match the intended text.

One thing worth flagging for your judgement: the scenario is **not reachable through today's UI**, because the details dialog renders a full-screen modal backdrop over the row menu that is the only dispatcher of a node change. I fixed it anyway, since the hook already handles a node change correctly and leaving the dialog inconsistent with it would be the worse state — but if you would rather keep the diff minimal, this fix is the part that could be dropped.</result>

<result>Done. Report:

## Mechanism (with evidence)

**(a) the wait budget and shape — not the FUSE-T SMB cache, and not an engine defect.**

- Runs [35281835180](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/actions/runs/35281835180) and [35211355950](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/actions/runs/35211355950) stop at the mount that must read the tab's upload. The mock record store served every read 200 through the whole 30 s window and received **no PUT after 22:29:25.113**, so the record existed and the mount had simply not adopted it yet. The desktop host logs are one line each (the shell installs no tracing subscriber), so they prove nothing either way.
- Run [35181867749](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/actions/runs/35181867749) stops at the **fresh browser tab** listing `both-devices` at the vault root, over 178 passes, with ~239 record GETs all served 200. That leg has no mount in it, so an SMB enumeration cache cannot be the scenario's mechanism.
- Local reproduction on macOS (full stack, FUSE-T, probe inside the wait) showed the state directly: `readdir=["by-the-mount.bin"]`, `stat by-the-tab.bin → ENOENT`, mount status `{"deadLetters":0,"staleness":"fresh","warnings":[]}`. A `stat` is a lookup and does not read a cached enumeration, so the listing was not stale at the host — the convergence had not happened. It converged a pass later.
- When the scenario passes, these waits take 1.2–2.3 s, so this is not budget drift; it is a pass that needs a forced nocache refresh and the cross-device budget.

## Diff (tests/cross-client only)

- `converges` — the mount-side cross-device wait: refreshes the mount on every pass, runs on `convergeMs`, polls at `readIntervalMs`. `projects` keeps the local-read semantics for `offline-convergence` and `leader-failover` (both read back a write of the same mount, and one runs with the API down, where a refresh is not available).
- `sizes` — the length rides the child's own record, so it is now a wait of its own instead of a `stat` in the same pass that found the name. The equality it proves is unchanged.
- `passUntil` (every web-side listing wait) moves from `refreshMs` to `convergeMs`.
- `secondSession.ts` uses both and drops its two now-duplicated pre-call refreshes. No assertion weakened, no platform skipped.
- Local gates: `pnpm -r typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm lint:tracker-refs`, cross-client vitest (16 tests) — all green.

## Verification

Two sequential `ci-e2e.yml` dispatches on the branch, both **success**, `all 7 scenarios passed` in each:
- [35284638971](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/actions/runs/35284638971) — second-session passed in 6295 ms
- [35285292368](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/actions/runs/35285292368) — second-session passed in 9970 ms

Both include `Cross-Client E2E (macos)`, `Desktop E2E (macos/linux/windows)` and `Web E2E Full` green.

**#1873** — https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1873 — DRAFT, branch `fix/1872-second-session-macos`, head `b6bdff786`. `closingIssuesReferences` confirms **#1872**. All PR checks pass (CodeRabbit shows "Review skipped: draft pull request" — no review requested, per the brief). Body verified as rendered; no attribution lines anywhere (`grep -ci 'co-authored|generated with'` = 0 on commits and body).

## Review gates (inline, quoted from `/tmp/w19/lane-F1/review.md`)

- **simplify**: the two new waits shared one poll option block; they now call one private `afterRefresh`, so the budget and the release path are stated once. No speculative generality: both new helpers have live callers. Comments short, rationale once, no tracker refs.
- **security**: test-harness code only; crosses no trust boundary, reads no key material. No assertion weakened — only the time bound grows. One nocache refresh per pass at `readIntervalMs`, the cadence `fileRoundTrip` already uses. The `poll` clock seam is untouched.
- **crypto-privacy**: touches no `crates/core` primitive, no seal or key material, no wire format; no KAT or adoption gate in scope; no secret material in an assertion or a failure message.

## The mechanism, with evidence

**The event is not read by any code.** Job inputs on `push` and on `workflow_dispatch` are identical: same runner image (`macos-26-arm64 20260907.0351.1`), same env block, same five restored cache entries byte for byte (`desktop-e2e-macos-cargo-…` 801694089 B, etc.). `grep` finds no `GITHUB_*` read in `apps`, `packages`, `crates`, `tests`, `tools`, `scripts`.

**A push-shaped probe proved the ref is not the variable either.** I put main's tree on a throwaway branch with one extra line in `ci-e2e.yml` (the branch named in the `push` trigger). Run **35290471028**, `push` event, not main, no fix: FAILED with the same message, `second-session`, 97 reads, `["by-the-mount.bin"]`.

**What tracks the split is host speed.** Cache restore throughput: failing runs 45.6 / 51.6 / 57.4 / 62.6 / 82.8 MB/s; passing dispatch runs 167.8 / 207.8 MB/s. Same cache entries, ~3x slower hosts on the failing side. The scenario carries a race a slower host loses.

**The race (failure mode A).** `FilesPage.upload` drove the picker straight after `files.open(FOLDER)` (a bare `dblclick`). The app renders the upload target as `&lt;UploadPanel folder={settled ? folder : null} /&gt;` and `UploadPanel` renders `UploadZone` only when `folder !== null`, so the target unmounts while the next folder's listing loads. A picker driven in that window belongs to the folder just left and the file never reaches the engine — silently. Proof from the record store on both attempts of run 35286293426: **zero PUTs** after the mount's own writes, while the tab polled the store 910 times in the same 100 s; the upload call plus the settled read returned in ~200 ms. `second-session` is the only scenario where a tab opens a folder of a vault another client filled (listing must come off the network → wide window); `file-round-trip` downloads a file from the folder first, so it lands the listing and passes.

## The change (PR #1874, DRAFT, branch `fix/1872-second-session-macos-main`, head `ea038fd7d`)

- `tests/web-e2e/page-objects/files.page.ts`: `open()` waits for the breadcrumb trail to name the folder — the same condition the upload target mounts on.
- `tests/cross-client/src/scenarios/secondSession.ts`: the tab proves its own listing before the mount wait (replaces the root `settled()` read, which proves nothing for a subfolder write).
- `tests/cross-client/src/web.ts` + `runAll.ts`: a failed scenario now reports each tab's engine view and last 30 engine events.

## Verification

- Push-shaped runs on the probe branch: **35292912625 PASS**, **35293435310 PASS** (all 7 scenarios, `second-session` end to end). **35291144531** with the fix: the upload lands (`uploadCompleted` in the new engine report, the mount converged) and the scenario ran on to fail later at the second defect below.
- `pnpm -r typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm lint:tracker-refs`, `@cipherbox/cross-client-e2e` vitest: green. PR CI: **28 pass, 3 skipping, 0 fail**.
- Probe branch deleted; run logs persist. Recipe to recreate: branch off main, add the branch name to `on.push.branches` in `ci-e2e.yml`, push twice (the first push to a new branch fails `Detect Main Gate Changes`, because `event.before` is zero and the filter falls back to `HEAD~1`).

## Left undone — a second, separate defect (not patched, per the brief)

In run **35291144531** the second desktop session listed both children and then reported `by-the-mount.bin` at **0 bytes for 99 reads over 100 s**, each read after a manual refresh. It also reproduces on a local macOS FUSE-T stack. The name comes off the parent record, the length off the child's own record, so a cold session that lists a child and never sizes it is not resolving that child's record. That is the desktop read path (`crates/fuse` `getattr` → `ttl_check`), not the harness. It needs an owner; I filed nothing and folded the evidence into #1872 (comment `#issuecomment-5723437840`). #1872 is reopened; the PR says `Part of #1872.`, not `Closes`, because the gate is not fully green.

## Lane F3 report

**PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1875 — DRAFT, branch `fix/1872-cold-session-child-size`, head `95688ed58302b637472c2cd115a83da93fb93507`, one commit `fix(desktop): journal an acked write when the mount tears down`. `Closes #1872.` registers (verified through GraphQL `closingIssuesReferences`). No attribution line anywhere; commit trailer grep prints 0.

**CI:** all 39 PR checks pass, none pending, none failed. Two push-shaped probe runs on a throwaway branch: run 35304933337 and run 35304962872, **every job green in both**, `Cross-Client E2E (macos)` all seven scenarios and `Desktop E2E (macos)` all five. Probe branch `test/1872-f3-probe` deleted; the PR branch carries no probe line.

**The mechanism — it is a write-path defect, not a read-path one.** Tracing on a local macOS FUSE-T stack proved each step:

1. The second session resolves the child record correctly and repeatedly; the record itself carries no version: `run_files node=&lt;by-the-mount.bin&gt; versions=0` on every pass, against `versions=1` for the file the tab uploaded. A record with no version projects no length, so the read path answers 0 correctly.
2. The first session never published a version. Its op queue only ever held two `create` ops, and the only file record it authored was `File versions=0`.
3. The bytes did reach the mount, and the teardown found the handle still dirty: `write handle=HandleId(1) len=1536` at t, `unmount dirty_handles=[(HandleId(1), 1536)]` at t+595 ms. No `flush` and no `release` arrived between them, and `OperationCore::unmount` dropped the spill with no error on any path.
4. The kernel decides when the flush arrives. In `file-round-trip` on the same stack the SMB client behind FUSE-T deferred the close by **43.7 seconds**. That scenario passes only because its own waits keep the session alive that long. Any quit that follows a copy into `~/CipherBox` sooner loses the write.

**The fix:** `OperationCore::quiesce_writes` journals what every dirty handle owes, and `Projection::tear_down` runs it between the adapter quiesce and the unmount, under the existing shutdown budget. This matches `blueprint/desktop.md` "Lifecycle" — acked-but-unpublished ops are journaled before the engine stops — so no blueprint change and no ADR was needed. `crates/fuse/src/ops.rs` +50, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/mount/projected.rs` +3, `crates/fuse/tests/fuse_op_core.rs` +97.

**One correction found by CI, worth flagging.** The first shape of the fix passed every local suite but wedged `Desktop E2E (macos)` for 35 minutes on the runner, with `diskutil` left as an orphan process. A tracing probe pinned it to `drop(mount)`, immediately after the journal: `commit` pairs its op with a kernel invalidation, and a notify pushed after the quiesce holds the unmount open. The journal is now split from that invalidation — `flush` keeps the pairing, the teardown does not. Two of the four new unit tests assert exactly that rule in both directions, so the regression cannot come back silently.

**Review gates** (inline, `/tmp/w19/lane-F3/review.md`):
- *simplify* — the fix sits at its home; one method and one split, no option, no new type, no new module; scope is the orderly teardown alone, so no await enters the session loop; comments state each reason once.
- *security* — no trust boundary, no key material; no false ack, since a failed journal leaves the handle dirty and its spill unopenable as before; no liveness hazard, since the teardown journal raises no kernel notify; bounded by the open dirty handles and the shutdown budget; no clock and no RNG.
- *crypto-privacy* — no `crates/core` primitive, no seal, no key, no wire format, so no KAT and no adoption gate; the sealing is the content path that already ran for every flush; no secret material in an assertion.

**Nothing filed, nothing folded, no issue body edits** — #1872 is the auto-filed nightly issue and its body states a run result, which stays accurate.

**Left undone:** no CodeRabbit review requested and the PR stays DRAFT, as instructed. The external-unmount path (`detach`) still drops an acked write; I kept it out of scope deliberately, because journaling there would add a network-capable await inside the session loop, and that path already tells the member to sign out and back in.</result>

## Findings and dispositions

CodeRabbit's review (2026-09-18T04:15:19Z) had **2 inline findings and zero body-section items** — no "Outside diff range", no "Nitpick comments", no "Duplicate comments". Both are the same finding split across the two files.

**1. `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/mount/projected.rs:246` — "Preserve dirty writes when the shutdown deadline expires"** (Major). **Remedy rejected, one real part fixed.**
- The remedy ("transfer unfinished spills to durable recovery") is **impossible by construction**. `crates/fuse/src/spill.rs` module header states the rule at its home: the sealing key "is minted per handle from injected entropy, lives only in this process's memory, and dies with the handle." A spill file kept past the process is ciphertext with no key. Durable recovery needs a new key-custody design in `blueprint/desktop.md`, i.e. an ADR.
- **Not a regression.** On `main`, `unmount` dropped *every* dirty handle, always. This diff journals them first, so only handles the budget/an error could not reach can still be lost. Strictly reduces the loss surface. The neighbouring test `a_crash_between_the_spill_and_the_release_loses_the_write` is unchanged and still passes.
- "Do not unmount until preservation is complete" removes the bounded quit `blueprint/desktop.md` "Lifecycle" requires — and the PR body records that the first shape of this change wedged `Desktop E2E (macos)` at exactly that point.
- I verified the one claim that *would* have been a new defect: does a dropped future leave a half-formed op? No. `Engine::commit_write` (`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:9185`) takes the handle out of the ledger before its first await, and `commit_write_inner` journals the op only at its end. A cancellation leaves staged blocks and no op — fail-closed.

**2. `crates/fuse/src/ops.rs:864-887` — "Preserve dirty handles when journaling fails"** (Major). **Remedy rejected, same fix applies.**
- Same impossibility. Additionally: a failed `journal` acks nothing — `pending.dirty` clears only after `commit_write` returns `Ok`, and `push_version` aborts its write handle on any inner error. No caller and no queue is told the write landed. Identical shape to a failed `flush` on `main`.
- The discarded per-handle error is the point: one file the engine refuses must not strand the rest. Stopping at the first error loses *more* acked writes.

**The one real defect inside finding 1, fixed:** `pending` is a `HashMap`, so which handles the host's 5 s budget reached was **not stable between runs**. The dirty handle list is now `sort_unstable()`ed before the pass (`HandleId` derives `Ord`, and ids are assigned monotonically), so the budget cuts at the same place every run and the longest-owing handle is journalled first. One line plus a three-line comment; no new branch. I deliberately did not add a test — asserting handle order behaviourally needs decoding queued op bytes, which the fuse suite has no helper for, and a 2-handle test would pass ~50 % of the time without the sort, so it would be a vacuous gate.
