<result>Lane G2 is done. Draft PR is open and CI is fully green.

## PR
- **[#1878](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1878)** — `fix(web): gate write affordances on the scope permission and hold a batch save open`
- Branch `fix/1868-1869-web-share-affordances`, head `8aaf63da29`, **draft**, closing refs verified as `[1868, 1869]`, body carries no tool attribution.
- CI: **36 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipping** (`Desktop / Desktop Build`, not applicable). Two polls at an unchanged state. `CodeRabbit` reports "Review skipped: draft pull request" — no review was requested, the PR was not marked ready, nothing was merged.

## #1868 — mechanism and fix
`SnapshotView` carried no permission, so `/files/&lt;scope&gt;` could not tell a scope this vault owns from one it holds on a read grant. Every write affordance was offered; the write was queued and the refusal arrived from the drain, where the change dead-lettered.

The received-share pass already rebuilds a per-scope map each pass, so it now records the permission each bookmarked scope was granted under beside the sharer map. `SnapshotView` carries a `permission` for the scope enclosing the listed folder: a received share reports what its accepted grant recorded (the value `/shared` renders), every other scope is this vault's own plane and reports write. The browser gates on that one engine-reported field and reads it closed — only a reported `write` offers a write — so there is no TypeScript re-derivation. A read-only scope renders a short notice in place of the withheld controls.

Diff: `crates/engine/src/grants/grafted.rs`, `grants/received_status.rs`, `facade.rs`; `crates/wasm/src/lib.rs` getter; `packages/client` protocol, codec and wasm interface; `apps/web` `FileBrowser`, `FileBrowserActions`, `SelectionActionBar`.

## #1869 — mechanism and fix
A streamed save dropped its hidden frame and withdrew its stream ticket in the same task the read settled in. The read settling is the tab having pushed the last window, not the browser having committed the save, and a batch starts its next save in that same task — so one file landed empty or under a name of the browser's choosing, with no refusal to report. The buffered route already waits `REVOKE_AFTER_MS` before revoking its blob URL for exactly this reason; the streamed route did not.

The two deferred-teardown mechanisms are now one, the streamed route waits the same grace, and a batch waits it out before its next navigation, so a selection still leaves one live ticket and one frame at a time.

A second finding, implemented in the same PR: the staging batch profile could not have caught this. Parallel `waitForEvent` calls all settle on the first download, so its one-download-per-file assertion passed on a single save. The page object now collects on one listener, and the byte-for-byte and name read-back is back in the profile.

## Tests added
Engine (a read-granted scope reports read for the whole scope; the pass records the granted permission), wasm exhaustive getter test, `packages/client` codec, `apps/web` vitest for both defects, plus a local `@full` web-e2e batch save case and an assertion in the staging writable-share profile that the downgraded share offers no writes.

## Review passes (`/tmp/w19/lane-G2/review.md`)
- **simplify**: the two deferred-teardown mechanisms collapsed into one map and one helper; no speculative generality (`deferRelease` takes one closure and reuses the existing constant); the permission is one field with one producer and one consumer chain; `saveSelected` lost its fan-out of waiters; comments state each rule once, no absence-justifying prose, no tracker references.
- **security**: a stream ticket is a bearer capability to plaintext — its life grows by one grace, a batch still holds one at a time, and an unmount flushes every deferral; the read-only gate is defence in depth only, the engine still refuses the write; the permission crosses through `permissionFrom`, which throws on any unnamed value, and the browser gate reads closed; no new sink, no unbounded resource, determinism seams untouched; nothing sensitive logged or rendered.
- **crypto and privacy**: no `crates/core` primitive, KDF edge, wire format or KAT touched; the engine value is a copy of a permission the accepted grant already recorded and `/shared` already renders; plaintext handling in the save path is unchanged; zeroization ownership unchanged. Noted honestly: that recorded copy can lag an owner's downgrade exactly as the `/shared` badge does, and because the engine stays the fail-closed authority the lag can only offer a write the drain refuses, never hide a refusal.

## Issue hygiene and follow-ups
Neither body was stale — both describe `main` as it stood — so neither was edited. No new issues filed: every finding was implemented here, including the page-object defect.

## Left undone
Puppeteer was not driven. The read-only path needs two identities and an accepted grant, and the batch defect needs a controlling Service Worker and real downloads; both are covered by the e2e cases this PR adds or restores, and the PR body lists the manual steps. One consequence worth flagging for review: a batch save now costs about one extra second per file, which is the price of keeping exactly one live ticket and one frame while still letting each save commit.</result>

## Mechanism, with evidence

**#1870 is not an engine, API, front or deploy defect. It is a defect in the staging profile.**

I reproduced it on `https://app-staging.cipherbox.cc` (v2.1.1, after deploy run 35328126513 finished) with two fresh wallet identities and full network traces on both contexts. Both accounts were deleted by the fixture on every run.

Trace evidence, before the change:
- Owner `POST /mailbox/messages` → `201`, id `e84ed8ca-61e5-405c-8cc2-977c0e09caba`, at t+139 s.
- Recipient `GET /mailbox/messages` → `200` at t+141 s carrying that exact id, and on all 115 further polls through t+737 s (the full 10-minute wait).
- Recipient never sent `DELETE /mailbox/messages/&lt;id&gt;` and **never resolved a single IPNS name it had not itself published** — so the pass stopped before `fanout_get_verify`.

The only silent `continue` before that resolve is the sender filter in `crates/engine/src/grants/inbox.rs`: an item whose sender the recipient's own contact book does not anchor is dropped with no event and no ack. The staging profile exchanged contact codes in one direction only (recipient's code → owner). That filter is normative — `blueprint/engine.md`: "Identity keys only ever arrive out-of-band; there is no directory" and "verified against the contact-code-anchored key; unauthenticated items are dropped before a wasted resolve". The shipped share dialog already tells the member the same thing ("send this to them — an exchange needs both codes").

Confirmed by re-running with a two-way exchange: the recipient's `/shared` row reached standing `granted` and the recipient opened the shared folder, on both a read grant and a write grant.

**Ruled out:** the routing front (every record PUT answered `200` on both endpoints in my runs), a cached vacancy, the adoption gate, the mailbox transport and the API.

**The coordinator's `ERR_ABORTED` lead is a second, distinct, intermittent defect** — my runs saw no aborted publish in the sharing flow. I did not file it; it needs its own reproduction.

## Diff

`fix/1870-grant-delivery-staging`, head `fc771f0ee40369e5ea6fba3a7af17497eee2cc39`, 5 files, +90/−12, test-side only, no Rust and no production TypeScript.
- `staging/sharing.ts` imports the owner's code on the recipient before the grant.
- `SharePage.importContact` extracted; `grantTo` now calls it.
- New `tests/web-e2e/tests/contact-grant.spec.ts`, a merge-gating local profile for the hand-exchanged contact-code grant. Nothing in the PR gate covered that path past the import call — the green cross-client test uses an invite link, and the link hands the claimant the owner's contact bundle as part of the claim.

### Per-thread disposition (all 3 replied in-thread and resolved)

| Thread | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| `apps/web/.../FileBrowserActions.tsx` L130 | Mutation dialog survives a transition to read-only and its confirm still writes | **Taken.** The rendered dialog is now derived, not raw state: a dialog whose kind is in a `MUTATIONS` set is dropped while `writable` is false, so create/rename/move/delete/share/edit unmount on the transition; details/preview unaffected. |
| `apps/web/src/hooks/useFileDownload.ts` L169 | `saveAll` mints a ticket after unmount | **Taken.** `if (!mounted.current) break;` after `await settling.current`. |
| `crates/engine/.../received_status.rs` L320 | Stale recipient permission after a write-to-read downgrade | **Taken, with a design deviation.** Reconciled at the render from the owner's live commitment, not at the durable bookmark. |
| Nitpick: `crates/wasm/tests/boundary.rs` L314 | Assert the `permission` JS property | **Taken.** |

Zero outside-diff and zero duplicate items in the review body. Disposition comment covering every body item: https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1878#issuecomment-5730597255

### The one finding worth your attention

CodeRabbit's engine finding is real and it is the module's own stated law being broken by this PR: `received_status.rs`'s header says every fact must come from a live resolve, "never from the bookmark's own copy of the permission." The first commit copied exactly that. A downgrade re-signs the set at `Read`, rotates the write plane alone, and posts no fresh `SharePointer`, so `accept_share` never re-runs and the bookmark keeps `Write` forever.

I did **not** take CodeRabbit's proposed shape (rewriting the persisted bookmark). Instead a new `committed_permission` reads this recipient's blinded-tag entry out of `candidate.grant_section.commitment.entries` — the same authority `accept_share` uses — gated on a `Granted` verdict so an impostor record cannot raise a permission. `ReceivedVerdict` now carries the value so a scope the refresh damper skips keeps the last resolved permission rather than falling back to the bookmark. Persisting is left to the accept flow's existing `reconcile` self-heal; a per-pass durable write would add a store write to the steady state for a map that is rebuilt every pass anyway.

### Tests added (each confirmed red on the previous commit)
- `FileBrowser.test.tsx` — open rename dialog, land a `permission: 'read'` snapshot, dialog is gone.
- `useFileDownload.test.ts` — unmount during the first file's grace period, only one ticket minted.
- `received_status.rs` ×2 — a commitment demoted to `Read` overrides a bookmark at `Write`; and that value survives a damper-skipped pass.
- `boundary.rs` — the `permission` property crosses the JS boundary.

Verification run before the single push: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine` (2463 tests), `cargo check -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --all-targets`, `pnpm -r typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm lint:tracker-refs`, `apps/web` vitest (757 tests). Attribution grep over `origin/main..HEAD` printed 0.

<result>## Lane G4 report — #1880

**Outcome: no PR. The issue is a missing capability, not a defect in an existing path, and the build does not fit one lane PR.** The worktree `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box-w20-G4` (branch `fix/1880-write-grantee-build`) is clean and carries no commit.

### Mechanism, with evidence (traced on `main` at `5b7c0b951f`)

The recipient's upload never reaches the queue. It is refused synchronously at the facade boundary.

- `crates/engine/src/grants/received_status.rs:188` — `merge_grafted` plants an accepted scope root with `upsert_node` and **no `link_next`**. The doc states it: "The scope root has no parent here to name it."
- `crates/engine/src/facade.rs:2755` — `refuse_outside_vault` returns `EngineError::ScopeExitRefused` for any held node whose ancestors do not reach the render root. A grafted root is parentless, so every target below it fails. Call sites: `Command::Create` `facade.rs:6681`, `begin_write` `facade.rs:8977` and `:8988`, `base_sequence_for` `facade.rs:10670`, `relocation_anchors` `facade.rs:10527`.
- The behaviour is already test-locked: `facade.rs:11866` `a_journal_target_outside_this_vaults_tree_is_refused`, and `crates/engine/tests/write_plane.rs:14420`, whose doc states the law "a browse reaches its files but the write plane cannot author under them". `crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs:3530` says "no pass ever drains a grafted root".

Lifting the guard alone would not make the write land. Six further sites are unbuilt: the write scope seed is dropped at accept (`grants/accept.rs:948`); `ReceivedShare` persists no seed (`accept.rs:139`); the focus leg deposits the read seed only (`received_status.rs:562`); the drain builds passes only from the own-root boundary walk (`facade.rs:6183`, `facade.rs:6381`); a queued op below an unlisted root takes `Halt::Unclassified` on the wide outage budget (`sync/drain.rs:2357`); the content-key AAD binds the vault root scope (`facade.rs:9288`); and `merge_folder` (`sync/project.rs:155`) departs any locally authored child the sharer's body does not name. `ReceivedSharesList::writable_scope_refs` (`accept.rs:279`) has no production caller.

Issue #1558 landed the graft for reading only. `blueprint/engine.md` and `CONTEXT.md` already require the capability, so **no blueprint change and no ADR are needed** — the build is.

### Reproduction

Reproduced from code, not from a stack: the refusal is proved on `main` by two existing engine tests and is unconditional. No local web-e2e case drives a recipient write (`tests/web-e2e/tests/contact-grant.spec.ts` asserts a read grant only), and `tests/web-e2e/staging/writable-share.spec.ts` is the only case that writes as a recipient. A local stack run would only re-observe a refusal the tests already pin.

### GitHub actions taken

- Mechanism comment on #1880: https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/issues/1880#issuecomment-5731584717 — includes a dependency-ordered decomposition into seven steps (seed through accept → deposit on the focus leg → grafted `DrainScope` → scope-correct content-key AAD → relax the guard for write grants only → hold the grafted base against a local child → refuse the pass anything above the grafted root).
- #1880 body: appended a dated "Body check 2026-09-18" section correcting the speculative "Where to look" section; original text kept. Title shortened to `fix(engine): a write grantee cannot build inside the granted folder` (dropped "on the deployed front", which implied a deployment-specific fault).
- Folded a body-check correction into open issue #1868 instead of filing anything new: https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/issues/1868#issuecomment-5731595110 — its body claims the write "is accepted and queued" and dead-letters from a keyless-write-plane drain halt. Both statements are false; nothing is queued and no drain pass exists. This matters to PR #1878, which adds the scope `permission` to `SnapshotView`: the write affordances must stay disabled for a **write**-granted scope too until the engine can author there.
- No new issues filed (filing bar), no PR opened, no CodeRabbit request, nothing merged.

### Files shared with open PR #1878

None — I made no code change. For ordering, note that six of the seven decomposition steps land in `crates/engine/src/facade.rs`, `grants/received_status.rs` and `grants/accept.rs`, which #1878 also touches.

### Review gates

Not run: there is no diff. No `review.md` was written.

### Left undone and why

The fix itself. It spans the facade guard, the accept path, the focus leg, drain scope assembly, the content-key AAD, the grafted base merge and pass-level refusals above the grafted root, in trust-boundary code where the drain alone is 8302 lines. Landing it as one PR would be unsafe. I did not land step 1 in isolation either: persisting a write scope seed with no consumer is speculative material at rest and breaks the narrowest-derived-capability rule. **Recommendation for the orchestrator:** treat #1880 as the parent and decompose it into the seven slices in the comment, ordered as a dependency chain, with the first three as the next wave's engine lane. The staging writable-share profile must stay held.</result>

## Step → issue

| Step | Issue | Title |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Carry the write scope seed through accept | **#1882** (new) | engine: carry the write scope seed through grant accept |
| 2. Deposit the grafted write seed on the focus leg | **#1883** (new) | engine: deposit the grafted write scope seed on the focus leg |
| 3. Build a DrainScope per grafted write scope | **#1884** (new) | engine: build a drain pass for each grafted write scope |
| 4. Bind the content key AAD to the target's scope | **#1885** (new) | engine: bind the content key AAD to the scope the write target sits in |
| 5. Admit a target below a grafted write root | **#1886** (new) | engine: admit a write target below a proved grafted write root |
| 6. Hold the grafted base against a local child | **#1887** (new) | engine: hold a locally authored child in a grafted base |
| 7. Refuse the pass anything above the grafted root | **#1888** (new) | engine: refuse a grafted pass every vault-level surface above the grafted root |

No existing issue covered any step. ## Lane G2 / PR #1878 — G4's finding folded in

**New head sha:** `73a489269ca857f290c3afc78a22aed2d11cd0f7`
**CI:** 36/36 completed, all `success` except `Desktop / Desktop Build`, which is `skipped` by its own path filter. PR is still a draft, still closes only #1868 and #1869, no review requested.

### The change

The engine now reports the graft itself, and the browser gates on that rather than on the grant.

- `refuse_outside_vault` was split: the predicate it refuses on is now a named `outside_vault(rendered, node)`, and the refusal calls it. `Engine::snapshot` reports the same predicate as a new `SnapshotView.received_share`. The UI gate and the `ScopeExitRefused` refusal therefore read one condition and cannot drift apart — no second rule to keep in sync.
- `permission` is untouched. It still reports what the grant carries, and #1880's build will consume it. It simply does not decide the affordance yet.
- `apps/web` computes `writable = !view.receivedShare`. No route parsing, no bookmark-list re-derivation, no TS permission rule.
- Notice text now holds for both grant kinds: "this folder was shared with you. you can open and download what is in it. changes to a shared folder are not supported yet."
- `receivedShare` threaded through `crates/wasm`, `packages/client` (`WasmSnapshotView`, `SnapshotDescriptor`, `readSnapshot`) and the e2e page-object comments.

### One thing worth flagging

The first push of this change failed `Rust / Core KATs`. The boundary test caught that `#[wasm_bindgen(getter)] pub fn received_share` crossed as `received_share`, not `receivedShare` — this crate does **not** rely on auto-camelCasing, it spells every multi-word getter with an explicit `js_name` (`folderName`, `retainedRecords`). The TS side already declared `receivedShare`, so typecheck was happy and every property read would have been `undefined` at runtime, silently making every shared scope writable. Fixed with `js_name = receivedShare`; I swept the rest of the crate and no other getter is missing one. The boundary suite now passes locally under `wasm-bindgen-test-runner` as well as in CI.

That took a second push. The one-push rule covers the review cycle; a red required check left no alternative.

### Tests

- `crates/engine`: new `a_folder_under_a_grafted_root_reports_itself_a_received_share` — own folders and the root report false, a parentless grafted root reports true, and a `Command::Create` there returns `ScopeExitRefused`. The earlier permission tests are kept.
- `crates/wasm`: the boundary fixture is now a write-granted received share, and asserts `receivedShare` crosses as `true`; the native getter test asserts the false case.
- `apps/web`: the read-grant case was retargeted onto `receivedShare`, a new case asserts a **write**-granted share is gated identically, and the dialog-closing case now drives the transition through a shared scope. All three tests I added last cycle are kept.
- `packages/client`: the `readSnapshot` round-trip maps the new field.

## PR
**#1889** — https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1889
Branch `fix/1876-external-unmount-acked-write`, head `36d725ff1d26a1b22b5376a221a070ffdd2bd492`. Draft. `Closes #1876` registers (`closingIssuesReferences` = [1876]). No tool attribution in the body or in the commit (`grep -ci 'co-authored\|generated with'` over `origin/main..HEAD` prints 0).

**CI: 39 of 39 checks pass, 0 fail, 0 pending.** CodeRabbit was not requested (it reports "Review skipped: draft pull request").

## Mechanism and the fix
`Projection::detach` released the mount and the operation core together. A write the mount acked and the kernel had not flushed died there with its spill: no `updateContent` op, no error on any path. `blueprint/desktop.md` "Lifecycle" says an acked-but-unpublished op is journaled and drains on the next mount, so the detach path did not do what the blueprint says the mount owes.

- `detach` now only drops the mount, so the wake stays free of an await and `next` stays cancel-safe.
- `Projection::quiesce_detached` journals every dirty handle, then releases the handles and cached plaintext. The session loop awaits it in the body of the `Ended` arm, outside the select, so the select carries no network leg and no kernel call waits behind the journal.
- The bounded journal is one function (`journal_acked_writes`) that both teardown paths call, so the five-second budget is stated once. The orderly teardown keeps its order. The journal stays separate from the kernel invalidation, which is the wedge the first shape of #1875 hit.
- Test: `crates/fuse/tests/fuse_op_core.rs` — a teardown journals every dirty handle, not the first one only.

Files: `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/mount/projected.rs`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/engine/mod.rs`, `crates/fuse/tests/fuse_op_core.rs`.

## Why no e2e scenario, and the log evidence
I wrote a desktop e2e `external-unmount` scenario and probed it on two dispatched Desktop E2E runs. Neither host it can run on proves the journal, so the scenario is not in the PR.

- **macOS: the external unmount never reaches the app.** `diskutil unmount force` reported success, then the session answered `{"mount":{"state":"mounted",...}}` on 194 reads over 20 s. Run **35359338272**, job `Desktop E2E (macos)`. The FUSE-T server keeps its fuse session open after the SMB client disconnects, so `next_op` never ends. The detach path is therefore unreachable on macOS today — which is why I could not reproduce the loss locally on FUSE-T either.
- **Linux: the scenario is vacuous.** `fusermount3 -u` does end the session, but the kernel flushes on `close(2)` first. A throwaway branch carrying the scenario and **not** the fix passed the Linux leg (run **35359527854**). Holding a descriptor open to keep the handle dirty makes the unmount refuse with `EBUSY`, so that shape does not work either. The throwaway branch is deleted.

## Follow-up filed
**#1890** — "fix(desktop): an external unmount on macOS is never reported to the app". `post-cutover`, labels `v2-build` + `comp:desktop`, sub-issue of #994, with the run link and the 194-read evidence. No body or title edit was needed on #1876; it states the current code facts.

## Review passes (from `/tmp/w19/lane-G5/review.md`)
- **Simplify:** the bounded journal is stated once and both teardown paths call it; `detach` keeps only the state the wake needs; no new type, trait or unused option.
- **Security:** trust boundaries unchanged; a failed journal still leaves its handle dirty and its spill unopenable; bounded by the five-second budget so it cannot wedge the session loop; runs outside the select, with no kernel call behind it; the only clock is the host timeout the teardown already used.
- **Crypto and privacy:** no `crates/core` primitive, no key derivation, no wire format, no trust-boundary read. The spill key stays per handle and in memory. No secret material in logs or assertions.

## Left undone
No local FUSE-T reproduction: the macOS detach path does not fire, as #1890 records. I started no local mount and no desktop process, so nothing is left running; the shared docker stack is untouched.</result>

## Inline threads (both resolved, both real)

**1. `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/FileBrowserActions.tsx` — gate the version mutations in `DetailsDialog`. TAKEN.**
Mechanism confirmed: `details` is correctly outside `MUTATIONS` because the dialog itself only reads, but its version history was not a read — the `restore` and `rm` controls reach `versions.write`, which calls `facade.restoreVersion` or `facade.deleteVersion`. A read grant carries only a read seed, so those controls dispatched refused writes. `writable` now travels `FileBrowserActions` → `DetailsDialog` → `FileDetails` → `VersionHistory`; the two write controls render only under an engine-reported write permission, and the `dl` control stays because the read grant carries the seed a version download needs. An unanswered confirmation also retires: the render-phase reset in `DetailsDialog` now fires on a scope that turns read-only, as it already did on a node swap.

**2. `apps/web/src/hooks/useFileDownload.ts` — serialize all streamed saves. TAKEN.**
Mechanism confirmed: the invariant the code itself states, one live ticket and one frame at a time, was enforced inside `saveAll` only. A row or preview download raised during a batch calls `save` directly, minting a second ticket and iframe inside the grace period the batch was waiting out, and overwriting `settling.current` so the batch then waited on the wrong grace. The save body moved to `runSave`; `save` now appends every call to a single lane whose tail waits out the grace as well as the read. `saveAll` waits on `lane.current`, which keeps the unmount check after the grace.

Three tests added; each was verified to fail against the previous commit.

## CodeRabbit CLI

First run reported 2 major findings.
- `FileBrowserActions.tsx:76` — the derived `open` guard hid the mutation dialog but left its state, so a scope that turned writable again resurrected a dialog the member never re-opened. **Real** — fixed by clearing the state instead of deriving around it, which also leaves one source of truth. Covered by an extension to the existing `FileBrowser.test.tsx` read-only case.
- `useFileDownload.ts:152` — assign the buffered path's `deferRelease` to `settling.current`. **False positive**, verified empirically: applying it fails two existing tests (`revokes each blob url on its own timer, not at the end of the batch` and `leaves no revoke running once the tab drops the hook`). The buffered fallback shares no single-slot resource — each blob URL owns its own timer — so serializing it on the grace would stall a batch by `REVOKE_AFTER_MS` per file for no benefit. Not applied.

## PR
- **PR 1892**, branch `fix/1879-read-grantee-refresh`, head `cd162d54f476d4edf5a80c35f7442262cf29b6f0`. Draft. No CodeRabbit review requested, not marked ready, not merged.
- **CI: all 36 checks pass, 0 failures**, 1 skipped (`Desktop / Desktop Build`, path-conditional). Includes Rust Fmt+clippy, Workspace tests, Engine simulation tests, Core KATs, Web Engine WASM, Web E2E Smoke, Tracker Refs, lint-pr-title.
- Closes #1879 (verified through `closingIssuesReferences`). No attribution trailer in any commit or in the PR body (grep count 0).

## Mechanism, with evidence
The received-share pass is the **only** leg that resolves a grafted scope root: `focus_by_scope` in `crates/engine/src/sync/tick.rs` drops a scope's own root from the focus window, because a scope root never resolves through the child gate. So an open shared folder's listing moves only when `ReceivedShareStatus::refresh` re-resolves that share.

That leg damped every share with `on_access_refresh_due` (the profile's `stale_after`), reading the same way for a poll tick and for a forced `Command::ManualRefresh`. The facade already computes the pass mode and branches on it for the sibling pointer-consult leg, but did not pass it here.

Numeric fit is exact: the web host runs `SyncTimingProfile::PRODUCTION`, `stale_after` 90 s. The issue measured 5 shared-root resolves in 340 s (one per 68–85 s) against 66 own-root resolves (the 5 s click cadence). A record cache is excluded — the browser record transport sets `cache: 'no-store'` on every routing GET.

Unit-level reproduction, red on main:
```
---- a_forced_pass_renders_a_file_the_owner_added_after_the_grant stdout ----
  left: ["photos"]
 right: ["photos", "after-the-grant"]
```

## Flake verdict: same mechanism, engine bug, tests unchanged
The CI profile has a 1 s poll cadence and 3 s `stale_after`. A poll tick that classifies the share before its record is reachable (one run shows the aborted routing GET) stamps a verdict, and the test's single forced refresh inside the next 3 s carries that verdict forward instead of re-reading — the standing reads `none`. The blueprint's contract is that one forced refresh delivers it, which is exactly what both specs assert, so the fix is in the engine and `cross-client.spec.ts` / `contact-grant.spec.ts` are untouched.

## Diff
3 files, +149 / −60. `ReceivedShareStatus` carries the pass mode as a struct field (the `FolderRefresh` convention; the argument form tripped clippy's `too_many_arguments`); a forced pass re-resolves every bookmarked share; the poll leg keeps its damper; both stay capped at `MAX_RESOLVES_PER_PASS`. Two engine tests added, plus a shared `shared_scope_fixture`/`seed_scope_root` helper that de-duplicates the harness. `tests/web-e2e/staging/sharing.spec.ts` un-held (`test.fixme()` removed); the writable profile stays held under #1880.

## Issue hygiene
- #1879 body and title were accurate against current code — no edit needed. The mechanism is posted as [comment 5732846148](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/issues/1879#issuecomment-5732846148), read back and verified.
- No follow-up issue filed; the one finding was implemented in this PR.

## Review passes (from `/tmp/w19/lane-G3/review.md`)
- **simplify** — de-duplicated the test fixture builder into one helper; mode is a struct field, not an eighth argument; no new type, trait or flag; doc states the rule once and cites `blueprint/engine.md`; no tracker refs, no absence-justifying comments.
- **security** — trust boundary unchanged (every extra resolve still runs the whole adoption gate, commitment verify, cut-epoch and sequence floors, grant-section auth); fail-closed unchanged; resource use bounded by `MAX_RESOLVES_PER_PASS` (16), manual refreshes coalesce and the tick loop is the only pass executor, and the trigger is the host user not a remote party; determinism preserved (`now` and `mode` both injected); no secret in a log or a test assertion.
- **crypto-privacy** — `crates/core` untouched, no primitive, wire format or KDF edge; no key-custody or zeroization ownership moves; the added work is reads only; the extra resolves read the recipient's own bookmark names, only sooner; tests reuse the module's existing fixed test identities.

## PR

**#1893** — `feat(engine): carry the grafted write scope seed to its own drain pass`
Branch `feat/1882-1884-grafted-write-scope`, head `cb9e1a0facc30ef66d04a7c68056533c59c82f5f`, **DRAFT**, not merged, no CodeRabbit review requested.
Diff: 5 files, +538/−14, Rust only (`facade.rs`, `grants/accept.rs`, `grants/grafted.rs`, `grants/received_status.rs`, `sync/drain.rs`).

## CI

All green on the second attempt: **26 SUCCESS, 4 SKIPPED** (path-filtered: API, Desktop, two Web typecheck jobs), 0 failures. The first attempt failed only on `Rust / Workspace tests (Linux)`, in four `cipherbox-desktop-seams` keyring conformance tests, with `org.freedesktop.DBus.Error.NoReply: Message recipient disconnected from message bus`. That is the CI D-Bus secret-service daemon dying, not the diff — the diff touches no desktop seam. One re-run cleared it.

## Per slice

**#1882 — NOT closed.** This is the finding the brief asked me to check for, and it holds. `ReceivedShareStatus::open` re-opens the grant blob on every focus pass and takes the read scope seed straight from it; it never reads a persisted seed. `pointerReadKey` is persisted because a resolve needs it before any record is in hand, but the write scope seed has no such bootstrap need — it sits in the very blob that leg already opens. So a persisted row would be read by nothing in slice 2, slice 3, or later, and it would outlive the grant: the live path fail-closes when the owner cuts the grant and this device locates no blob at its tag, while a row at rest keeps the capability. I posted the finding on #1882 (comment `5732925726`) recommending it be closed as not needed, and fixed its two stale doc claims in the PR.

**#1883 — closed by the PR.** The focus leg recovers the write scope seed from the same grant blob under a committed write permission and deposits it in the per-scope write cell. Refused for a scope id this vault owns (the cells are keyed by scope id alone and the sharer authors that id, so a grant naming an own scope could have overwritten this vault's own write seed). Held to `deposit_write_seed`'s proof that the seed derives the shared root's published name. Stamped with the grant blob's read epoch and evicted by `evict_grafted_write_seeds` against the granting identity's read-epoch floor, so an owner rotation revokes the write capability with the read one.

**#1884 — closed by the PR.** One `DrainScope` per grafted scope that holds a committed write permission, both recovered seeds, a granting identity, and a rendered root name. `destination: None`, `ascent_node_seed: None`, `owner_identity` is the sharer's rather than this vault's. Its root joins the routing set, so a node below a grafted root resolves that root instead of halting unclassified. Grafted passes are appended *after* `charge_the_identity_to_one_pass`, so a grafted pass never takes the budget that answers for this vault's own keyless roots.

**Capability persisted, and why it is narrowest:** none at rest. In-session the material is the write scope seed itself, which is narrowest because `deposit_write_seed` proves it by deriving the root name from it and the drain derives every per-node name and signer from it; nothing narrower derives those, and a per-node key would not let the pass mint a node the sharer's body does not yet name.

## Review passes (from `/tmp/w19/lane-G6/review.md`)

- **Simplify** — dropped slice 1's persistence entirely (6 codec fields, a seal round trip and 4 tests for material no consumer reads); narrowed `GraftedWritePass` to private and cut its doc to one line; no new abstraction, the pass reuses `DrainScope`/`ScopeEnd`/`deposit_write_seed` unchanged; no invariant restated — the seed-names proof stays at `seed_names`, the floor-namespace rule at `floor_view`.
- **Security** — fail-closed gate is four-way (committed permission, seed present, not an own scope, seed derives the root name); own-scope collision closed; expiry under the granting identity's label with an own-scope skip so a sharer floor cannot evict this vault's material; nothing at rest; pass list bounded by `MAX_RECEIVED_SHARES` and `MAX_RESOLVES_PER_PASS`; identity charge ordering fixed; no clock or RNG added.
- **Crypto/privacy** — `crates/core` untouched, no primitive, codec or wire format changed; the seed goes HPKE-blob → `Zeroizing&lt;[u8;32]&gt;` → `CachedSeed` (zeroizes on removal and drop) and is never logged, encoded or persisted; zeroize at the terminal owner only, matching the read seed; no test asserts key material (tests assert cell membership, a derived public name, and an identity public key).

## Left undone, and why

One real gap, **folded onto #1886** (comment `5732923402`) rather than filed as a new issue: `Drain` holds one floor store for the tick and reads every epoch floor through it, under this vault's own namespace. A grafted pass that actually published would read and raise a sharer scope's floor in the wrong namespace, against the law `floor_view` states. Nothing can reach that path in this PR because the facade write guard stays closed and no op can be queued below a grafted root — #1886 is the slice that opens the guard, so it must land there first. The comment names the shape that works (carry the granting `ContactLabel` on the pass and read epoch floors through a `SharerScopedFloorStore`; `own` is a pass-through so own-vault passes are unchanged; sequence floors are `ipnsName`-keyed and need no change) and the exact `drain.rs` call sites.

**What slice 4+ needs from this PR:** the write seed is in `scope_write_seeds` keyed by grafted scope id, and the grafted root is in the pass's `scope_roots`, so #1885 can bind content-key AAD to the scope the target sits in and #1886 can admit a target below a proved grafted write root by consulting that same routing set. #1886 must also close the floor-namespace gap above.</result>

| Propagate the per-handle journal `Err` out of `quiesce_writes` | **Refused as written.** The per-handle `let _` is deliberate and documented at its home: one refused file must not strand the rest. Returning the first `Err` would lose every later handle — the very defect this PR removes. |

## Code changes made

- `crates/fuse/src/ops.rs`: `dirty_writes()` counts handles that still owe the queue; `release_stranded_write()` aborts a write handle a dropped pass left open.
- `apps/desktop/.../projected.rs`: `journal_acked_writes` returns the residual count and releases the stranded handle after the bound; the quit path prints it.
- `apps/desktop/.../engine/mod.rs`: `Warnings::record_unjournaled` raises an `unjournaledWrites` warning on the external-unmount path (count only, no record name).
- `apps/desktop/src/vault.ts` + `frontDoor.tsx`: the new warning kind and its label.
- Two new tests: `what_the_quiesce_could_not_journal_is_countable_before_the_unmount`, `writes_an_unmount_could_not_journal_are_never_silent`.

## CodeRabbit CLI

Two iterations found real defects the web review missed, both fixed:

1. **Leaked staging reservation.** `tokio::time::timeout` drops the journal future mid-flight, the one exit where `journal` cannot abort its own write handle. `Engine::begin_write` holds the staging reservation for the handle's whole life, and the engine keeps running past an external unmount, so the leak would refuse a later write room that is free. `OperationCore` now records the open handle; the host calls `release_stranded_write()` after the bound expires.
2. **Abort-ordering.** The slot is now cleared *after* `abort_write` returns, never before, on both the error path and the stranded path.

## Rebase onto main
`git reset --hard origin/feat/1882-1884-grafted-write-scope` then `git rebase origin/main` (main tip `ad7a3a78f`). One conflict, plus two silent-semantic checks:

1. **`crates/engine/src/grants/received_status.rs` — test fixture conflict (real).** Main's #1892 extracted `shared_scope_fixture(children)` and `seed_scope_root(records, fixture, sequence)` out of `RenderedScope::rooted_at`, for its `republish` helper. My slice 2 had replaced the same body with a permission-parameterised `RenderedScope::granting`. Resolved by mechanism, not by side: `shared_scope_fixture` now takes `(children, permission)`, `granting` builds through both of main's helpers, `rooted_at` delegates at `Permission::Read`, and `RenderedScope` carries a `granted: Permission` field so `republish` re-seals at the permission the fixture was created under instead of silently downgrading a write fixture to read. Both mechanisms hold: main's forced-pass republish tests and my write-seed tests run on one fixture.
2. **`ReceivedShareStatus::mode` struct literal (checked, auto-merged correctly).** The new `mode` field is present in the facade construction (`facade.rs:6695`) and in the test `pass_in`, alongside my `ScopeRender` fields (`write_seeds`, `own_root`, `own_descendants`).
3. **Forced pass runs the write path (verified).** The deposit sits in `ReceivedShareStatus::open`, which a share reaches only when it is `due`; under `ResolveMode::NoCache` every bookmarked share is due, so a forced pass deposits and re-proves the write seed as well as re-resolving. The eviction legs (`evict_grafted_read_seeds` / `evict_grafted_write_seeds` in the facade tick) are not damped by mode at all, so they run on both legs.

## CodeRabbit CLI
`coderabbit review --agent --base-commit ad7a3a78f`. First attempt died on a WebSocket close; the immediate retry ran, no cooldown was reported. One finding:

- **major, `received_status.rs` around the deposit — the write-seed cell is not cleared when a grant stops granting write. ACCEPTED in part, fixed by mechanism.** Revocation and bookmark removal were already covered (`evict_grafted_write_seeds` drops any scope absent from `GraftedSharers`, and raises out a seed under a risen read-epoch floor; both have tests). The real hole was the third case CodeRabbit named: an owner that re-seals the grant blob down to `Read` need not move any floor, so the cached write seed survived the downgrade until some later floor rise. The deposit is now an explicit two-way decision on non-own scopes — deposit when the committed permission is `Write` and the blob carries a write seed, otherwise remove the cell entry (removal zeroizes, `CachedSeed` holds `Zeroizing`). Own scopes are still skipped, so this vault's own write seed is never touched by a sharer-authored `scopeId`. New test `an_owner_downgrade_to_read_drops_the_cached_write_seed`. The suggested extra transition tests for revoke and bookmark removal were not added — those paths already have named tests in `grants::grafted::tests`.

The fix was folded into the slice-2 commit rather than added as a fourth commit, so the one-commit-per-slice layout holds.

<result>Lane G7 report.

**PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1895 (draft) — branch `fix/front-contract-aborted-publish`, head `081d707c4a1a34afe2dc87c564a21623a673e097`. CI: all 31 checks pass (Repo, Rust Result, API, Contract Suite, Web, Web E2E Smoke, Web Engine WASM, Desktop, zizmor, lint-pr-title); Desktop Build, Rust and Web Bundle report `skipping`; CodeRabbit reports `Review skipped: draft pull request`. No CodeRabbit web review was requested, the PR stays a draft, and nothing was merged.

**Who aborts, with evidence.** Nobody refuses the publish. The routing front answers it, and the browser then drops the answer. I ran the same journey against staging with a fresh identity, instrumented with request start, response and failure times. Each name shows a pair one millisecond apart, for example `34258ms START PUT .../ipns/k51qzi5uqu5dk1dg...`, `34477ms RESPONSE PUT 200 after 219ms`, `34478ms FAILED PUT net::ERR_ABORTED after 220ms`. The cause is in `packages/client/src/seams/recordTransport.ts`: `putRecord` reads `response.ok` and never reads the answer body, so the browser cancels the unread body and reports a failed request after a landed answer. The read leg shows the same pair whenever the transport calls `response.body.cancel()` on a vacancy. The watcher in `tests/web-e2e/staging/frontContract.ts` listens to `response` and `requestfailed`, so one landed publish entered `publishes` and `refusedPublishes` at the same time. Ruled out: the front (the publish preflight answers 204 with `PUT` and `content-type` allowed, and a record PUT is answered, not dropped), the 30 s `AbortSignal.timeout` (the aborts fire 0.2 s after the request starts), and the engine fan-out (`crates/engine/src/net/fanout.rs` stops polling losing legs but sets no abort signal).

**Verdict: (a), a test defect.** No product defect, so no issue was filed. History check: the profile failed on all six staging runs since it landed with #1831 and #1867 — it never passed.

**Diff** (two files, test only): the watcher records the request object of every publish the front answered 2xx and does not count a later failure of that same request as a refusal; keyed on the request object, not the URL, so a genuinely refused second publish of the same IPNS name is still counted. A new control stages the answer-then-drop shape against a local front that never finishes the answer body, because `page.route` always serves a whole body.

**Proof.** The whole file passes against deployed staging with a fresh identity, which the fixture removed: 6 passed. The new control fails with the watcher reverted (`an answered publish is never refused`). The three negative controls still pass, so the check still refuses a blocked publish, a 502 publish and a cacheable vacancy. CodeRabbit CLI on the diff: `{"type":"complete","status":"review_completed","findings":0,"reviewedFiles":["tests/web-e2e/staging/front-contract.spec.ts","tests/web-e2e/staging/frontContract.ts"],"outcome":"completed","message":"Review completed"}`.

**Review passes** (full text in `/tmp/w19/lane-G7/review.md`). Simplify: one WeakSet and one guard clause, no new abstraction, the local front is the only way to stage the shape and the reason is stated once. Security: the three negative controls keep the check able to fail red; the request-object key closes the bypass a URL key would open; a publish with no 2xx answer is still refused; the control server binds 127.0.0.1 on an ephemeral port and closes in a `finally`; no secret enters a log or an assertion. Crypto and privacy: the diff touches no crate, primitive, key material or wire format; the watcher records URLs and `cache-control` only, so the accelerator pseudonym stays out of the report artifact.

**Body checks and follow-ups.** No issue body or title needed a correction, and no open issue tracks this failure. Considered and rejected, recorded in the PR body: draining the publish answer body in `putRecord`, because the answer comes from an untrusted public endpoint and a drain needs a cap, while the cancel is correct for an answer the transport does not use.

<result>All checks green. Round complete.

## Per-thread disposition

**1. `grants/grafted.rs:413` — "Invalidate grafted capabilities on a no-blob revocation" (Major) — TAKEN IN PART**

Mechanism confirmed real. `ResolutionClass::RevocationSignal` carries the prior permission forward and never calls `open`, so both cached seeds survive. A cut that only re-seals the committed set moves no read-epoch floor, so `evict_grafted_write_seeds` never reaches them either. The next tick's `grafted_write_passes` had permission, both seeds, sharer and name — a live write pass on a revoked share. This also contradicted the PR's own doc claim that "the capability lapses with the grant".

Fix in `ReceivedShareStatus::refresh`: on a `RevocationSignal` for a scope this vault does not own, the row drops to `Permission::Read` and both cached seeds are removed on that pass. In memory only — the commitment covers each row and not the blob set, so a stripped blob destroys nothing at rest and a later granted pass re-deposits. Guarded by `!is_own_scope`, since a sharer authors the `scopeId` it bookmarks under; without that guard any contact could cut this vault off its own plane.

Rejected with evidence: dropping the sharer entry (`grafted_sharers` is the floor namespace a revoked share's leg must keep reading — it is rebuilt each pass over every renderable scope for exactly that reason); re-basing eviction on the cut-epoch floor (separate durable bar, separate namespace, and every seed stamp is a read epoch); clearing the rendered root (unchanged read-plane design, outside this diff).

**2. `grants/received_status.rs:663` → `install_descendant_scopes` (Major) — TAKEN**

Confirmed: the write seed is deposited only on `Ok`, and no path removes a pre-existing entry. Past promotion the scope is in `own_descendants`, so the eviction pass takes the own-scope arm and never measures it again. The `Ok` arm has the same hole, because `deposit_write_seed` refuses a seed that does not name the root and leaves the old entry standing. Fixed by removing the entry at the promotion boundary only (reached minus already-known, taken before the extend), before the deposit. A scope already in the set keeps its seed, so a transient dark write plane does not drop proved material.

**3. `sync/drain.rs:8237` — test the grafted descendant (Minor) — CONCERN TAKEN, SUGGESTION REJECTED**

Measured both ways. The concern is real and worse than stated: I replaced the ancestor-chain lookup with an exact-target one and all 1829 engine unit tests passed; only two `mount_convergence.rs` integration tests caught it. But the literal change fails — with `FOLDER` the call returns `Err(Halt::Unclassified)` in both arms (the harness serves no child body), so the plane-root assertion dies and the halt no longer discriminates. The case now runs both targets on fresh harnesses: `HARNESS_ROOT` keeps the plane-root assertion, `FOLDER` asserts the pass holds `HARNESS_ROOT`, which is true only when the chain routed through the parentless root. Verified to fail under the mutation.

## CLI result

First run: 1 major finding — the removal arm beside the deposit runs only where `open` opens the grant blob, so a row the pass may not render (contested, unrenderable) keeps a stale write seed. Real, though not exploitable on its own since `grafted_write_passes` also filters on permission. Fixed: the cached write seed now follows the live permission on every arm. Second run verbatim:

## Judgement on a second web review

**Yes, it deserves one.** The round is not cosmetic: two of the four changes alter authorization-bearing state transitions in trust-boundary code (a revocation boundary that now mutates the permission the host gates write affordances on, and a promotion boundary that drops key material), plus a new unconditional clear keyed on the live permission. That is roughly 90 changed lines across two files, all of it in the fail-closed path this PR introduced. The local CLI is clean, but it did not catch the promotion or revocation holes in the first place — the web review did. A second pass over the new boundaries, particularly the interaction between the `!is_own_scope` guard and the `permission != Write` clear, is worth a slot.</result>

## Report — lane G9, PR #1900

**PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1900 — DRAFT, branch `feat/1885-grafted-write-aad`, head `ac563369fa4ef07f534809c2ef2c3a1bd99889ce`. Not marked ready, not merged, no CodeRabbit web review requested.

**CI:** 30 checks, all COMPLETED. 28 SUCCESS, 2 SKIPPED by path filter (`API`, `Desktop`, plus the three `Web /` sub-jobs that no TS change triggers). No flakes, no re-runs needed.

**Issues closed:** #1885 (verified: `closingIssuesReferences` = [1885]).

### Order verdict

Both #1887 and #1888 can be built and tested with the facade write guard closed, so #1886 goes last.

- #1887 lives in `merge_folder` plus its exemption plumbing. The projection unit tests build snapshots and resolved bodies by hand, and the focus-leg harness already plants a parentless grafted root.
- #1888 lives in the drain. The drain test module already builds a grafted `DrainScope` with a parentless source root and `destination: None`, and queues ops through the staging store.
- Neither harness consults `refuse_outside_vault`. #1887 and #1888 also do not depend on each other, so they can run in parallel.

I deliberately shipped **#1885 alone**. #1887 needs its own durable hold record — a persistence-surface design decision that does not fit one reviewable PR. #1888 is a separate concern with its own blast radius in `crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs`, and the sharer-scoped floor namespace lane G6 reported belongs with it. Both are now unblocked and launchable immediately.

### What #1885 turned out to be

The issue's stated mechanism is wrong, and the real defect is wider. The content key blob is HPKE auth-mode sealed to the **writer's own** enc subkey, so no sharer ever opens it and no scope seed enters it — the two "Done when" items about a grantee opening the blob are unreachable. The real bug: the commit bound `snapshot.root`, while `upload_blocks` opened under `scope.source.root` — the root of the pass that publishes the op. Those agree only for a target in the vault's own root scope. **Every** scope root below the vault root hits the mismatch, and an AAD failure is classified permanent, so the version is dead-lettered and its staged blocks released — silent data loss, already reachable by a promoted own interior scope.

Fix: the commit resolves the authoring scope from the write target, reads that scope's floor in the right namespace (granting identity's for a grafted scope via `floor_view`, own for every root the session authors under itself, fail-closed when a grafted scope names no identity), and the pair rides the op record so both readers open under the carried value. The record is HPKE auth-sealed to the writer's own enc subkey, so the carried pair is authenticated to the same degree as the blob.

**Own-vault byte identity:** for a target in the vault root scope the resolved scope *is* the render root and the floor read is the same own-namespace read, so the AAD inputs are identical — the seal bytes cannot differ. Proved behaviourally: the full pre-existing write-plane suite stays green, and `a_write_binds_its_key_blob_to_the_scope_root_it_is_authored_under` asserts the vault-root write still binds the vault root. `content_key_aad`, `seal_content_key`, `open_content_key` and the core KAT set are untouched — no format change, no ADR needed.

**Red-before-green proof:** I reverted the seal-site expression and confirmed `a_write_binds_its_key_blob_to_the_scope_root_it_is_authored_under` and `a_key_blob_does_not_open_under_another_scope` both fail on the parent commit.

**Diff:** 10 files, +282/−13. Engine only, no TS. Kept clear of lane G10's regions (facade focus grouping ~6200-6360 and `sync/tick.rs`).

### Review passes (`/tmp/w19/lane-G9/review.md`)

- **Simplify:** one new private method, one new field, three changed call sites. The render the commit already took for a `NewFile` target is now taken once and reused for both target shapes, so the change removes a duplicate read. No new public surface or trait. Comments state each rule once, none defends an absent path.
- **Security:** the value the drain opens under comes from the op record, HPKE auth-sealed to the writer's own enc subkey, so it is as authenticated as the blob. `authored_scope_epoch` fails closed on a grafted scope with no granting identity rather than measuring it in this vault's namespace. No clock or entropy added; determinism seams unchanged. No secret logged, asserted on, or newly retained.
- **Crypto/privacy:** blob format untouched; only the scope value the engine chooses changes, and only below a scope root of its own. Cross-scope refusal still holds and is now test-locked. No key bytes in assertions. Core KATs supply fixed scope/epoch inputs, so they are unaffected.

<summary>Agent "Design study for issue 1887" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result># Design study for #1887 — hold a locally authored child in a grafted base

## 1. How the own vault holds such a child today

A create never enters the base. The command seals an op and queues it (`facade.rs:11072`, `sync/staging.rs:81`). The row comes from the overlay, which is recomputed from the queue on every render (`sync/overlay.rs:33`, `facade.rs:10554`). The overlay entry and the queue entry have one lifetime.

After the publish, the drain writes the child into the base itself. It repaints the parent from the children it just published (`drain.rs:5677`, `drain.rs:5846`), and it repaints before it removes the op (`drain.rs:1775`), so no render loses the row. A later merge cannot depart the child either: the publish raises the record's sequence floor, and a folder body at or below that floor is rejected before `merge_folder` sees it (`net/child.rs:349`, `gate/floor.rs:258`).

## 2. Does the grafted path already have this?

A write grant carries the write scope seed at the grant root (`CONTEXT.md:27-28`), so the grantee signs and publishes the parent folder body itself. The blueprint says the sharer only catches up on pin rows (`blueprint/api.md:135`). The grantee's drain therefore runs the same repaint and the same floor raise. The premise in the issue body — that the row dies when the op drains — holds only if the grafted pass repaints under a floor namespace the read legs do not read. The read legs read the sharer-scoped namespace (`facade.rs:6337`); the drain reads one `self.floors` per pass (`drain.rs:5771`), and that namespace is the item lane G6 already reported on #1886.

So the first act of this slice is a test, not a store.

## 3. Options

1. **No new record.** Prove the repaint and the floor with an engine test that drives an upload, a drain and a following pass in a grafted scope. Align the grafted pass's floor namespace with the read legs. Cost: one test file and a small wiring change. Benefit: no persistence surface at all.
2. **A bounded hold set inside the received-share record.** Each bookmark gains a list of node ids this device authored that the sharer's body has not named. No key material, no new key prefix, no new seal kind, no KAT change, no seam change; the hold dies with the bookmark when the grant is cut. The two merge sites already share `GraftedPlane::split`, so the ids pass through the existing `withheld` slice and `merge_folder` stays unchanged.
3. **A new staging prefix and a new `OwnerLocalKind`.** Rejected: a new kind is a frozen registry entry with cross-kind negative vectors (`blueprint/core.md:480`) for data option 2 already carries.

**Recommendation: option 1, with option 2 as the fallback inside the same PR.**

## 4. Risk and PR fit

A departure in a grafted scope now feeds the owner capture path, and a grafted root is drained since #1893, so the stale claim at `drain.rs:3531` must be restated or the recipient can bin its own live file.

One reviewable PR, guard closed. No ADR: this repository gates none, and neither option touches a wire format.</result>

## PR

**#1901** — `feat(engine): hold a grafted drain pass inside the scope it was granted`, branch `feat/1888-grafted-pass-boundary`, head `7a30864959255c07c3deabbe4ca0d88be5e59217`, **draft**, not merged, no CodeRabbit web review requested. ## The surface list

These are the vault-level surfaces a grafted pass reached in `crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs`, and the disposition of each. The table is also in the PR body.

| Surface | Where | Now |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Owner's bin index — soft-delete entry | `publish_delete`, `to_bin` branch | Refused |
| Owner's bin index — restore reads and drops the entry | `publish_restore` | Refused |
| Owner's bin index — purge reads and reclaims | `publish_purge` | Refused |
| Owner's bin index — capture adoption | `adopt_observed_unlinks` | Captures dropped, bounded set not starved |
| Owner's bin index — retention sweep | `expire_bin_entries` | Sweep does not run |
| Retire ledger and doomed journal — hard delete | `publish_delete`, doomed branch | Refused |
| Retire ledger — version prune and version delete | `journal_retire_debt` | Refused |
| Doomed journal — settle replay | `settle_journalled_deletes` | Replay does not run, no budget spent |
| Cuts this vault owes on its own scopes | `cut_exited_scopes` | Cut not driven |
| Identity-wide charge | `charge_the_identity_to_one_pass` | Never lands on a grafted pass |
| Another pass's captures | `take_captures` | Already scoped; comment corrected |

Every refusal is a plain `Err` return of `Halt::Permanent(DeadLetterReason::GraftedScopeVaultSurface)` — a new reason wired through the staging tag codec, the WASM boundary ordinal, `packages/client` and the `apps/web` dead-letter notice. The refusal tests also pass under `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine --release`.

## Own-pass identity proof

`ScopeEnd` carries the granting contact's `ContactLabel`, and one accessor picks the floor namespace for every floor call of a pass. The own arm is `SharerScopedFloorStore::own`, whose `scoped` is a `Cow::Borrowed` pass-through, so an own pass's durable keys are byte-for-byte unchanged. Proof: the whole 1844-test engine unit suite plus 23 integration binaries are green with no fixture change, and one new test raises through an own end's view and reads the value back at the plain scope-id key on the bare store. The bin-index loads and publishes keep the unscoped store, because the bin index is this vault's own record.

## The two coordinator notes

1. **Floor alignment for #1887 — done and pinned.** A grafted pass reads and raises its epoch floors under the granting contact's label, which is the namespace `grants::grafted::floor_view` gives every read leg below that root. The test `a_grafted_end_ratchets_where_the_read_legs_read_and_an_own_end_does_not_move` raises through the pass's view and reads the value back through `SharerScopedFloorStore::granted_by` built from the same sharer, and asserts the own namespace stays empty. A second test pins that sequence floors stay shared, so a grafted pass's post-publish raise bars a replay for every leg that reads that name. #1887's lane can build on both.
2. **`take_captures` comment — corrected, and the path did need a refusal.** The clause "no pass ever drains a grafted root" is removed and the comment restated to what the code does. The capture path is a real hole: a departure inside a granted scope now feeds `adopt_observed_unlinks`, and binning it would re-key the sharer's node under this vault's own bin held key, ending the owner's access. It is in the surface list and refused, and the captures are dropped rather than put back so the bounded set cannot starve.

## Review passes (quoted from `/tmp/w19/lane-G11/review.md`)

**Simplify.** One predicate (`DrainScope::is_grafted`) and one refusal (`refuse_vault_surface`) serve every surface, so no site repeats the rule. One accessor (`ScopeEnd::floors`) decides the floor namespace for every floor call of a pass, so the two arms are stated once. The stale absence-justifying clause in `take_captures` is removed rather than extended. No new type, no new trait and no new generic parameter beyond the two adopter signatures the view needs. `is_grafted` is private to the module.

**Security.** Trust boundary: the granting label comes from `GraftedSharers`, which the live resolve builds from a verified grant, never from a bookmark. Fail-closed: every refusal is a plain `Err` return, release-active, and the op dead-letters with a reason the member can read. Resource exhaustion: a grafted capture now leaves the bounded unadopted set instead of being retained by every pass for ever, and a grafted pass spends none of the tick's journal-open budget. Concurrency: nothing new is shared or ordered. Determinism: no clock and no RNG is read; the identity-wide charge picks the first non-grafted pass, which is a pure function of the pass list.

**Crypto and privacy.** No new KDF edge: `ContactLabel::of` is the existing `kdf::contact_label` edge, used exactly as `grants::received_status` and `net::rotation` use it. No wire format, no seal and no KAT is touched; the only new durable value is local dead-letter tag 19, which round-trips in its own test. The label is a deterministic function of this account's own seed and a public identity key, it never leaves the device, nothing logs it, and no new material is held past the pass that borrows it. The change closes a real hazard: a sharer's `scopeId` that collides with this vault's anchored all-zero root id could raise this vault's own epoch floor through the drain, which is a permanent lockout the ratchet has no descent from. It also stops a grantee re-keying a sharer's node under this vault's bin held key.

`coderabbit review --agent --base-commit 41bbbd6de` reported **0 findings** over all 11 changed files.

## Left undone, and why

- **One commit, not one per concern.** The two concerns share the `sharer` field on `ScopeEnd`, and their hunks interleave inside one region of `drain.rs`. Splitting them by hand risked a wrong intermediate, so they ship as one commit whose message names both. A squash merge collapses them either way.
- **No issue body edit.** #1888 asks for a refusal of "a vault pointer re-point". The drain plans none — a re-point is authored by the rotation paths, and the site the issue cites is the reclaimed-root `HeldKey::ScopePointer` removal on the reclamation path, which this PR refuses whole. The "Done when" list is satisfied by the table; the body states current code facts otherwise, so nothing was rewritten.
- **No follow-up issue filed.** A write grantee cannot delete, restore, purge or prune inside a granted folder while this stands. That is the fail-closed answer the slice asks for, and the capability question belongs to #1880, which stays open until the staging profile runs green.
- **The "completes and publishes" half of the acceptance list** is covered by a negative: an op that stays inside the sharer's scope is not refused. A full grafted publish needs the facade write guard, which #1886 opens.</result>

## Conflicts and resolutions

**1. `crates/engine/src/facade.rs`, the `crate::seams` import (only textual conflict).** Both sides added a name to one `use` list: main added `SharerScopedFloorStore` (used by #1900's `authored_scope_epoch`), this branch added `ContactLabel`. **2. Silent semantic conflicts.** None broke the test target. `#1900` added `StagedContent.scope` and updated every fixture in `op.rs`, `overlay.rs`, `rebase.rs`, `record.rs`, `staging.rs`; this branch added no `StagedContent` literal, so the two merged cleanly. `cargo check -p cipherbox-engine --all-targets`, the debug suite and the release suite all build and pass.

## Namespace-rule check (a) — a real divergence was found and folded

Before the rebase there were two ways to pick the sharer-scoped floor view:

- `grants::grafted::floor_view` (main, used by #1900) decides the **owned arm ahead of the sharer map**: `is_own_scope` first, the map second, `None` when neither answers.
- `facade::grafted_write_passes` (this branch) read `sharers.get(scope_id)` **directly** and built the label from it, with no own-scope precedence.

`grafted_sharers` is built in `received_status.rs` from the bookmark list with no `is_own_scope` filter — its own comment says "a sharer authors the id it bookmarks under", which `floor_view` deliberately does not trust (its doc: "a bookmark that names one of this vault's own roots cannot redirect that root's leg", and `grafted.rs` tests that case with `GraftedSharers::from([(OWN_ROOT, SHARER)])`). So a bookmark naming one of this vault's own scope roots could have produced a grafted write pass that ratcheted an **own** scope's epoch floors under a contact label.

The fold:
- New `FloorNamespace { Own, GrantedBy(ContactLabel) }` with `view(&amp;floors)`. No `Debug` derive — the label is the cross-scope correlator the blinded tag exists to deny.
- New `floor_namespace(...)` holds the choice rule once; `floor_view` becomes `floor_namespace(...).map(|ns| ns.view(floors))`, so every existing caller is unchanged.
- `ScopeEnd.sharer: Option&lt;ContactLabel&gt;` became `ScopeEnd.floor_namespace: FloorNamespace`; `ScopeEnd::floors` delegates to `view`, and `DrainScope::is_grafted` matches on `GrantedBy`.
- `grafted_write_passes` now takes the namespace picker (as a closure, to stay inside clippy's 7-argument limit) and returns no pass for a scope that resolves to `Own`.
- New test `a_bookmark_that_names_a_scope_this_vault_owns_drains_no_grafted_pass`, covering both the vault root itself and a proved descendant scope root.

## Staging tag codec check (b)

No collision. `#1900` added a field to the op record (`StagedContent.scope`) and **no** `DeadLetterReason`, so tag 19 remains this branch's alone. `every_parked_reason_round_trips_its_tag` — which also asserts that tags `1..=len` all decode and `len+1` does not — passes in both debug and release.

<result>PR #1903 is open as a draft and all of its CI checks pass. The reopened condition on #1879 is not a read defect: the owner's file never published. The engine fix is already on `main` in PR #1900, so this PR changes tests only.

- **PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1903
- **Branch:** `fix/1879-new-file-reaches-grantee`
- **Head:** `b591d1b96bdf1e2c74d9d44ce91605f2d19bf10d`
- **Closes:** #1879 (one bare `Closes #1879.` line; GitHub lists #1879 as its closing reference)

**The mechanism, with evidence**
- **Staging screenshots:** the Playwright report of staging run 35398134501 shows the owner page with `[!] 1 change will never publish`. The upload row says `contentUnrecoverable, so this upload will never publish`, and the `granted` listing is empty. The recipient page shows the grafted scope root open and empty.
- **Engine cause:** the grant makes the shared folder a scope root of its own. On v2.2.0 the commit sealed the content key blob under the vault root scope, but the drain opened it under the new scope root. The key did not open, so the drain dead-lettered the op. PR #1900 merged after v2.2.0 was cut, and it fixes this at its home.
- **Why the staging spec did not stop at the owner:** the `published()` wait only looked for pending marks. A dead-lettered write leaves the listing, and that also clears its mark. The wait passed, and the spec then waited 300 s on the recipient for a file that did not exist.

**Why #1892 was not enough:** #1892 is correct and still applies, because the forced pass does re-resolve every bookmarked share. But it cannot show a file that the owner never published.

**Proof before and after #1900:** I ran two `web-e2e.yml` smoke dispatches with the new spec.
- v2.2.0 (`41bbbd6de`) plus the spec, run 35415079996: fails at the dead-letter notice, with the same `contentUnrecoverable` message as staging.
- `main` (`f95370d6f`) plus the spec, run 35415081459: passes. The owner's write publishes, and the recipient lists the file after a nocache refresh.

I deleted the throwaway proof branch after the runs. The engine tests for both halves also pass on `main`: `a_file_written_inside_a_granted_scope_root_publishes_its_version` and `a_forced_pass_renders_a_file_the_owner_added_after_the_grant`.

**Diff summary (tests only, 4 files)**
- `tests/web-e2e/page-objects/files.page.ts`: new `FilesPage.published()`. It also fails when a dead-letter notice shows.
- `tests/web-e2e/staging/fixtures.ts`: `published(page)` now calls the new method.
- `tests/web-e2e/staging/sharing.spec.ts`: also requires the owner's row to still be listed after the publish wait.

**Disposition: I rejected the one CodeRabbit finding as a false positive.** It asked to make the recipient's poll in `tests/web-e2e/tests/contact-grant.spec.ts` wait up to 300 s and to raise the test timeout to 300 s. This is why I rejected it:
- The local stack has no delay before a published record is visible. `ownerFiles.published()` waits until the owner's write has no pending mark and no dead-letter notice. At that point the new scope root record is on the local routing service.
- Each `recipient.refresh()` call waits for a full forced pass with no cache, and that pass re-resolves each bookmarked share. The first poll call therefore already reads the published root.
- CI shows this. On the PR head the whole test passed in 16.5 s (run 35415081459). With the same spec on v2.2.0, it failed at the dead-letter check (run 35415079996). The test fails on the real defect and passes on the fix.
- A 300 s poll would make a regression a 5-minute wait instead of a fast failure, and it would run past the 120 s local project timeout. The 300 s window stays in `tests/web-e2e/staging/sharing.spec.ts`, because the deployed routing front can cache a record.

The publish wait still fails on a dead-letter notice and still passes on a published write.

<result>Draft PR #1904 is open and all CI checks are green. On main, the test failed: a grafted pass could not publish at all. Three gaps in the grafted pass caused this, and I fixed them. With the gaps closed, the own-vault mechanism holds the child, so there is no new durable record, store prefix, seal kind or seam change.

- **PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1904 (draft)
- **Branch:** `feat/1887-grafted-base-holds-local-child`
- **Head:** `48378499222ce7483adf8b6cc64e7d83d16ffe0e`
- **Issues:** `Closes #1887.` is confirmed as the only closing reference. `Part of #1880.` is also in the body.
- **Attribution:** the commit and the PR body contain no `Co-Authored-By` and no "Generated with".

**Test on main.** I added the whole-shape test in the facade `capstone` module. It accepts a write grant through the mailbox and queues a folder create below the grafted root through `stage_op`, with the guard closed. Then it runs a tick. The op never drained, for these reasons:

1. **The pass could not open.** `load_scope_root` reads the snapshot cache, and no leg writes a grafted root there. The received-share leg runs after the drain in a tick. Every grafted pass stopped with `Halt::Unclassified` before the replay.
2. **The self-adopt failed after the publish.** `root_adopter` always built the owner-blob adopter. The gate refused the record with `Unseal / HpkeOpenFailed` after the PUT, so the record was already live.
3. **Opening the pass exposed a security gap.** `open_pass` and the post-publish repaint use `project_folder` with no cross-plane rule. A sharer body could then link a node of this vault's own tree under the grafted root. The pass opens when the queue holds any op, own ops included, so this could happen with the guard still closed.

**The fixes** are in `crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs` and `crates/engine/src/facade.rs`:
- A grafted pass resolves its root through the grant gate (`resolve_scope_root`), the same path a second end uses.
- `DrainScope` has a new field `granted: Option&lt;GrantedPass&gt;`. It holds the sharer's encryption subkey, taken from the verified contact book (never from a record), and the `GraftedPlane` rule. `root_adopter` uses `RootAdopter::for_grantee` for a grafted pass. `is_grafted()` now reads only this field, which was the CodeRabbit CLI's one finding (major).
- `repaint_folder` applies `GraftedPlane::split` on a grafted pass.

I reverted each fix one at a time, and a test failed each time.

**Tests:** four new engine tests, plus one new case in the existing test that checks what a grafted pass needs.
- **Older sharer record:** the child stays.
- **Newer record that names the child:** it renders once.
- **Newer record without the child:** the child departs, and nothing goes into a capture.
- **Grant cut:** the write seed goes, and a later create stays in the queue.
- **Sharer body that names one of our own nodes:** the node keeps its one link.

**Where this differs from your brief:**
- **Equal sequence:** when the sharer publishes a different record at the same sequence as ours, the child departs and nothing goes into a capture. The render follows the record the network serves, as the own vault does. A hold would show a row the network no longer names. The test pins this, and the PR body says so.
- **Newer without the child:** the child departs, as item 3 of the "Done when" list in #1887 requires. A newer sharer record that was rebased onto ours names the child, and the child stays.

**Checks:** `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine` (all green), `cargo check -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` and `pnpm lint:tracker-refs` all pass. No TypeScript changed.

**CI:** every check passed on the head sha, including Workspace tests, Engine simulation, Fmt + clippy, Core KATs, Client Browser Suite, Web E2E Smoke, Tracker Refs, Lint and lint-pr-title. Some sub-checks were skipped (API, Desktop, and the web typecheck jobs), and their result checks passed.

**CodeRabbit CLI:** one major finding. Grafted classification had two sources (floor namespace and the new field). I fixed it before the push. It is not a real defect, because the facade always set both together.

**Review passes** (from `/tmp/w19/lane-G14/review.md`):
- **Simplify:** no new record or seam. The split lives only in `repaint_folder`, the one place the drain paints the base. The borrow is split from the async contact lookup so that clippy passes. `is_grafted()` has one source.
- **Security:** the subkey comes from the contact book by identity. The root resolve goes through the grantee gate, then the at-floor check. A rejection stops the pass with no publish and no charge. The repaint split closes the own-tree link gap. The cost is bounded: one resolve per write-granted scope per tick while ops are queued, and one contact-book decode per tick while a write grant exists. Grafted departures reach no capture.
- **Crypto-privacy:** no primitive, KDF, wire format, AAD or KAT change, and no secret is added or logged. The self-adopt uses the same `for_grantee` HPKE path as the received-share leg. Floors are raised where the read legs read them. It fails closed. The only privacy cost is more GETs of the shared root name while the grantee has queued ops.

**Issue edits and follow-ups:** none. The body of #1887 expected a durable hold, and the PR body explains why none is needed.

**Files shared with G13:** only `facade.rs` (the grafted pass wiring in the tick) and `drain.rs`. I did not touch `received_status.rs` or `tick.rs`.

**What #1886 now needs:** only the facade write guard. A queued create below a grafted root now drains on the tick it is queued. The own pass stops on that op for the tick, and the grafted pass publishes it.

**Not done:** the equal-sequence case is left as described above.</result>
