<result>## Durable client-side records in the v2 engine and hosts (git `main`, HEAD `0a64b9721`)

The most important finding: on main, `StagedContent.scope` has **no** `#[serde(default)]`. The default you described exists only on branch `fix/greptile-post-merge-findings` (commit `6245644d9e`), not on main.

The `scope` field arrived in #1900 (`eaeaebb07`), which did not bump `OP_RECORD_V` (still 3, `crates/core/src/seal/op_record.rs:47`). So after a deploy, a content op queued by an older build now fails `decode_body` and is classed `Retained(UnsupportedBody)`. It never drains and its staged blocks stay pinned. This comes from reading the code; I did not run a test.

### Engine seams (`crates/engine/src/seams/`)

| Record | Codec | Seam / host | Version field; decode failure | Harm if lost |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Op record** (`v ‖ ownerTag ‖ contentRootCid? ‖ HPKE-auth sealed JSON Op`) | seal/encode `sync/record.rs:36`; classify `record.rs:87-114`; body `serde_json` `sync/op.rs:660-669`; `StagedContent` `op.rs:55-76` | StagingStore queue. Web: IDB `ops` (auto-increment key), `packages/client/src/seams/stagingStore.ts:87`. Desktop: `ops/&lt;id&gt;.op` plus `next_op_id` counter, `crates/desktop-seams/src/staging_store.rs` | Yes, `OP_RECORD_V=3`, also bound into the AAD. Header unreadable, seal fails, or content-CID mismatch → `Undecodable`: dead-lettered and removed. Other version or other owner → `Retained`. Seal opens but body doesn't parse → `Retained(UnsupportedBody)`. Retained records are never replayed or removed, and their roots still count for GC. | **Harmful**: a pending user write. Retained records are stuck, not deleted. |
| Staged content blocks (keyed by CID) | raw sealed blocks, `sync/staging.rs:1066-1071` | StagingStore bytes. Web: OPFS `-staged` dir. Desktop: `staged/&lt;hex&gt;.bin` | Root `ROOT_FORMAT_V=1` (`content/dag.rs:43`). A root this build can't decode is kept as `Opaque` (`staging.rs:472-474`). | Harmful (file content) |
| Preserved dead-letter set | tag `PRESERVED_FORMAT_V3`, `staging.rs:113-117`; read `:550`, write `:620` | StagingStore | Unknown tag or bad shape → `None`, meaning "preserve as-is, never overwrite" | Harmful: it is the only carrier of the content key |
| Dead-letter notices | `NOTICE_FORMAT_V1`, `staging.rs:129-134`, `:647-686` | StagingStore (owner-scoped) | Same as above: `None`, left untouched | Mild: a UI notice is lost |
| Upload progress mark `cbx/um/` | `sync/upload_mark.rs:21`, `:60-66` | StagingStore | Unreadable → "unknown", read fail-closed (never taken as "never uploaded") | Mild: resume is less efficient |
| Drained / published op marks | 8-byte BE u64, `sync/drain.rs:122-128`, `:6411-6417` | StagingStore | No version. Wrong length → `None` (no mark) | Unclear (guess: mild) |
| Op attempts | `ATTEMPT_FORMAT_V2`, `drain.rs:612-640`, `:318-321` | StagingStore | Bad tag or shape → empty counts | Mild: retry budget resets |
| Retire ledger `cbx/rl/`, tombstones `cbx/rt/` | `net/retire.rs:161,176,262-390` | StagingStore (sealed) | Unreadable entry → `None`. On `owe` it is repaired, i.e. rewritten (`:306`) | Storage leak (server reclaim missed) |
| Doomed-name journal `cbx/dj/` | `FORMAT_V4` in seal, `sync/doomed.rs:48,261,328` | StagingStore | Unreadable → read as "no journal". Never swept; rotated past by `MAX_BOOKKEEPING_OPENS` | Reclaim debt stranded |
| Scope-exit debt `cbx/sx/` | `FORMAT_V1`, `sync/scope_exit_debt.rs:45,221` | StagingStore | Unreadable → `None` | Same as doomed journal |
| Contact book `cbx/cb/` | sealed CBOR, `CONTACT_BOOK_V=1`, `grants/contact_store.rs:51,417-424` | StagingStore | Fails closed: `Unreadable` error | Harmful (trust state) |
| Invite records `cbx/iv/` | `INVITE_RECORDS_V=3`, `grants/invite_store.rs:69,319-326,413` | StagingStore | Fails closed: `UnsupportedVersion` / `Unreadable` | Harmful (live links) |
| Received-share bookmarks `cbx/rs/` | `STORED_LIST_V=2`, `grants/accept.rs:330,436-451`; load `received_share_store.rs:86-95` | StagingStore | Fails closed: `Unreadable`, never read as empty (test `:163`) | **Harmful**: the delivering mailbox items were already acked |
| Epoch / sequence floors | u64 values | FloorStore. Web: IDB `cipherbox-…-floors`, stores `epoch`/`sequence`, hex keys, number values (`floorStore.ts`). Desktop: LE u64 files plus intent journal | No version. Key = `ownerTag(32) ‖ nameLabel(32)` for sequence, `ownerTag ‖ scopeId` for epoch (`floor_store.rs:136-160,219`) | **Harmful**: security floor |
| Settings / bin-index revision marks (`settings-revision[-mint]/`, `bin-index-revision[-mint]/`) | `settings.rs:653-659`, `bin_index.rs:213-218` | FloorStore, sequence namespace (so also name-labelled) | As for floors | Harmful: rollback / fork bar |
| Snapshot cache: record bytes (key = ipnsName), `settings-head/`, `bin-index-head/` | `net/resolve.rs:315,330`; `record_plane.rs:295,360`; `bin_index.rs:239` | SnapshotCache. Web: IDB `-snapshot-cache` (the only store swept for accounts the profile no longer holds, `accountStores.ts:41`). Desktop: one file per key | Engine seals; no host version | **Harmless** cache |
| Refresh token | opaque | CredentialStore. Web: no-op. Desktop: keyring / file (`desktop-seams/src/credential_store.rs`) | Missing → re-login | Harmless-ish |
| Web sealed Core Kit store | AES-GCM JSON `{v,iv,ct}`, `ENVELOPE_VERSION=1`, `apps/web/src/auth/sealedStore.ts:35,55-75,189-206` | localStorage plus wrapping key in IDB `cipherbox-corekit` | Bad or old envelope, or won't open → `removeItem`, returns `null` | One re-login |

### Your two questions

**Snapshot-cache entry fails to decode.** The fetch always runs first; the cache is only a fallback ("the cache never short-circuits the fetch", `record_plane.rs:293-295`).
- Record plane: a cached copy that won't open gives `Degraded(reason)` instead of `Stale` (`record_plane.rs:259-265`).
- Bin index: `cached_bin_index` returns `None` (`bin_index.rs:238-244`).
- Rotation: a cache miss or a failed gate → `RootGateVerdict::Rejected` (`net/rotation.rs:1654-1660`).
- Vault boot: the cache only sets the `rehydrated` flag (`sync/boot.rs:275`).

So it effectively refetches. A seam *error* on `resolve.rs:315` propagates with `?`.

**Floor-store entry fails to decode.**
- Desktop: a value that isn't 8 bytes → `SeamError("corrupt floor value")` (`desktop-seams/src/floor_store.rs:85`). A corrupt intent record fails the whole `open` (`:254-261`, test `:472`). The engine then returns `FloorUnreadable` ("never treated as no floor", `record_plane.rs:296-298`). This is fail-closed.
- **Web: fail-open.** `optional_u64` turns any non-number IDB value into `None`, meaning no floor (`crates/wasm/src/seams_bridge.rs:49-54`). That is a silent reset.
- By design, a key-format change such as ADR 0016's name label has no migration. Old floors read back as absent and re-seed from the network, so the device should be forgotten rather than upgraded (`floor_store.rs:160-164`).

Not covered: `crates/fuse/src/spill.rs` and `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/mount/projected.rs` write files, but I didn't check whether anything there survives a restart.</result>

<result>ADR 0020 is drafted and open as a PR: https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box-next/pull/78. Its status is Proposed, for the owner to flip to Accepted.

- **Branch and commit:** branch `adr/0020-durable-queue-reads-previous-release`, one commit `6382822`, one new file: `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box-next/decisions/0020-the-durable-op-queue-reads-the-previous-release.md`.
- **Format:** the structure and status line copy 0019 as it was first proposed.
- **Attribution:** I searched the commit message, the PR title and the rendered PR body for attribution text. None is present.
- **Lint:** cipher-box-next has no markdown lint of its own. The file passes markdownlint and prettier when run with the cipher-box configs.
- **Wayfinder map:** I added an ADR 0020 entry to issue 1, after the ADR 0013 line. There is no README or index in `decisions/`.

**The scope I chose.** It is wider than the op queue alone, and it follows your guidance. The test is simple: the rule applies to a durable client record when a deploy can leave it on a device and the new build cannot get it back from the network. If such a record is refused after a deploy, the member loses a write or is locked out of that state.
- **In scope:**
  - the op queue: record framing, the grammar of each op kind, and the seal envelope.
  - the staging-store records that finish a queued op: staged blocks, the preserved dead-letter set, notices, drained/published marks, attempt counts, upload progress marks, the retire ledger and its tombstones, the doomed-name journal, and the scope-exit debt.
  - the ADR 0006 sealed stores that fail closed: received-share bookmarks, the contact book, and invite records. The bookmarks have no network source, because the mailbox items that delivered them are already acknowledged.
  - the value format of a floor.
- **Out of scope:** a change to a floor key keeps its own ADR, as ADR 0016 did, because old floors re-seed from the network. The snapshot cache, the refresh token and the web sealed Core Kit store also stay out, because a miss there is safe.

The ADR also covers these points:
- A header version bump (`OP_RECORD_V`) counts as a change that needs a migration step or its own ADR.
- The rule covers one release only (D6). A device that skips a release has no guarantee. The retained-record rule keeps its bytes, but its writes do not publish.
- The test obligation is D5. The test must decode the bytes that the previous release wrote, and it must fail when the default is removed.

**The blueprint sentences.** They go at the end of the "Ops" bullet in `blueprint/engine.md` "Sync core", after "...rebases only onto gate-passing state (FSM1/cipher-box-next#33 D5–D7).":

&gt; A build decodes, opens and drains every queue record that the previous release wrote (FSM1/cipher-box-next ADR 0020). A new field on a queued op takes a decode default equal to the value the older build used, and that default passes every check a written value passes. A change that cannot take such a default — a removed op kind, a changed meaning, a header version bump that stops reading the previous version — needs a migration step that runs before the first drain, or its own ADR. The same rule holds for the staging-store records that finish a queued op, for the owner-local sealed stores that fail closed, and for the value format of a floor; caches and credentials stay out. Every change to such a record carries a test that decodes the previous release's bytes.

**Unclear or needs attention:**
- **The scope is wider than the brief.** Your brief asked for the queue only plus the smallest honest scope. The orchestrator's guidance pushed it wider. If the owner wants it narrow, the fix is to cut the "In scope" list and the second-to-last blueprint sentence.
- **The wayfinder map was already behind.** It listed ADRs only up to 0013. ADRs 0014 to 0019 were never added, so 0020 now appears right after 0013. I did not add the missing ones.
- **v2.3.0 already shipped the bug.** It has the required `scope` field and no default, and I confirmed this against the v2.2.0 and v2.3.0 tags. Devices on v2.3.0 hold their v2.2.0 content ops as retained until #1905 ships. The ADR records this as residual E3.
- **#1905 is still open.** The ADR names it as the first implementation, and the gate requires it to be merged.
- **The ADR has no header line saying it bounds the #6 rule.** 0019 has no such line to copy, so I cite #6 under "Relates to" and explain the bound in the Context section.</result>

<result>The Greptile P1 finding is real, and it is now fixed and pushed on PR #1905.

**Mechanism.** `note_unindexed_scope_roots` (`crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs`) checks each parent only against the index of that parent. Thus, in one walk, the stale body of parent A can name root X without material, and the index of parent B can prove X. X then goes into both `walked.proved` and `walked.unproved`. The old `install_unproved_scopes` removed the proved roots first and then added the named roots, so X went back into the unproved set. The focus leg then skipped the subtree of X as unreachable for the rest of the session.

**Fix.** `install_unproved_scopes` in `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box-w20-G16/crates/engine/src/facade.rs` now adds the named roots first and then removes the proved roots. The proof wins. No other code writes to this set. I did not copy the suggestion without a check: I confirmed the mechanism on this branch before I changed the code.

**Commit pushed.** `6562f879e` ("fix(engine): let a proved scope root win over a stale name in the same walk"). This was one push. The commit is unsigned and has no attribution lines.

**Test added.** `facade::tests::a_root_one_walk_both_proves_and_names_stays_released` puts one root in the proved set and the named set. It failed before the fix and passes after the fix.

**Gates.**

<summary>Agent "Lane G17 web floor store fail-open" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>The fix is done and draft PR #1907 is open with all CI checks green. The web floor store now fails closed: a floor value that it cannot read is an error, not "no floor" and not 0. The PR is still a draft. I did not request a review, mark it ready, or merge it.

**PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1907
- Branch: `fix/web-floor-store-fails-closed`
- Head: `a1bbb0b73e80ebf3fc070a959f3ce77632a30b8e`
- It closes #1906 only. GitHub shows #1906 as the closing issue.
- The PR body has no tool attribution. The commits have no `Co-Authored-By` trailer (the check prints 0).

**The defect (confirmed):**
- In `crates/wasm/src/seams_bridge.rs`, `optional_u64` read any value that was not a number as `None`. The engine reads `None` as "no floor".
- The `f64 as u64` cast changed NaN and negative values to 0, dropped fractions, and lost precision above 2^53.
- `required_u64` gave 0 for a raise result that was not a number.
- In `packages/client/src/seams/floorStore.ts`, `IdbFloorStore.raise` did `Math.max(stored, value)` with no check on the stored value. A corrupt stored value gave NaN, and the store then wrote NaN back.
- A floor is the rollback bar. Because of this defect, the adoption gate on web could accept a replayed older record.

**Values above 2^53:** they can occur. The engine itself never makes an epoch or a sequence near 2^53. But the signer of a record chooses those values, so a sharer can publish a larger one. The engine tests already raise a floor to `u64::MAX`. The bridge now refuses such values in both directions and never rounds them. The result on web is that such a record does not adopt, and only the signer of that scope can cause that. Desktop still accepts these values, so web and desktop now behave differently here.

**What changed:**
- **`crates/wasm/src/seams_bridge.rs`:**
  - `required_u64` and `optional_u64` now return `SeamResult`. `null` or `undefined` gives `None`, and a non-negative integer up to 2^53 - 1 gives the value. Any other value gives a `SeamError`, so the engine reports `FloorUnreadable`.
  - A new `js_number` refuses a raise above 2^53 - 1 before it reaches JS.
  - The same rule now also applies to op ids and the staged byte total.
  - `capped_count` changes an unreadable count to `usize::MAX`, so the result stays a refusal.
- **`packages/client/src/seams/floorStore.ts`:** a new `checkedFloor` helper checks the value on a read, on the raise argument, and on the stored value before a raise.
- **Desktop:** no change.

**Tests (all failed before the fix):**
- wasm-bindgen tests in `seams_bridge.rs` cover a string, a boolean, an object, NaN, infinity, -1, 1.5 and 2^53, on every read and every raise. They also check that a raise of 2^53 or `u64::MAX` never reaches JS. Before the fix, 3 of the 4 new tests failed. The fourth checks the correct cases (no floor, and exactly 2^53 - 1).
- New `packages/client/src/seams/floorStore.test.ts`: before the fix, 14 of 15 cases failed.

**Verification:** all of these passed locally:
- `cargo fmt --all --check`
- clippy on the workspace, and wasm32 clippy on core, engine and wasm
- `cargo check -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --all-targets`
- `cargo test -p cipherbox-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` (18 + 16 pass)
- `pnpm -r typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm lint:tracker-refs`
- the client vitest suite (738 pass) and the client browser suite (40 pass)

**CI:** all checks succeeded on the head commit, including Rust Core KATs, Workspace tests, Client Browser Suite, Web Engine WASM, Web E2E Smoke, Contract Suite, API, Desktop frontend and lint-pr-title. Desktop Build was skipped. GitHub reports the merge state as CLEAN.

**Issue work:**
- I filed #1906 (labels `v2-build` and `comp:web`, because no `comp:client` label exists; milestone `post-cutover`; sub-issue of #993).
- I made no other body or title edits and filed or folded no other issues.

**CodeRabbit CLI:** the first run hit the rate limit, and the retry completed with 1 major finding: a key that is present but holds `undefined` still reads as "no floor". I rejected it and gave the reason in the PR body. The store never writes `undefined`, and anything that can store `undefined` there can also delete the key, which has the same effect.

**Review passes** (from `/tmp/w19/lane-G17/review.md`):
- **Simplify:** one strict helper owns the rule on each side (Rust `required_u64`, TS `checkedFloor`). `optional_u64` and `js_number` reuse it, and the adapters lost their `Ok(...)` wrappers. There is no new abstraction, and the rule is stated once, where it is defined.
- **Security:**
  - Every number that comes back from a JS seam now fails closed, and a raise above 2^53 - 1 is refused before JS.
  - `capped_count` stays a refusal.
  - The TS raise throws inside its transaction before any write, so a corrupt stored value is not overwritten.
  - The error messages carry no values and no keys.
- **Crypto-privacy:** no primitive, key or seal material changed. The change only makes the rollback floor fail closed, which matches `FloorUnreadable` and the desktop store.

Nothing in the brief is left undone.</result>

<result>The engine reorder did not cause the failure. A race in the test did, and it also fails on the engine from before the reorder. I fixed the test, kept the engine change, and Web E2E Smoke passes on the new head.

**Mechanism.** `ForcedPass::landed()` answers at `manual.settle(...)`, which is before the drain, the mailbox pull (`ShareInbox::pull`) and the received-share status leg (`ReceivedShareStatus::refresh`). The status leg is the only code that writes `received_verdicts`. So `refresh()` in the test returns before the verdict of that pass exists. The test then read `/shared` once and asserted with `toHaveAttribute` for 5 s. Nothing re-read the list, so the page kept the verdict-less read and showed `none`.

- **CI trace (attempt 2):** `refresh()` ran from 18370 to 18458 ms. The pull stored the share, and its mailbox DELETE ran at 18553 ms. The "read again" click ran from 18582 to 18618 ms. The status leg resolved the scope root at 18630 ms, 12 ms after the read. Attempt 1 has the same order: the read at 18002 ms, the mailbox DELETE finished at 18237 ms, and the status leg after that.
- **Why the reorder is not the cause:** `install_unproved_scopes` behaves differently only when a root is in both `proved` and `named` in one walk. For the recipient, both sets are empty: it has no descendant scope, and its one folder publishes at a derived name. The verdict path does not read `unproved_scope_roots` either.
- **Local reproduction, old test:** with the engine from 06ac33f32 (before the reorder), it failed 3 of 8 runs with the same `none`. With 6562f879e it failed 1 of 5.
- **Greptile P1:** the finding is real and the fix is safe. `focus_scope_roots` joins the proved and unproved sets, so a proved root stays a boundary. I kept the engine change and its test. I did not reply in the thread, because the finding was correct.

**Fix: commit `1eaf3f71e`**, "test(web-e2e): re-read the shared list until the forced pass records its verdict".
- I added `SharedPage.readStanding(scope, resolution)` in `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box-w20-G16/tests/web-e2e/page-objects/shared.page.ts`. It re-reads the list every 250 ms, for at most 10 s, until the standing matches. It does not start another sync pass.
- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box-w20-G16/tests/web-e2e/tests/contact-grant.spec.ts` now uses it.
- `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box-w20-G16/tests/web-e2e/tests/cross-client.spec.ts` had the same race, so I changed it the same way. Its "one nocache refresh" claim still holds.
- I did not change any engine code or engine test.

**Gates**
- `cargo fmt --all --check`: pass.
- `cargo clippy -p cipherbox-engine --all-targets -- -D warnings`: pass.
- Engine lib tests: 1856 passed.
- The web-e2e typecheck, eslint and prettier: pass.
- Local runs of the fixed specs on the 6562f879e engine: 6 of 6, 16 of 16, then 8 of 8 after the CodeRabbit fix.
- In the local smoke slice, the 5 `device-approval` specs fail with "Internal server error" from `packages/login/dist/identity.js`. They run against the API another worktree (`cipher-box-w20-G1`) left on port 3000, not this branch's API. They pass in CI.

The design gap stays: a forced refresh answers before the share legs. Any other test that reads a share verdict right after `refresh()` can hit the same race.</result>

## What the engine admits and refuses below a grafted root

| Command | Proved write pass | Read grant, cut grant or unproved pass |
|---|---|---|
| Create, upload, new version | admitted | `ScopeExitRefused` |
| Rename, move and relink inside the share | admitted | `ScopeExitRefused` |
| Delete | admitted, unlink only | `ScopeExitRefused` |
| Move or relink out of the share, into it, or between two shares | `ScopeExitRefused` | same |
| Rename, delete or move of the grafted root itself | `ScopeExitRefused` | same |
| Any write at or below a scope root nested in the share | `ScopeExitRefused` | same |
| Restore into the share, version delete, version restore | `ScopeExitRefused` | same |
| Purge | refused (the node is not in this vault's bin) | same |

A "proved write pass" means that the last tick built a grafted drain pass for the root, and that the permission is still `write` and the write seed is still in memory.

**Tests:**
- These tests go through the real facade and the real drain:
  - `a_write_grantee_authors_every_admitted_write_inside_the_granted_scope`
  - `a_write_grantees_delete_only_unlinks_the_node`
  - `a_write_grantee_is_refused_what_leaves_the_scope_or_reaches_the_owners_surfaces`
  - `a_read_grant_or_an_unproved_write_pass_admits_no_write`
  - `an_op_a_cut_grant_strands_parks_rather_than_stalling_the_queue`
- `a_grafted_relocation_stays_inside_one_share` is new.
- I re-stated the two pinned tests, and each refusal that stays has a case:
  - `a_journal_target_outside_this_vaults_tree_is_refused`
  - `a_version_command_on_a_file_outside_this_vaults_tree_is_refused`

**Decision for you:** I refuse `RestoreVersion` below a grafted root, although the owner decision does not list it. The command reads the version history first, and that read fails for a grafted file (#1909). I can admit it after #1909 is fixed.

## Owner capture evidence

`a_write_grantees_delete_reaches_the_owners_bin_by_owner_capture` in `crates/engine/tests/owner_actions.rs` uses two real engines:
1. The owner grants write access to a folder.
2. The recipient's engine accepts the share and deletes a node in it.
3. The recipient's bin stays empty, and its queue drains.
4. The owner's engine bins the node, with the granted scope and folder as origin.

The first run of this test failed. Owner capture did not see an unlink at the root of an interior scope, because the boundary walk merged that root and reported nothing. I fixed `install_descendant_scopes` to report those departures.

## Defects found and fixed during the work

- **Stranded op stalled the queue.** When the sharer cut a grant after an op was queued, the op held the head of the queue for ever and blocked every later write. The engine now parks such an op as a dead letter, and the queue drains.
- **Findings from the independent reviewer (four, all fixed):**
  - Owner captures below a read-only graft stayed held for ever. A folder refresh below a grafted root now records no captures.
  - A move with `replacing` could delete a nested scope root.
  - The check at command time was narrower than the drain's check. The facade now admits only roots that the last tick built a pass for.
  - The own-scope exclusion missed roots that this session minted and roots it could not prove.

For each of the stranded-op test, the nested-root refusal and the owner-capture test, I removed the fix and the test failed.

**Fix** (`crates/engine/src/facade.rs`, `resolve_versions`):
- The read now takes the scope from where the file sits: the nearest scope root this session knows, or the vault root.
- The read seed comes from `scope_read_seed` for that scope.
- A new helper, `scope_floors`, picks the floor namespace. `scope_read_seed` uses the same helper, so a grafted scope reads its floors in the granting contact's namespace.
- A node that is below no known scope root now returns `ContentUnavailable`. It does not fall back to the root seed (security rule 6).
- All read paths go through this one function: `read_content`, `read_version_content`, `file_versions`, `open_content_stream`, `RestoreVersion` and `DeleteVersion`.
- There is no new durable record, no seam change and no wire change.

  - `the_recipient_reads_a_file_below_a_grafted_root`: a real recipient session accepts the share through its mailbox, then reads both files, under a read grant and under a write grant.
  - Both tests fail on main and pass with the fix.
- `a_file_below_no_held_scope_root_is_unavailable` (in `facade.rs`): a mutation check. When I put a root fallback in place of the refusal, this test fails.
- `tests/web-e2e/tests/contact-grant.spec.ts` (smoke slice): the owner and the recipient each download the file added after the grant and compare the bytes. Web E2E Smoke passed.

- **Why I did not fix it here:** The fix needs a design decision on trust: which argument lets a read accept a record below the epoch floor. This PR does not change that behaviour.

# #1911: An epoch-lagged file does not read after a cut. Design study (main d1f05ee30)

## 1. Cause

**The read path has no arm for a lagging record.**
- `resolve_versions` (`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:10677-10696`) resolves the file through `resolve_child` with `ChildAdopter`.
- The adopter runs `floor::check` at `StrictlyNewer` (`net/child.rs:343-351`), then at `AtFloor` (`net/child.rs:205-213`).
- `floor::check` refuses any epoch below the scope read-epoch floor (`gate/floor.rs:291-298`).
- A cut raises that floor at once (`rotation/rotate.rs:363-368`). Every interior node that no write has re-sealed is below the floor "by construction" (`blueprint/engine.md:798`).
- `EngineError::from_gate` reports the refusal as `TrustViolation`. The error class is wrong.

**The sweep is not a durable job.**
- The facade runs `run_sweep` one time per cut, with `SWEEP_MAX_PASSES = 3` (`facade.rs:4624`, `facade.rs:5919-5927`).
- `run_sweep_job`, the idle-cadence job that the blueprint names (`engine.md:819-822`), has no production caller (`rotation/sweep.rs:875`).
- A sweep that fails, or a restart after a cut, leaves files lagging until a write reaches them.

**The measurement probably did not run the sweep.**
- The test `tick` polls only the task list that `boot` took (`tests/write_plane.rs:265-267`, `:314`).
- The sweep that `RotateNow` spawns stays in the scheduler (`testkit/fakes/scheduler.rs:205-207`).
- Thus "20 ticks" does not show that the sweep fails. It shows that reads fail during every sweep window.

## 2. What the floor protects, and where the blueprint applies it

- **On a scope root**, the floor refuses a pre-cut root. Such a root republishes the old override seed, and every revokee who cached that seed can read it. This is a revocation bypass (ADR 0003, "Rejected alternatives"; `engine.md:824-833`).
- **On an interior node**, the stage protects no seed. The record "carries no seed, grant blob or commitment for the stage to protect" (`engine.md:798-800`). The epoch tag attests nothing (ADR 0017 D1).
- **The docs disagree.** Stage 5 applies to "every resolved record" (`CONTEXT.md:74`; `engine.md:206`; #33 D7). But "Epoch lag" says the node is "readable via history links" (`CONTEXT.md:21`). The sweep section names exactly two sanctioned readers below the floor (`engine.md:793-806`). ADR 0012 D2 says: "A third path needs its own decision."
- **Answer to Q1:** No text says that a file must stay refused. No text lets a read admit one.

**Answer to Q2:** The design intends lagging files to be readable. Two parts are missing:
- a read-plane lagging arm, which needs a decision;
- a durable sweep, which is a code defect against `engine.md:819`.

Code applies the floor exactly where the literal gate text applies it.

## 3. Recommended fix: (c), a read-plane lagging arm

**What to do:**
1. When `resolve_child` gets `EpochBelowFloor` for a record with no grant section, anchor on the gated scope root. Take its epoch and its history links, as the drain does at `sync/drain.rs:2197-2230`.
2. Walk the seed back with `seed_at_epoch` (`rotation/reseal.rs:614`).
3. Open the record with `ChildAdopter::open_interior_under` (`net/child.rs:248`).
4. Move `seed_for_lagging` (`drain.rs:506`) out of the drain so that both paths share it. ADR 0012 says that a second copy of a trust check is the defect to avoid.
5. Map the errors:
   - An epoch that the ratchet cannot reach, or an epoch above the root: `ContentUnavailable`.
   - An AAD failure under the seed that the ratchet reached: `TrustViolation`.
6. Wire `run_sweep_job` as the idle job. The blueprint already requires this.

**Trust argument.** The arm holds the four conditions of ADR 0012 D2:
- Stage 3 refuses a grant section before any floor stage runs (`child.rs:112-121`). A scope root is never admitted.
- The replay bar stays (`AtOrAboveFloor`). A record below the per-name sequence floor fails.
- The AAD binds the epoch. A relabelled record opens under no seed.
- The seed comes only from the root that this device gated. The member already holds that key through key regression, so the member learns nothing new and a revokee gains nothing.
- The arm moves no epoch floor.

**Residual.** On a device with no sequence floor for the name, a server can serve an older pre-cut record of the file. The sweep already reads under this same bar, and it then publishes that record forward (`net/rotation.rs:2724-2731`). A read that publishes nothing therefore adds no exposure. The ADR must state whether the arm raises the sequence floor after an unseal, as the sweep does (`rotation.rs:2843-2849`).

**Scope of change:**
- Blast radius: the read path (`resolve_child`), and the focus leg if it opts in.
- No wire format change. No op queue change.
- An ADR is necessary: a third sanctioned reader. Update `engine.md:793-806` ("exactly two" becomes three), `CONTEXT.md` "Adoption gate", and the `Strictness::AtOrAboveFloor` doc.

## 4. Alternatives

- **(a) Parent-named epoch.** `ChildRef` has no epoch field (`core/src/seal/body.rs:78-92`). This needs a wire change and a parent rewrite for each child re-seal. Reject.
- **(b) Reads wait for the sweep.** Reads stay unbounded. A read-only member cannot carry the wave (`engine.md:809`). Offline owners and the retained-window roll-off make lagging files unreadable for good. Reject as the only fix. Wire the durable sweep anyway.
- **(d) Report `ContentUnavailable` and change nothing more.** This fixes the error class only. The file stays unreadable. Reject.

## 5. The share recipient (Q4)

- Today `resolve_versions` reads the owner's vault root scope only ("subscope reads are a later slice", `facade.rs:10666-10669`). A recipient does not reach this path yet.
- When shared reads land, the defect hits the recipient harder. A cut of another member raises the recipient's floor. A read-only recipient cannot sweep or drain, so it waits for the owner.
- The recommended arm covers the recipient. It needs only the current seed and the root's history links, and a current member holds both.

## 6. Tests

1. **Cut, then read.** The test must not drive the sweep. `read_content`, `file_versions` and `read_version_content` return the pre-cut bytes. No floor moves.
2. **Sweep harness.** Take the tasks that the scheduler spawned after the cut, and show that the sweep re-seals the file.
3. A record with a grant section below the floor is refused.
4. A record below the sequence replay bar is refused.
5. A record above the root epoch is `ContentUnavailable`.
6. An epoch that no held link reaches is `ContentUnavailable`, not `TrustViolation`.
7. A record under a relabelled epoch opens under no seed, so it is `TrustViolation`.
8. After a restart, the idle sweep job converges a lagging file.

<result>I drafted ADR 0021 and opened PR #79. It is Proposed, and I merged nothing. I started no timers.

- **PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box-next/pull/79, titled `docs: ADR 0021 a read opens an epoch-lagged interior record`. The PR body has no attribution text. The commit is unsigned and has no trailer.
- **ADR number and file:** 0021, at `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box-next/decisions/0021-a-read-opens-an-epoch-lagged-interior-record.md`. 0020 is still only on PR #78. The file passes markdownlint and prettier with the cipher-box configs.
- **Wayfinder map:** I added the ADR 0021 line to issue 1, directly after the ADR 0020 line and in the same style.
- **Comment on #1911:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/issues/1911#issuecomment-5740393664. I read it back, and it is correct. It says that the durable sweep wiring is a separate code fix and needs no ADR.

**Sequence-floor recommendation (D4):** the read arm raises the per-name sequence floor after an unseal that the AAD confirms. The raise is monotonic-max and waits until the accepted record is durable. The reasons:
- The normal child adopt path already raises this floor, and so does the sweep's interior read (`floor::advance_sequence_on_unseal` in `net/rotation.rs`).
- A read that does not raise it leaves a rollback window open.
- The raise cannot lock the member out, because every lagging reader uses the replay bar (`AtOrAboveFloor`), and that bar admits the record at the floor.
- The drain does not need the raise, because it publishes the node forward in the same pass.

**Citations:**
- All the code and blueprint citations in the study are correct on main `d1f05ee30`. One line range is slightly off: at `rotate.rs:363-368`, the floor raise is at about lines 367-371. The ADR cites files and function names only, the same as earlier ADRs.
- One wrong claim is in the existing documents, not in the study. ADR 0012 D2 and `blueprint/engine.md` say that each lagging reader "moves no floor". This is not true: the sweep's interior read raises the per-name sequence floor. The ADR states the condition as "moves no read-epoch floor", and Consequence 4 records this correction. The blueprint wording in Consequence 1 carries the fix.

**Unclear items for the owner:**
1. The study left the focus leg as optional. I decided that the arm goes in `resolve_child`, so the content read and the focus-window folder refresh both get it. The reason is that `resolve_child` is documented as the one function both paths share, so the two paths do not diverge.
2. The study did not give exact text for the "Adoption gate and floors" stage 5 sentence or the `CONTEXT.md` "Adoption gate" sentence. I wrote proposed text for both in Consequences 1 and 2, and the owner should check it.</result>

<result>The Greptile P1 was a real bug. It is fixed and pushed as `d60afbcd6`, all CI checks pass, and Greptile re-reviewed the push at 5/5.

**What was wrong.** A grant only added the new scope root to `minted_scope_roots`. Two lookups did not use that set:
- `scope_floors` found the owner's own scopes only through `descendant_scope_roots`, so for a fresh scope it returned `None`.
- The grant never put the new scope's read seed into `scope_read_seeds`. Only a later boundary walk put it there.

Also, the tick's eviction pass drops any seed whose scope has no floor namespace. So even a seed that was put there early would be lost if the walk could not complete. I proved this with a test: grant a folder, then read with no tick. The read failed with `ContentUnavailable: no read seed held for the node's scope`.

**Main before this PR:** I ran the same test against the merge-base `facade.rs`. The read also failed there, with `TrustViolation … [unseal]: [seal-open-failed]`, because every read used the vault root seed.

**The fix** (seed stays in memory only; no durable record, seam change or wire change):
- `crates/engine/src/grants/create.rs`: `CreateGrantOutcome` now carries a new `GrantedReadScope { seed, epoch }`, filled from the minted override seed. Its Debug output does not show the seed.
- `crates/engine/src/grants/invite_mint.rs`: `mint_invite_link` now returns `(PendingInviteLink, GrantedReadScope)`.
- `crates/engine/src/facade.rs`:
  - The contact grant, the invite link and the resumed write share all give the scope's read material to `share_scope`. The resumed share takes it from the scope root that `resumable_write_share` resolves through the gate.
  - `share_scope` puts the seed into `scope_read_seeds` at once, stamped with the seed's own epoch.
  - A new helper, `own_descendant_scopes` (proved plus minted roots), is now used by `scope_floors` and by the tick's eviction pass.
  - A node below no known scope root is still `ContentUnavailable`. There is no fallback to the root seed.

**Tests** (`crates/engine/tests/mount_convergence.rs`):
- `the_owner_reads_a_file_inside_a_folder_it_granted` now reads straight after the grant, and again after four ticks.
- New: `the_owner_reads_a_shared_folder_no_walk_has_proved`. It covers a contact grant and a write invite link. The owner reads at once. Then four ticks run while all record endpoints fail, so the walk cannot complete. Then the endpoints heal and the owner reads again.

**Mutation checks:** I removed each of these in turn: the deposit, the minted roots in `scope_floors`, the minted roots in the eviction pass, and the invite-link deposit. Each one made a test fail with `ContentUnavailable`. The resumed write share path has no test of its own.

**Gates:** all pass: `cargo fmt --all --check`, workspace clippy with `-D warnings`, engine clippy on wasm32, `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine`, and `pnpm lint:tracker-refs`.

**CodeRabbit CLI:** one minor finding: "also read while the endpoints are still failing." I rejected it for this PR. When I tried it, the contact-grant case passes. The write invite link case fails with `TrustViolation [seal-open-failed]`, even with no tick. The seed is present, so this is a different failure from the one this PR fixes. The likely cause is the cached record that the offline read falls back to after the write cut, but I did not confirm it. **This needs your decision on a follow-up.**

<summary>Agent "Diagnose invite-link offline read" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>The failure is real, but PR #1912's deposit does not cause it. The cause is a separate defect that is also on `main` (class c). I filed it as #1913.

## What triggers it

A **write** share triggers it, not the invite link. I ran all four cases, reading with all record endpoints failed and with no tick:

| Share | Offline read |
|---|---|
| Invite link, write | `TrustViolation [unseal]: [seal-open-failed]` |
| Invite link, read | reads |
| Contact grant, write | `TrustViolation [unseal]: [seal-open-failed]` |
| Contact grant, read | reads |

The contact grant in the original test passed only because `grant_to_recipient` grants read permission.

## Mechanism (from debug output)

1. The grant re-seals the folder interior under the new scope and publishes the file record at sequence 3. The snapshot cache still holds sequence 2, which is sealed under the vault root scope.
2. The deposit is correct. It stamps epoch 1 with the correct seed, and the online read opens under it. A write cut changes the write plane only, not the read plane.
3. The write-scope cut runs the name wave. `WriteWaveNet::interior_source` (`crates/engine/src/net/rotation.rs`) adopts the sequence 3 record and commits the sequence floor raise to 3 itself. It does not write the bytes to the snapshot cache. The source comment says "The wave keeps no snapshot".
4. Online, `resolve_gated` (`crates/engine/src/net/resolve.rs`) gets `Current`, because sequence 3 is at the floor. That arm does not write the cache either.
5. Offline, `resolve_child` (`crates/engine/src/net/child.rs`) gets `NoUpdate` and opens the cached sequence 2 record. On the PR branch, the scope check in `ChildAdopter::assemble_envelope` rejects it: the cached record names scope `00..`, the read expects `94e3..`, and the check maps that mismatch to `Unseal/SealOpenFailed`.
6. On a read share no wave runs, so the online read adopts sequence 3 and caches it. That is why the read share works offline.

This breaks `resolve_gated`'s own rule that floors move only on the pass that also leaves the bytes as last-known-good.

## Check on origin/main

I ran the same probe against a copy of `b4ffb2fa6`:
- **Write share:** the offline read fails with `TrustViolation [sequence]: [sequence-not-newer]`. The floor is 3 and the cache holds 2. The online read also fails there, which is #1909.
- **Read share:** the offline read works.

The mechanism is the same; only the stage that rejects differs.

## Classification

- **Class c:** a separate defect, on main too.
- **Not class a:** the deposit's epoch and seed are correct.
- **Not class b:** #1911 is an epoch-lagged record rejected at the `[epoch]` stage. This one is a stale cache entry below the sequence floor.

## Error class

`TrustViolation` is wrong here. The cached bytes are this device's own record, which passed the gate before. A cached record below the sequence floor is a stale cache, not an attack, so rule 6 does not apply. It should be `ContentUnavailable`.

## Filed

**Issue:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/issues/1913, "fix(engine): a write wave raises a file's sequence floor but leaves the cached record behind".
- Labels `v2-build` and `comp:engine`, milestone `post-cutover`.
- Sub-issue of #1702, confirmed through `parent_issue_url`.
- I read the body back and it matches what I wrote.

**Done when (from the issue):**
- The wave writes the adopted bytes to the snapshot cache before it raises the floor, in the same order as `resolve_gated`.
- `resolve_gated`'s `Current` arm refreshes an older cached copy.
- An offline test in `mount_convergence.rs` passes after a write share.
- A cached record below the floor that the network cannot replace returns `ContentUnavailable`.

## Worktree

`git status` in `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box-w20-G18` is clean at `d60afbcd6`. I committed and pushed nothing. I deleted the temp copy of main and its target dir.</result>

**PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1914. It is a draft. Branch `fix/1913-wave-caches-what-it-adopts`, head `ee608967a674526e0a115d89cdce0d30711e376f`, one commit on `origin/main` `b4ffb2fa6`. The body has a bare `Closes #1913.`, and GitHub records #1913 as the closing reference. The commit and the body have no tool attribution. I did not request a CodeRabbit web review, did not mark the PR ready, and did not merge.

**CI:** 31 check runs finished and 27 passed. The 4 skipped checks are path-gated (API, Desktop, and the two Web typecheck jobs). Web E2E Smoke passed on the first run.

**Test and its result on `main`:** `a_write_share_leaves_every_record_it_touches_cached_at_its_sequence_floor` in `crates/engine/tests/mount_convergence.rs`. It runs a write share by contact grant and by invite link. Then it checks that the cache holds the vault root, the shared folder and the file at their sequence floors. On `main` it fails for the file: the cache holds sequence 2 and the floor is 3. With the fix it passes. On `main` the online read of a file in a shared folder also fails (#1909). Thus I built this test to measure the cache and floor order directly, as the brief allows.

**Full offline read (needs #1912):** I applied the fix to the #1912 branch in a temporary worktree. There I ran `read_content`, `file_versions` and `read_version_content` with every record endpoint failed, for both share types. With the fix all three return the correct plaintext. Without the fix the read fails with `[unseal]: [seal-open-failed]`. I saved the test in `/tmp/w19/lane-G20/e2e-offline-test.rs`. Add it when #1912 is on `main`. #1912 also adds tests at the end of `mount_convergence.rs`, so expect a small conflict at the end of that file.

**The fix:**
- **Wave:** `WriteWaveNet` now has a `snapshot_cache` field. `interior_source` writes the adopted bytes to the cache before it commits the sequence raise. If the write fails, the floor does not move. `net/cut.rs` passes the cache in.
- **Owner sweep:** `open_interior_record` writes the bytes to the cache before `advance_sequence_on_unseal`.
- **Refresh of an older copy:** a new helper `refresh_last_known_good` in `net/resolve.rs` does the work. The owner-root `Current` arm of `resolve_gated` calls it only when `recover_own_scope_material` checked the record again at the floor. The `Current` arm of `resolve_child` calls it after `open_at_floor`. The helper replaces only a cached copy with a lower sequence, and keeps a copy at the same sequence. My first version replaced any copy that was different. That broke the existing `write_plane` test that plants a changed root in the cache, so I changed the rule.
- **No other changes:** no seam, wire or durable record kind changes. The cache format is the same (raw signed record bytes), so the previous release's cache decodes.

**Floor-raise paths checked:**

| Path | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Wave `interior_source` | Fixed. |
| Owner sweep interior read (read cut and grant move) | Fixed. |
| `resolve_gated` `Current` arm (roots) | Fixed: refreshes an older copy. |
| `resolve_child` `Current` arm (content read and focus leg) | Fixed: refreshes an older copy. |
| Drain self-adopt | Already correct: cache write, then commit. |
| Settings, bin index and defaults planes | Already write the cache before the raise. |
| Pointer plane (`publish_pointer_inline`) | Nothing caches a pointer name, so there is no gap. |
| Root rotation reads (`gated_scope_root` through `adopt_root`), and the late cache writes in `ScopeWalk::descend` and `resealable_root` | Not changed: they raise the floor with no cache write before it. No read opens a cached root below its floor as a trust verdict, and the root `Current` refresh repairs the cache on the next online tick. I did not file an issue. |

**Error mapping:** in `resolve_child` with no network update, a cached record with sequence below the floor now gives `ContentUnavailable`. A cached record above the floor stays `TrustViolation`, as the existing capstone test requires. Every AAD, binding and signature failure also stays `TrustViolation`. Nothing renders from a stale cache. After a read cut, the sweep now caches the lagging-epoch record. An offline read of it is still refused at the epoch stage, which is the separate defect #1911.

**Other tests added:** wave caches before the raise, and a refused cache write raises no floor. Sweep caches before the raise (extended an existing test), and a refused write raises no floor. Root `Current` refresh versus a non-owner record. Child: below-floor cache is unavailable offline, a transplanted cache stays a trust verdict, and `Current` replaces an older copy.

**Gates:** `cargo fmt --all --check`, workspace clippy with `-D warnings`, engine clippy on wasm32, all `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine` tests, and `pnpm lint:tracker-refs` all pass. The CodeRabbit CLI run (`--agent --base-commit b4ffb2fa6`) reported 0 findings on the 5 files.

**Review summaries** (full text in `/tmp/w19/lane-G20/review.md`):
- **Simplify:**
  1. There is one helper, at its home in `resolve.rs`.
  2. It refreshes only an older copy, and it returns early when the bytes are equal.
  3. The wave has one new field and one cache write.
  4. The sweep passes down the bytes it already holds.
  5. Each ordering rationale is stated once, at its home.
- **Security:**
  1. Floors stay monotonic, and they move only after the bytes are durable. Tests prove that a failed cache write leaves the floor where it was.
  2. The cache receives only records that passed the gate.
  3. The change of error class is narrow: only a cache below the floor.
  4. Nothing renders from a stale cache.
  5. There is no new DoS surface: one Ed25519 verify happens only when the cached bytes are different.
- **Crypto-privacy:**
  1. No primitive, KDF, wire or KAT changes, and no cache format change.
  2. The cache stays ciphertext-only at rest.
  3. The adoption gate does not change.
  4. A copy at the same sequence is never overwritten.
  5. A below-floor record that a host plants in the cache now gives `ContentUnavailable`. It never opens and it moves no floor. The issue accepts this change.

**Shared files with open PRs:** the new test is in `mount_convergence.rs`, which #1912 also changes. I did not touch `facade.rs` or any of the functions on the exclusion list.

**Body edits and follow-ups:** none. The body of #1913 was current. I filed no new issues and folded nothing into other issues.

- **Issue link:** the body says `Part of #1911.` and closes no issue (`closingIssuesReferences` is empty).

## Part 1: the idle sweep job

**How it works:**
- `Engine::start` now spawns the idle sweep job (`spawn_sweep_job` in `crates/engine/src/facade.rs`). On each `sweep_cadence` it runs one pass over each scope that is due.
- A scope is due when all three are true:
  - This vault owns it: the vault root at its held name, or a scope root that the vault's own boundary walk proved (`walked_read_epochs`).
  - Its known read epoch is above `GENESIS_EPOCH`. The vault root epoch comes from the durable floor. A nested scope's epoch comes from the walk.
  - No pass in this session has confirmed it converged at that epoch.
- There is no new durable record and no new seam. After a restart, each scope past its genesis epoch is due one time. The pass builds its work-list from the published records.
- A grafted scope is never a target.
- One `Sweeper` builds the owner rotation net for both callers: the task a cut spawns (3 passes) and the idle job (1 pass per round). `run_sweep_job` now takes the per-scope sweep as a closure, because each scope needs its own ascent seed.
- The lagging read arm is not in this change. ADR 0021 was accepted on main (#1915), so #1911 stays open for that work.

**Tests** (new section in `crates/engine/tests/mount_convergence.rs`):
- **(a)** `the_sweep_a_cut_enqueues_re_seals_the_folder_it_left_behind`: the test runs the tasks that `RotateNow` spawned, with no clock advance. The folder moves to the new epoch.
- **(b) Restart:** in `after_a_restart_the_idle_sweep_converges_what_a_failed_sweep_left`, the cut's own sweep fails because all endpoints fail, then the device restarts.
  - After the restart the folder still lags, and a cold start alone re-seals nothing.
  - Within two sweep cadences, the idle job moves the folder to the new epoch with no write to it.
- **(c)** `a_read_only_member_never_runs_the_wave`: a read grantee runs three sweep cadences after the owner's cut. No record changes on any endpoint. The owner's idle job then re-seals the folder.
- Tests (b) and (c) fail when the job is not spawned.
- Two existing tests now include the job among the tasks that `start` spawns, and check that it stops when the engine drops.

## Part 2: version restore below a grafted write root

**Verdict: admitted.** `publish_restore_version` in `crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs` only reorders the file's own version list (`rotate_right`) and republishes the record. It keeps every version, prunes nothing, and touches no bin index, retire ledger or doomed-name journal. The facade now admits it for a proved write pass. `DeleteVersion`, `Restore` and `Purge` stay refused.

**Tests:**
- The facade harness test (real facade and real drain) now includes a restore. I moved the restore case out of the refused set.
- New two-engine test `a_write_grantees_version_restore_reaches_the_owner`: the owner reads the restored content as the head, and the old head as the prior version.
- Both tests fail when the refusal comes back.

**Web:** in a share granted for writing, the version history shows restore again and still hides version delete. A new test in `versions.test.tsx` covers this.

- **Inline review passes** (in `/tmp/w19/lane-G21/review.md`):
  - **Simplify:** one net builder for both callers; the vault-root helper is shared; the sweep is passed as a closure; no new seam.
  - **Security:** only this vault's own scopes are targets, and every pass checks the scope root again. A floor-read error or a sweep error does not mark a scope converged. The job does no work on an idle vault. No `RefCell` borrow stays open across an await. The restore admission reaches no bin or version-history surface of the owner.
  - **Crypto-privacy:** no new primitive, KDF edge, wire field or durable record. Seeds stay `Zeroizing` and are held for one round only. No secret material goes to logs or test assertions.
- **CodeRabbit CLI:** 1 minor finding — the test snapshot read only the first endpoint. Fixed: it now reads every endpoint.

<result>No hard breaches of a documented standard. I found one terminology breach that is near-hard and seven judgement calls. I did no git writes and no file edits.

**Documented standards**

1. **Terminology (CONTEXT.md, "Version history"): near-hard.**
   - `crates/engine/src/facade.rs`, `Command::RestoreVersion`: "A reorder of the file's own history…".
   - `versions.test.tsx`: "A restore reorders the file's own history".
   - CONTEXT.md says that a restore "publishes a new record whose head is that version and never rewinds history". "Reorder" is not a CONTEXT term, and it is incorrect: the old head becomes a prior version. Use "a restore publishes a new head and drops no version".

2. **Comments (AGENTS.md "Comments", absence-justifying): judgement.** In the `spawn_sweep_job` doc, "A granted scope is never a target: the job walks only the scopes this vault owns." The second clause describes the code, so remove the first clause.

**Baseline smells (all are judgement calls)**

3. **Primitive Obsession / Data Clumps: three web components.** `DetailsDialog`, `FileDetails` and `VersionHistory` all carry the pair `writable` + `receivedShare`. Two booleans encode three states: read-only, owner write and grantee write. `receivedShare` has no meaning when `writable` is false. Give it one string-literal union, for example `'read' | 'owner' | 'grant-write'`. This agrees with the user preference for string literals.

4. **Fail-open default.** `DetailsDialog` declares `receivedShare?: boolean` with `= false`, but `FileDetails` and `VersionHistory` make it required. If a new caller omits it, the UI shows the delete button in a share. Make it required.

5. **Duplicated Code: `mount_convergence.rs`.** This line occurs two times:
   `let polls = u32::try_from(cadence.as_secs() / …poll_cadence.as_secs()).expect("a small ratio");`
   Extract it to a helper, for example `polls_per_sweep(&amp;engine)`.

6. **Duplicated Code: `sweep/tests.rs`.** Two tests contain the same closure, `async |scope| Some(run_sweep(&amp;scheduler, net, net, scope, cadence, 1, &amp;|| true).await)`. Put it in one helper.

7. **Primitive Obsession: `facade.rs` `Sweeper`.** `Option&lt;Result&lt;SweepOutcome, SweepError&gt;&gt;` uses `None` to mean "session gone". The `run_sweep_job` signature repeats this. A named outcome, for example `SessionGone`, is clearer. Also, the `converged: RefCell&lt;BTreeMap&lt;[u8; 16], u64&gt;&gt;` map has a comment that tells what it holds. A typed key and value would make that comment unnecessary.

8. **Middle Man: `Engine::vault_root_scope`.** It now only delegates to `held_vault_root_scope`. This is acceptable because the job needs the free function, but you can inline it at the callers.

9. **Mysterious Name.** `run_to_end` and `drive` in `mount_convergence.rs` are close to the existing `tick` and `poll_tasks_until_parked`. `drive(…, times)` only loops `tick`. Think about a name such as `tick_n`.

**Clean**

- The engine uses no direct clock or RNG. The only time source is `scheduler.sleep` (determinism law).
- The tests assert behavior: record epochs, heads and spawned-task counts. No test asserts source text.
- The diff adds no tracker refs. The obsolete "open edge" note on `SWEEP_MAX_PASSES` is gone.</result>

<result>**Spec review: PR #1917 (branch fix/idle-sweep-and-grafted-restore)**

**(a) Missing or partial**

1. The restart test does not cover the vault root. Brief: "(b) after a restart following a cut whose sweep failed, the idle job converges the lagging file". The test cuts a nested scope because of the defect in #1916. After a restart, the vault-root path takes its epoch from the durable floor (`owned_sweep_targets`, `spawn_sweep_job` in `crates/engine/src/facade.rs`). No test runs that path. Record this gap on #1916.
2. A write grantee never sweeps. Blueprint `engine.md` "sweep" says: "Runnable by any write-capable client". The job walks only the scopes that this vault owns. The lane brief does not ask for grantee sweeps, so this is a note and not a defect. The PR body must state the limit.

**(b) Not asked for (scope creep)**

1. PR body: "(owner decision of 2026-09-19 on #1886)". This text says that the decision authorizes the version restore. It does not. The decision does not name the version restore. Change the text to "the decision does not name it".
2. The new tests are in `crates/engine/tests/mount_convergence.rs`, which lane G20 (#1914) also changes. Brief: "stay out of those functions; report any shared file". The PR reports the shared file, so the brief is satisfied. But there is a risk of a merge conflict. The tests do not have to be in that file.
3. The extraction of `held_vault_root_scope`, the test rename and `routing_keys` are refactors that support the change. They are acceptable.

**(c) Implemented, but possibly wrong**

1. Brief: "A version restore that writes the old content as a NEW head version is a write". `publish_restore_version` (`crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs:5057`) does not add a new version. It moves the old version to the head (`versions[..=at].rotate_right(1)`). This moves the version out of its old position. The head also takes the old `modified_at`. So the restore changes the order of the owner's version history. It does not only add to that history.
2. Is "restore" in decision item 2 the bin restore or the version restore? The decision says: "may not restore, purge, prune a version, or delete a version … Each of these acts on the bin or on the version history of the owner". Item 1 says: "Only the owner can restore or purge the node". Both uses of "restore" mean the bin. The four verbs match four existing ops: `Op::Restore`, `Op::Purge`, `Op::Prune` and `Op::DeleteVersion` (`crates/engine/src/sync/op.rs`). The text therefore reads as the bin restore, and it does not name `RestoreVersion`. But the reason in the decision ("acts on … the version history of the owner") can include a reorder of the history, which item (c)1 shows occurs. Before this PR, the web UI also called the version restore an "owner-only action". **Recommendation:** get a one-line confirmation from the owner on #1886 before the PR is marked ready. The code does not need to change.
3. The web change agrees with the brief: the restore shows only when `writable` is true, and a delete never shows in a received share.

No commit contains `Closes` or `Co-Authored-By`. `Part of #1911.` is present.</result>

1. **`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:3050-3069`**: the resume branch in `flat_root_cut` repeats the last part of `rotate_scope` (vouch, then `raise_epoch_floor`, then `spawn(make_sweep())`, then `RotationOutcome`). **Simpler:** pull steps 2 and 3 of `rotate_scope` (`crates/engine/src/rotation/rotate.rs:369-381`) into a `finish_cut(floors, scheduler, scope_id, epoch, sweep)` helper and call it from both places. **Cost:** there are now two copies of the step order a crash depends on. 2. **`crates/engine/src/net/anchor.rs:48-52` and line 178**: `landed: Cell&lt;Option&lt;u64&gt;&gt;` is changed from inside a publisher so that its value survives retries. 3. **`crates/engine/src/net/anchor.rs:149-183`**: `AnchoredRoot&lt;P, A&gt;` is generic, but its only impl fixes `A = VaultAnchor`, and it wraps a single call site. 4. **`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:3130-3139`**: the `VaultAnchorOf` alias is used once, as the return type at line 7827. **Simpler:** write the type inline or return `impl` of a small trait. **Cost:** 10 lines kept only for one signature.

<result>I found no network round-trip added to a cold start that owes nothing. There are three smaller costs, plus one blocking await when a vouch is owed.

1. **`crates/engine/src/facade.rs:5401-5418` (`catch_up_vault_anchor`), cold start with nothing owed.** No network I/O is added. The only work is a local `read_epoch_floor` read, and the function returns early unless `floor &gt; vouched`. One small waste: the tuple `(floor &gt; vouched, root_name, self.vault_anchor(api))` evaluates `vault_anchor()` before it is matched. So every start that has a stored floor derives two keys (`pointer_read_key` and `vault_pointer_signer`, then an Ed25519 key from the seed) and clones the root name, even when `floor &lt;= vouched`. It costs microseconds, but it also puts key material in memory for no reason. Cheaper: check `floor &lt;= vouched` first and return, then build the anchor.

2. **`facade.rs:5311`, cold start when a vouch is owed.** The catch-up is awaited inline in `start()`. That adds a fan-out GET of the vault pointer plus a pointer publish (API call and inline fan-out) before startup continues. Cheaper: spawn it with `self.seams.scheduler`, since it is best effort anyway, or run it after the loops start.

3. **`crates/engine/src/net/anchor.rs:276`, `vouch_read_epoch` at cold start.** It GETs the vault pointer again, even though the cold start just resolved and opened that same record (`outcome.vault_pointer`). Cost: one redundant fan-out GET plus one `open_repoint` decrypt and verify. Cheaper: keep the adopted record's sequence and `RepointObject` in `VaultPointerAdoption` and pass them in. The pointer can move between the two reads, but the CAS on `standing.sequence` already catches that.

4. **`anchor.rs:300`, the `repoint_regression` check.** Each vouch re-reads the floor store. At cold start the caller has just read the same floor (`facade.rs:5410`). Cost: one extra local store read. It is minor, and it can stay if it is meant as a safety re-check.

5. **`facade.rs:7871-7877`, `rotate_now` on the vault root.** It builds `vault_anchor()`, which derives the keys, before `bounded_rotation`, so the keys are derived even if the rotation fails early. Cost: negligible. Cheaper: build it inside the closure.

One thing the diff does well: `publish_pointer_over` reuses the sequence `vouch_read_epoch` already fetched, which saves a second GET compared with `publish_pointer_inline`. The retry path at `facade.rs:3042` (when `landed` is already set) still does one GET per retry, which is expected.</result>

<result>**Verdict:** The Greptile P1 finding is valid. Suppose a pass caches sequence 7 and then fails to commit its floor, so the floor stays at 5. Later the network serves sequence 6. Sequence 6 is above the floor, so it passes the gate, and the raw write puts it over the cached 7. The sweep's `open_interior_record` and `WriteWaveNet::interior_source` both did this. The adopt path of the resolve driver on main has the same raw write, and the same failure happens there.

**Fix (`crates/engine/src/net/resolve.rs`, `rotation.rs`, `child.rs`):**
- All cache writes on a gate pass now use one rule, `replace_if_older`: the new bytes replace the cached copy only if that copy is older.
- The rotation sites call it through `cache_last_known_good`. Resolve passes call it through `refresh_last_known_good(.., mode, ..)`.
- A cache-first pass compares against the copy it already read. A nocache pass reads the cache to compare.
- The sweep and the write wave now pass the verified sequence to the cache write.
- The order does not change: the cache write comes first, and a failed cache write raises no floor.
- **Floor:** if the cache keeps 7 and the pass read 6, the floor goes to 6. This keeps it at or below the cached copy. The raise takes the maximum, so the floor never goes down.
- I also fixed the driver's adopt path. It is one line and has the same failure.

**Tests:** four new tests. Each gives the cache a newer copy, gives the floor a lower value, and has the network serve an older record:
- `an_interior_read_keeps_a_newer_cached_record` (sweep; also checks that the floor is 6)
- `the_wave_keeps_a_newer_cached_interior_record` (wave; also checks that the floor is 6)
- `an_adopt_keeps_a_newer_cached_copy` (driver, both modes)
- `an_own_current_root_keeps_a_newer_cached_copy` (both modes)

**Mutation check:** I put the raw write back at each site: sweep, wave, driver adopt, and the nocache read. Each time, the test for that site failed.

**Gates:** all pass: fmt check, workspace clippy, engine clippy on wasm32, `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine` (23 result lines, all ok), and `lint:tracker-refs`.

**CodeRabbit CLI:**
- **Run 1: one major finding, fixed.** On a `NoCache` pass the own-current path, and the Current path in `child.rs`, passed `last_known_good = None`. This skipped the sequence check.
  - My first fix read the cache again on every pass. That doubled the cache-read count that `write_plane::the_on_access_file_queue_stops_admitting_past_its_ceiling` uses to count resolves (128 against a ceiling of 64).
  - The final version reads the cache again only in `NoCache` mode.
- **Run 2: rate limited.** I waited inline.
- **Run 3: two major findings, both rejected:**
  - *"Make the cache check-and-write atomic (a CAS on SnapshotCache)."* The gap between the read and the write already exists in the cache-first driver. A CAS would change the seam trait on every host. The worst result of a lost race is the old raw-write behavior, which is an availability problem, not a trust problem.
  - *"Do not report the fetched record as Adopted when the cache is newer."* Sequence 6 passed the gate, and it is what the network serves on this pass. The cached 7 stays for the offline read, and the floor at 6 stays below it. To gate the cached copy again would change how the resolve works, which is not in the scope of this PR.

**Push:** one push of `01c254c1a5007de2268dba1b4ce4462bf7d2a40c` at 12:00:37 UTC. The commit is `fix(engine): keep a newer cached record over an older gate pass`, unsigned, with no attribution lines.

**Greptile thread:** I replied with GraphQL, read the reply back, and resolved the thread (`PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM6j_MQA`).

**CI:** 36 of 36 checks finished (27 success, 9 skipped). No reruns were needed. The PR is still ready and `mergeStateStatus` is CLEAN. I did not request a CodeRabbit web review. No timers are running.</result>

**Hard violation (probable)**

`crates/engine/src/net/child.rs`, `resolve_child`, the `NoUpdate` arm:
```rust
// ... stale, not a verdict on anyone (rule 6).
GateError::Rejected(rejection) if matches!(rejection.reason,
    RejectionReason::SequenceNotNewer { floor, sequence } if sequence &lt; floor) =&gt;
    ChildResolveError::Unavailable(...)
```
- This code changes a gate rejection into "unavailable". `AGENTS.md` rule 6 and `CONTEXT.md` "Adoption gate" say: "A failure is a fail-closed trust violation, never mere staleness." `blueprint/engine.md` §"A gate failure is never mere staleness" says the same.
- The comment cites rule 6 as support, but rule 6 says the opposite.
- A cached copy of your own may be a valid exception. But the blueprint does not state that exception. Your memory rule says a blueprint change needs the full ADR process. Get an ADR, or remove the downgrade.

**Documented-standard judgement calls (AGENTS.md "Comments", "state rationale once, at its home")**

- The "cache before the floor moves" rationale is in four locations:
  - the `cache_last_known_good` doc comment in `resolve.rs`
  - the `interior_source` doc comment in `rotation.rs` ("as on the resolve driver's pass…")
  - the sweep comment in `rotation.rs` ("The bytes become last-known-good first, as on the resolve driver's pass")
  - the existing "Durable-first" comment in `resolve.rs`

  Keep it at `cache_last_known_good` only, and use a short cross-reference at the other locations.
- The 6-line `cache_last_known_good` doc comment is permitted domain rationale, but it is near the length limit.

- **Duplicated Code** in the `child.rs` test `a_transplanted_cached_record_stays_a_trust_verdict_offline`. Its `block_on(resolve_child(... seeded_adopter(..., NODE, LAGGING_EPOCH) ...))` is the same as `resolve_under(&amp;serving(None), &amp;cache, &amp;floors, &amp;other_node)`. Use the helper.

  - The empty `ReadBody::Folder { .. }` literal occurs two times.
  - The `floor_of` closure occurs in one test, but the tests next to it write `block_on(floor::sequence_floor(...))` inline three times. Extract one helper.

- **Middle Man**: `refresh_last_known_good` → `cache_last_known_good` → `replace_if_older` is three functions for one "put if newer" operation. The two outer functions only choose where `cached` comes from.
- **Mysterious Name**:
  - `refresh_last_known_good` and `cache_last_known_good` do not show how they are different (one uses the resolve mode, the other reads the cache).

<summary>Agent "Spec review of PR 1914" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>PR #1914 does most of what #1913 asks. I found one wrong behaviour caused by the one-line fix to the resolve driver's adopt path. Two review replies say more than the code does.

**(a) Missing or partial**

1. "`resolve_gated` writes the bytes of a `Current` record to the snapshot cache when the cache holds older bytes." This is partial. The root `Current` arm refreshes the cache only when `recover_own_scope_material` returns material, so a foreign root is not refreshed. The reason is valid: a foreign `Current` record did not pass the gate again. The PR body states the limit, but the issue does not.
2. The lane brief says "Check every other path that raises a sequence floor without leaving bytes … fix any with the same gap." Two root rotation reads still raise the floor first. Then they do a raw `let _ = snapshot_cache.put(...)` that ignores its error (`rotation.rs` near line 1192, and `resealable_root` near line 1591). The PR table says these paths do no put at all. That is wrong: they do a put, but in the wrong order. They were left open, not fixed.

**(b) Not asked for**

- The adopt arm of `resolve_gated` changed from `put` to `refresh_last_known_good`. The issue did not ask for this. It is in scope only because it gives the same rule to all cache writes. But it has a side effect: `drain.rs` `resolved_bytes` (`Adopted` arm) reads the cache back to get "its own gate-passing bytes". When the cache keeps 7 after adopting 6, the drain now builds its next record on 7. This pass did not gate 7 again, and the network serves 6. I found no test for this. Either the drain must use the adopted bytes, or this change must be reverted.

**(c) Implemented, but looks wrong**

1. Floor 6 with cache 7. The spec says: "floors move only on the pass that also leaves the bytes as last-known-good." This holds in intent: the floor does not go above the cached copy, and a failed put raises no floor. But the pass does not leave its own bytes. The result is that an online read returns 6 and an offline read returns 7. I think this is acceptable. The (b) item is where it causes harm.
2. The reply to the first Greptile finding says: "every cache write on a gate pass now goes through one rule." That is false because of the raw puts in (a)2.
3. The race between the cache read and the cache write is real. That thread has no reply and is not resolved. In `CacheFirst` mode, `last_known_good` is read before the network and gate awaits, so the check can use an old value. The full fix is a conditional put, which is a seam change that the brief forbids. A rejection is valid only if the reply says two things: a regression can put the cache below the floor again, and the result is now `ContentUnavailable`, not `TrustViolation`. The next online `Current` read repairs it. A smaller fix that needs no seam change: always read the cache again just before the put.

**The rest meets the spec**

- The wave writes the cache before the floor moves, and a failed write leaves the floor unchanged.
- The child `Current` arm refreshes the cache.
- `a_file_under_a_write_share_reads_with_every_record_endpoint_down` checks `read_content`, `file_versions` and `read_version_content` offline.
- A cached record below the floor that the network cannot replace (`sequence &lt; floor`) maps to `ContentUnavailable` (`facade.rs:10879`). A transplant stays `TrustViolation`.
- No seam, wire or durable record changes. The cache still stores raw record bytes, so the previous release's cache still decodes.</result>
