<result># Rule 6 misreadings on origin/main (d1b9b94e1)

I found 5 misread items on main and 6 unclear items. Open PR #1914 still adds the downgrade the owner rejected. Three issue texts state rule 6 in reverse.

## Misread items

| # | File:function | Code or comment | What the user sees now | Correct mapping |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `crates/engine/src/net/focus.rs` `rejection_verdict` | `RejectionReason::EpochBelowFloor { .. } =&gt; None`. The doc says "It is not abuse either, so nobody is accused." | A folder below the read-epoch floor is skipped with no event and no verdict. A manual refresh reports success, and the listing stays old. | Use the ADR 0021 arm in `resolve_child`. If the ratchet cannot reach the epoch, the result is `Unreachable`. Every other failure is `Rejected` and emits a trust violation. |
| 2 | `crates/engine/src/grants/received_status.rs` `ReceivedShareStatus::open` | After a failed `adopt(...)`: `_ =&gt; None` | A shared scope root that the gate rejects gives no warning. The row keeps its last render. | Emit a trust violation and fail the share leg. The equal-floor arm stays. |
| 3 | `received_status.rs` `resolved` and `classify` | `if sequence_floor.is_some_and(\|floor\| verified.sequence &lt; floor) { return None; }` gives `Unresolvable`. The test doc says "a replay, not a verdict". | The row shows "unresolvable", which the blueprint defines as "unknown/stale". This happens for a rollback or a replayed pre-cut commitment. | Report a trust violation. The classifier in `blueprint/engine.md` has no trust class, so the owner must decide where it goes. |
| 4 | `crates/engine/src/sync/drain.rs` `resolved_bytes`, `load_child_node`, `open_for_reauthor` | `ResolveOutcome::TrustViolation(_) =&gt; Err(Halt::Unclassified)`. A rejection that is not an epoch rejection gives `Halt::UploadAttempt`. | The drain writes nothing. But the op stays at the queue head on the outage budget (about 1 hour), then goes to dead letters. The drain emits no trust event. The user sees a stalled write. | Emit a trust violation and use a halt that names the verdict, as `report_author_refusal` does. |
| 5 | `crates/engine/src/facade.rs` `focus_scope_roots` doc | "availability laundered into a trust verdict (security rule 6)" | Comment only. The code is correct. | Rule 6 forbids the opposite direction. Remove the rule 6 citation. |

**PR #1914 (open, head 01c254c1a):** the head still adds the downgrade in `net/child.rs` `resolve_child`. It maps `SequenceNotNewer` with `sequence &lt; floor` on a cached record to `Unavailable`, with the comment "stale, not a verdict on anyone (rule 6)". The test `a_cached_record_below_the_sequence_floor_is_unavailable_offline` also asserts this. Remove both. The cache-before-floor ordering in the same PR is the real fix.

## Unclear items

- **`net/rotation.rs` `walk_verdict`:** `Superseded` gives `WalkFailure::Unavailable`, and `facade.rs` then does `Some(WalkFailure::Unavailable) =&gt; {}`. A descendant scope root below its read-epoch floor is ignored with no event. ADR 0003 D2 sanctions this, but `CONTEXT.md` lists only one exception. The `RootGateVerdict` doc says every arm other than the sweep folds `Superseded` back into a rejection, and this arm does not.
- **`net/adopter.rs` `recover_own_scope_material`:** `GateError::Rejected(_) =&gt; Ok(None)`. The resolve tick keeps `Current` and holds the record for renewal, with no event. The rotation folds the same `None` into `Rejected`.
- **`facade.rs` `resolve_error`:** a rejection at an unvouched name gives `UnsupportedTarget`. This is an input class, not availability, and it looks deliberate.
- **`record_plane.rs` `load_record`:** `RolledBack` and `RevisionRolledBack` give `RecordLoad::Stale`, which shows as `SettingsOrigin::Stale`. The settings plane emits no trust event. The bin plane emits one only through the drain.
- **`drain.rs` line 6218:** a rejection at the unseal stage counts as proof that a record stands at the name.
- **`grants/accept.rs`:** any `SequenceNotNewer` for a share that is already bookmarked is acknowledged as success. The check does not test `sequence == floor`.

## Issue texts that state rule 6 in reverse

- **#1909 (closed):** "a missing capability and not a trust verdict (security rule 6)".
- **#1911 (open):** "an epoch-lagged record is not abuse … so a read must not report a trust violation (security rule 6)". It cites focus.rs item 1 as precedent. Its acceptance criteria match ADR 0021 D5.
- **#1913 (open):** "a stale cache, not an attack. Security rule 6 does not apply to it." Its acceptance criterion "returns `ContentUnavailable`, not `TrustViolation`" conflicts with the decision. Rewrite it.
- **#1916:** does not restate rule 6.
- **Merged PRs:** #1912 cites rule 6 for a scope pick that runs before any fetch. The code is correct because nothing is gated. #1905, #1907 and #1910 do not have the pattern. `FloorUnreadable` in #1907 is a host I/O failure.

## Other effects of option A

**Benign causes that reach the user as a trust violation:**
- **#1913 on main:** an offline read after a write share gives a trust violation. It stays until the #1914 ordering fix merges.
- **#1911:** every read of an epoch-lagged file after a cut gives a trust violation until the ADR 0021 arm lands.
- **#1916:** after a read cut of the user's own vault root, the next cold start fails closed. `ColdStart` maps to a trust violation, so FUSE returns `EIO`.
- **`focus.rs`:** `SequenceNotNewer` gives `Rejected`. An old own cached folder record, for example after a race with the user's own wave, fails the whole refresh pass and emits a warning.

**How hosts treat the two classes:**
- **FUSE:** a trust violation gives `EIO`. An unavailable result gives `EAGAIN`, and a seam error also gives `EIO`.
- **WinFsp:** a trust violation gives `STATUS_DATA_ERROR`. An unavailable result gives `STATUS_RETRY`.
- **Desktop:** the tray shows "CipherBox refused an update that failed a trust check" for the rest of the session.
- **Web:** a toast says "verification refused an update: …". The user must close it, and at most 5 stay. `FileBrowser` blanks the listing and shows "trust violation: …" with no retry. `contentUnavailable` is also fatal on web. Only `tooManyStreams` and `refreshFailed` recover.

**Retry and escalation:**
- `rotation/retry.rs` `bounded` never retries a verdict.
- `RefreshVerdict::Rejected` outranks `Unreachable`, and the pass does not record a success.
- A bin load verdict uses the attempt budget, then goes to dead letters.
- The withheld-update escalation does not look at the error class.

## Correct items (count)

- Correct `TrustViolation` mappings: about 20. These include `from_gate`, the resolve and rotation verdict functions, `reread_at_floor`, `boot.rs`, and the CID mismatch.
- Real availability failures with no gate involved: about 15. These include seam errors, no seed held (#1912), `FloorUnreadable`, entropy, `Absent`, and the sweep's `Unreadable`.
- Sanctioned lagging readers (drain and sweep, ADR 0012 and ADR 0021): 3.
- Equal-sequence `Current` recovery of the device's own record: 4.</result>

<result>I resolved all open findings on PR #1914. I pushed one commit, `e43e534ed5a7712cbf86705c44aa0942ea38c52a`, at 12:48:32Z, before the 13:05Z limit. Head branch: `fix/1913-wave-caches-what-it-adopts`. CI is green on that commit: all checks passed and the others were skipped. The PR is still ready, not a draft. It closes #1913 and has no unresolved threads.

**Owner decision (option A).** I removed the change in `resolve_child` (`crates/engine/src/net/child.rs`) that reported a cached record below the sequence floor as unavailable. I also deleted the comment that cited rule 6 for it. The facade mapping needed no change: a gate error goes to `from_gate`, which gives `TrustViolation`. I did not change the ADR 0021 exception.

**Findings**
1. **Race: fixed.** The new file `crates/engine/src/net/last_known_good.rs` has one helper, `keep_newest_last_known_good`. The resolve driver, the child resolve, the sweep, the write wave, the owner root reads and the drain self-adopt all use it. Each path calls it before its floor moves. A failed write raises no floor.
   - **Lock:** a thread-local map from each name to the tasks that wait for it. The lock releases on drop, also when a future is cancelled. It covers only the cache read, the sequence compare and the cache write. The helper reads the cache again inside the lock. No network call, gate stage, floor commit or other lock runs inside it, so it cannot deadlock. The engine is not `Send`, so each engine runs on one thread; wasm has one thread. There is no seam change.
   - **Test:** `an_older_pass_that_finishes_last_keeps_the_newer_copy`.
2. **Drain bytes: fixed.** The drain now uses `resolve_gated` and builds on the bytes that its own pass adopted. It does not read the cache back. Test: `an_adopt_authors_on_the_bytes_it_gated_not_the_cache`.
3. **Raw puts: fixed, with more scope than the brief.** A new function `gate_root_pass` checks the child binding (envelope id and ascent link). The check now runs before the floor commit and before the cache write. `gated_root_cached` puts the bytes in the cache before the commit.
   - This covers `descend` and `resealable_root`. From the spec review, it also covers the other owner root reads that have a cache: the sweep child scope root, the promoted root, the wave `root_source`, the wave child-scope check and `gated_root_at`.
   - I removed `adopt_root`, `gated_scope_root`, `gated_child_root` and `gated_at_anchor`. I renamed the references to `gated_child_root` in the doc comments.
   - Test: `a_scope_root_read_caches_the_root_before_it_raises_that_names_floor`.
4. **Standards: fixed.**
   - The "cache before the floor moves" rationale is now stated once, on the helper. The other sites cross-reference it.
   - The three-function chain is now one helper. It takes only the record bytes and gets the sequence from them.
   - I merged the two `open_at_floor` branches in `resolve_child`. I renamed `serving(None)` to `offline()`.
   - New test helpers: `cached_under_floor` and `sequence_floor_of`. The transplant test reuses `resolve_under`, and the wave tests reuse `interior_body()`.
   - I fixed a doc comment that was on the wrong drain test and a stale comment about NoCache.
5. **Foreign root `Current`:** I kept it as it was and described it in the PR body.

**Behavior changes to know about**
- The helper reads the cache one more time for each name. Because of this, two write-plane tests needed changes:
  - `the_on_access_file_queue_stops_admitting_past_its_ceiling` now counts distinct names, not all cache reads. It can no longer see a pass that resolves the same file twice.
  - To keep `a_manual_refresh_resolves_the_owner_root_without_reading_the_cache` passing, the vault-root walk does not write the cache. Its caller holds bytes that the resolve driver already cached.
- The grantee root read (`GranteeRotationNet`) still raises a floor and writes no cache, because that net has no cache. I did not file an issue for it.

<result># Security review: branch `fix/1916-root-read-cut-repoints` (diff `origin/main...HEAD`)

**No findings at confidence 8 or above.**

The diff shown in the skill preamble was empty because that preamble ran in the main checkout. This review used the lane worktree `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box-w20-G22` and the diff `origin/main...HEAD`.

## Candidates considered and rejected

1. **An old vault pointer laundered through the catch-up or the cut.** Rejected. `open_repoint` checks the owner signature. The code refuses a standing re-point that names a root other than the session root. `minReadEpoch` is the only field that changes, and it only goes up. `writeEpoch`, `prevRoot` and `scopeId` carry over, so nothing rolls back.
2. **`minReadEpoch` raised from attacker data.** Rejected. The value comes from one of two places:
   - in the cut, the epoch this device just published;
   - in the catch-up, the local durable floor, which rises only after a gated adopt of an owner-signed root.
3. **An older pointer overwrites a newer one because the CAS bar is bypassed.** Rejected. A publish over a replayed lower sequence loses record selection on honest endpoints and reports `LostRace` or `Unconfirmed`. The attack works only if the attacker controls every endpoint, and then it is withholding, which is DoS and out of scope.
4. **The `landed` resume skips the gate.** Rejected. The value comes only from this device's own confirmed root publish and lives for one `rotate_now` call.
5. **The floor is raised past the vault pointer.** Rejected. The order is root publish, then vouch, then floor raise and sweep. A vouch that fails leaves the floor where it was.
6. **Entropy, nonces, key exposure and logging.** Rejected. The nonce comes through the injected `Entropy` seam. The pointer read key is `SecretBytes`. Nothing is logged.
7. **Trust vs availability.** Not a vulnerability. An `Absent` vault pointer maps to `Rejected`, which fails closed. An attacker who returns 404 everywhere can only stall a cut, which is DoS.</result>

<summary>Agent "Lane G22 root read cut repoint" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>PR #1919 is open as a draft and all CI checks pass. The fix works: after a read cut of the vault root, the device and a second device start again. One narrow lockout remains, and only an ADR can close it.

**PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1919 (draft). Branch `fix/1916-root-read-cut-repoints`, head `c7eb783be`, one unsigned commit with no attribution trailer. The body has a bare `Closes #1916.`, and GitHub shows #1916 as the closing reference.

**CI (one wait):** all checks are success. That includes Rust fmt + clippy, workspace tests, engine simulation tests, Core KATs (native and WASM), adapter tests (Windows and macOS), Client Browser Suite, Web Engine WASM, Web E2E Smoke, Contract Suite, Lint, Tracker Refs and the PR title check. The API, Desktop and Web typecheck jobs were skipped by change detection. No re-run was necessary.

**What was wrong:** the cut raised the durable read floor, but the vault pointer still vouched the old epoch. The cold-start check then refused the device (`vouched 1 below durable floor 2`). A second device also failed, on its second start, because its first start adopted the cut root and raised its own floor. The issue text says the second device starts. It does, but only one time.

**Order chosen and why:**
1. Publish the root at the new epoch.
2. Vouch the new epoch at the vault pointer. The owner signs the same re-point with only `minReadEpoch` raised, and the vouch refuses a re-point that names another root.
3. Raise the durable floor.
4. Enqueue the lazy wave.

- **Why the root goes first:** if the vouch goes first and the root publish fails, every device seeds a floor above the only root. The owner then cannot cut again, so every device is bricked.
- **Why the vouch goes before the floor:** if the floor goes first and the vouch fails, this device is locked out. That is the bug in #1916.
- **Retry:** a retry finishes the cut that already landed and does not cut again. A new cut would first adopt the landed root, and that raises the floor past a vault pointer that did not move.
- **Cold-start catch-up:** when a start adopts the root above the vouched epoch, it vouches that epoch before the loops start. If this fails, it sends `RenewalFailed` with the vault-pointer name.
- **Refusal:** a cut of the vault root with no vault-pointer signer is refused before anything is minted.
- **Unchanged:** `repoint_regression`, the seams, the wire format and the durable record kinds do not change.

**Path table:**

| Path | Result |
| --- | --- |
| RotateNow at the vault root | Fixed |
| Scope-exit cut | Cannot name the vault root; it only holds descendant roots |
| Revoke at the root, read or full, with or without the write arm | Refused before any rotation; the vault root has no grants |
| Downgrade at the root | Refused before any rotation |
| Grant at the root | Refused (`UnsupportedTarget`) |
| Grant of a child folder | Parent root republishes at the same epoch |
| Write wave at the root | Carries the durable floor as `minReadEpoch`; the facade cannot reach it |
| Sweep and drain wave | Re-seal interior nodes only |

**Tests:** I added 9 tests in a new section of `crates/engine/tests/mount_convergence.rs`:
- The same device restarts after the cut. This test failed before the fix.
- A second device starts two times.
- The vouch never lands: the device still starts, the next start vouches the epoch, and the cut happens one time only.
- The root never lands: the vault pointer does not change.
- A failed catch-up sends its event.
- Each of the two vouch refusals fires.
- Grant, revoke and downgrade at the root, followed by a restart.

All 16 tests in the file pass, and the full engine suite passes. Of 8 mutations, 8 were killed.

**UI reachability:** only `packages/client` exposes `rotateNow`. Nothing in `apps/web` or `apps/desktop` calls it, so no user can do this cut from the product today. The severity is low.

**Reviews (all run in the foreground):**
- **simplify:** applied a shared `complete_cut`, a `From&lt;PointerPublishFailure&gt;`, the new root refusal, the failure event and shorter comments. I rejected the shared vouch body because the two bars are different.
- **code-review:** added tests for the two refusals. I renamed the types to `VaultPointerVoucher` and `VouchedRoot`, because the blueprint uses "vault anchor" for the root scope.
- **security-review:** no finding at confidence 8 or above.
- **crypto-privacy reviewer and the independent reviewer:**
  - They found that the catch-up comment wrongly said the next start runs it again. I fixed the comment.
  - I changed the missing-signer refusal to Unavailable.
  - I kept the stale-record re-sign finding at Low. Records that name one root differ only in `minReadEpoch`, and the vouch only raises it. To fork the record, an attacker must control every endpoint.

All summaries are in `/tmp/w19/lane-G22/review.md`. I did not run the CodeRabbit CLI at any time.

**Decision for you — the lockout that remains:**
- **When it happens:** the vault-pointer PUT fails while record reads work, for longer than the retry bound. The same happens when a root publish comes back unconfirmed but did land. In both cases the running session adopts the cut root and raises its floor. The next start of that device is then refused, until another owner device starts and vouches the epoch. A user with one device stays locked.
- **Why I did not close it:** the crypto-privacy reviewer confirmed that closing it needs a change to the floor law or to the cold-seed check. That is a trust-boundary change and needs an ADR, so I stopped there.
- **Partial option without an ADR:** a catch-up in the running tick would make the window smaller. It would also keep the owner signing key in the tick, so I did not add it.

I filed no follow-up issue and made no issue edits. The PR stays draft; I did not mark it ready or merge it. No timer or background command is running.</result>

<result># Security review: feat/1911-lagging-read-arm (`git diff origin/main...HEAD`)

No vulnerabilities at or above the confidence threshold. The first pass reported no findings, so no false-positive filter pass was needed.

What the pass checked:
- **Anchor authenticity:** the check verifies the IPNS signature and the head CID. It also checks the id and scope binding and the `AtFloor` floor check. A lagging record is always below the read-epoch floor, so every walk opens at least one history link, and that link is AEAD-bound to the held seed and the anchor epoch. A forged or stale anchor therefore gives `ContentUnavailable`. It never gives a wrong seed or a false accusation.
- **Revocation:** a pre-cut scope root is refused (its epoch is below the floor). A grant section is refused before any floor stage runs. A revokee cannot walk the ratchet without the current seed. The write-plane forgery window is the documented residual that the sweep already accepts.
- **Floor law:** the read-epoch floor does not move. The sequence floor rises only after the AAD-confirmed unseal and after the bytes are cached. The replay bar is `AtOrAboveFloor`.
- **Error classes:** the code matches the ADR 0021 D5 table. The focus leg now reports every remaining gate rejection as a trust violation.</result>

<result># Security review: lane G24 (`fix/rule-6-gate-verdicts`)

**Scope:** `git diff origin/main...HEAD` in `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box-w20-G24`.

## Findings

No finding meets the confidence of 8 or more that the report needs. No high or medium finding.

## Items examined

- **Event text:** `emit_trust_violation` passes the routing key through `RedactedText`. The detail text holds only the gate stage and check name, or `DefaultsReason::check()`. No seed, key, body or grant bytes reach an event.
- **Received-share leg (`grants/received_status.rs`):** A refused record now fails the row and emits an event. A seed is put in the cache only after the body opens. The equal-floor arm still requires `sequence == floor` and `epoch &gt;= epoch_floor`. A seam error still gives no verdict and emits no event.
- **Equal-floor own-root recovery (`net/adopter.rs`, `net/resolve.rs`, `net/rotation.rs`):**
  - A rejection now becomes `TrustViolation`. It caches nothing, holds no record and deposits no seed.
  - Before this change, a failed recovery still gave `Current` with a held record, so the new behaviour is stricter.
  - In `rotation.rs`, a rejection still maps to `Rejected`, and a seam error still maps to `Unavailable`.
- **Share accept (`grants/accept.rs`):** The re-ack needs `sequence == floor` now, so a replay below the floor is not acked.
- **Drain (`sync/drain.rs`):**
  - A refusal now takes `Halt::RecordRefused`, which uses the attempt budget.
  - No path re-authors on last-known-good after a refusal.
  - The dead letter uses the existing `AttemptsExhausted` reason, so the durable queue format does not change.
- **Settings plane:** `DefaultsReason::is_verdict` is the same match as the old `bin_load_is_a_verdict`. The new report only emits an event. It does not change which placement or which settings a load selects.
- **Determinism:** The diff adds no clock or random-number call.

## Correctness note (not a vulnerability)

The equal-floor recovery now reports an `open_seeds` failure as a trust violation. I checked the risk that an honest record gets this report:

- The record at the floor is one that this device already adopted through the same kind of adopter.
- On adopt, the gate runs the same `open_seeds` and already treats this failure as a rejection.

For these reasons, an honest record does not reach this path.</result>

**PR:** https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/1921, branch `feat/1911-lagging-read-arm`, head `9c2fe1795` (one commit, unsigned, no attribution trailer; the trailer count is 0). The passed checks include Rust Fmt + clippy, Workspace tests, Core KATs (native + WASM), Engine simulation tests, Adapter tests on macOS and Windows, Web Client Browser Suite, Web E2E Smoke, Contract Suite, Tracker Refs and lint-pr-title.

## Design of the arm
- **Where it runs:** in `resolve_child` (`crates/engine/src/net/child.rs`). It starts when the gate gives `EpochBelowFloor`, on the adopt leg or on the at-floor re-open. The content read (`read_content`, `file_versions`, `read_version_content`) and the focus refresh both use it.
- **Anchor:** the arm takes the scope root record from the snapshot cache first, and from the record plane second. The floors identify the gated root: its sequence must equal the floor of the root name, and its epoch must be at or above the read-epoch floor. The facade gives the root name through the new `scope_root_record_name` helper.
- **Recipient case:** the recipient path does not write the shared scope root to the snapshot cache, so the record-plane fallback is necessary for a share recipient.
- **Seed walk:** the new `lagging_read_seed` in `rotation/reseal.rs` is the one seed walk. The drain calls it through `seed_for_lagging`, which now only maps a miss to a `Halt`; that is my only change in `drain.rs`. The sweep's interior read also calls it now.
- **Open, then floors:** the arm opens the record with `open_interior_under`. It then writes the bytes to the cache and raises the per-name sequence floor (ADR 0021 D4). No read-epoch floor moves.
- **Anchor guard:** the anchor must be strictly newer than the record. The AEAD on the first history link binds the held seed to the anchor epoch. Thus a stale or mismatched anchor gives a miss, and never a wrong seed.
- **Focus leg:** in `net/focus.rs`, I removed `rejection_verdict`, its silent `EpochBelowFloor =&gt; None` skip and the "nobody is accused" doc. Every rejection that is still returned is now `Rejected` and sends a trust-violation event.
- **Floor doc:** the `Strictness::AtOrAboveFloor` doc names the three readers. The other docs link to it.

**Behaviour change:** the old test `an_epoch_lagged_focus_folder_rejects_without_raising_abuse` expected a lagging folder to stay pinned to its last-known-good copy. I changed it to expect that the folder renders.

## Error mapping
| Outcome | Class |
|---|---|
| No gated anchor held, or the anchor is not newer than the record | ContentUnavailable |
| No held history link reaches the epoch | ContentUnavailable |
| `EpochBelowFloor` but the record bytes are not held | ContentUnavailable |
| The record carries a grant section | TrustViolation |
| The sequence is below the replay bar | TrustViolation |
| The AAD check fails under the reached seed (relabelled epoch) | TrustViolation |
| Transplant (wrong id or scope) | TrustViolation |

## Tests and mutation results
- **`write_plane.rs`:** a cut, then reads with no sweep, on the device that cut and on a second device. The content, the version list and a prior version read correctly. The read-epoch floor does not move. The sequence floor holds the record's sequence.
- **`mount_convergence.rs`:** the recipient of a share reads a lagging file after the owner cuts. The focus refresh lists a lagging folder and sends no abuse event.
- **`child.rs`:** 9 new unit tests:
  - the ratchet reaches the epoch;
  - above the anchor, or no link reaches the epoch;
  - an anchor that no history link binds to the held seed;
  - a relabelled epoch;
  - grant section, replay bar and transplant;
  - a pre-cut cached root anchors nothing;
  - with no anchor, every resolve path gives unavailable;
  - a lagging record that carries a grant section stays a trust violation;
  - the arm anchors on the cached scope root.
- **Other new tests:** one in `reseal/tests.rs` (the walk tells an above-anchor record from an unreachable one) and one in `focus.rs` (a lagging folder with no anchor is unreachable and not accused).
- **Mutation checks:** 11 mutants, and each one made at least one test fail:
  - disable the arm;
  - drop the sequence floor raise;
  - drop the cache write;
  - map "unreachable" to a trust violation;
  - skip the anchor floor check;
  - skip the cached anchor;
  - relax the anchor-newer guard;
  - change the drain's miss mapping;
  - remove the above-anchor check in the shared walk.

One mutant was equivalent: an unseal of the anchor root body. I removed that check, because the floor check and the link AEAD already cover the same case.

**Local gates (all pass):** `cargo fmt --all --check`, workspace clippy with `-D warnings`, all `cipherbox-engine` tests, the wasm32 check, and `pnpm lint:tracker-refs`.

## Review summaries (from `/tmp/w19/lane-G23/review.md`)
- **/simplify** (4 foreground agents):
  - Fixed: the facade now uses the existing `scope_name` parser; I removed repeated docs and four repeated comments; I merged two test helpers.
  - Skipped: a shared anchor loader and a shared `Anchor` type with the drain. They need edits in `drain.rs` outside `seed_for_lagging`, and lane G24 owns those functions.
  - Skipped: a per-scope anchor cache for the focus leg.
- **/code-review** (Standards and Spec agents, foreground):
  - Standards: no hard violations. I fixed the "three readers" text so it is stated only once. I rejected the shared `Anchor` type (G24 owns that code) and the shared test chain builder (the two fixtures use different seeds and versions).
  - Spec: nothing is missing. I kept the sweep's use of the shared walk: both sweep callers refuse `v != ENVELOPE_V` first, so the version in the AAD does not change.
- **/security-review** (foreground agent): no findings at or above the threshold. It checked anchor authenticity, revocation, the floor law and the D5 mapping.
- **crypto-privacy-reviewer** (foreground):
  - No critical or high findings.

## Things left, and why
- **Anchor load per lagging child:** in a focus pass, each lagging child loads the anchor again, which is one more root head fetch for each child. This is an availability cost only, and only between a cut and the sweep. I accepted it and did not file an issue.
- **Forgery-window residual:** the crypto pass says that the write-plane forgery window (CONTEXT.md "Forgery window") now also reaches readers. A revoked writer can publish at an old name before the name wave rotates that name. ADR 0021 accepts this together with the sweep, and the name wave bounds it. ADR 0021's trust argument does not name this case. Changing the ADR is a decision for the owner.
- **Cold start after a vault-root cut (not changed):** a second device that cold-starts after a cut of the vault root holds no read seed until its first tick. I saw this in a test and did not investigate it, because it is outside this lane. The test boots the second device before the cut.
- **Shared file with lane G24:** in `sync/drain.rs` I changed only the body of `seed_for_lagging` and the `use crate::rotation::{...}` import. `facade.rs` has new code near `nodes_in_scope`, in `resolve_versions`, and at the two `FolderRefresh` sites. I did not change `focus_scope_roots`.

No timers or background jobs are running.</result>

## Results by item

**Item 1: `ReceivedShareStatus::open` (fixed).**
- A failed adopt now returns the rejection. The pass sends a trust-violation event and the row becomes `Unresolvable`. The equal-floor arm stays.
- The review found two more refusals in the same function, and I fixed them. A body at the floor that does not open is now a stage 6 refusal. A scope root at another node id is now reported.
- Tests show that each refusal is reported and that nothing renders. A seam failure that stops the adoption from committing accuses nobody.
- Mutation: each of the four mutants I ran made a test fail.

**Item 2: record below the sequence floor (fixed, owner option B).**
- The row stays `Unresolvable` and the pass sends a trust-violation event. There is no new `ResolutionClass`. The check now uses the floor law's own `check_sequence`.
- This is wider than the brief: `facts_from` now also reports a stage 2 commitment refusal (forged or superseded by a cut) and a scope transplant. The PR body states this.
- Tests: a rollback is reported, and a record at the floor is not. An unreadable floor or a cut the store cannot record accuses nobody.
- Mutation: each mutant made a test fail.

**Item 3: drain (fixed).**
- In `resolved_bytes`, `load_child_node` and `open_for_reauthor`, a refusal now sends a trust violation and takes the new `Halt::RecordRefused`. That halt uses the attempt budget, so the op dead-letters after 5 attempts and not after about 1 hour.
- **Durable queue:** the dead letter uses the existing `AttemptsExhausted` reason. I added no new reason tag, so the queue still decodes the records of the previous release.
- The lagging leg (`open_interior_under`) stays `UploadAttempt`. Its seed comes from a history-link walk. The gate checks each link, but not their order, so an unseal failure there cannot tell a forged body from a wrong seed.
- Tests: a rolled-back folder and a cached copy below the floor are reported and dead-letter. A dark record plane waits and accuses nobody.
- Mutation: each of the six drain mutants made a test fail.

**Item 4: `facade.rs` (fixed).** I removed the inverted rule 6 citation from two doc comments.

**Item 5a: equal-floor own-root recovery (fixed).**
- `recover_own_scope_material` now returns a `GateError` when its re-check fails. The resolve tick then reports `TrustViolation` and does not keep `Current`. Rotation keeps its `Rejected` verdict.
- Tests: rejections at the sequence stage and the epoch stage. The resolve gives `TrustViolation`. The control is our own root at the floor, which gives `Current`.
- Mutation: each mutant made a test fail.

**Item 5b: settings plane (fixed, then narrowed).**
- At start and at the running re-check, the engine now sends a trust violation for `RolledBack` and `RevisionRolledBack`.
- The crypto-privacy review found that a body from a newer release also gives `Unreadable`, so a newer device would be accused. I do not report `Unreadable` for settings.
- `bin_load_is_a_verdict` is now `DefaultsReason::is_verdict`.
- Tests: a replayed record is reported at start and at the re-check. A missing record and a body from a newer release accuse nobody.
- Mutation: each mutant made a test fail.

**Item 5c: drain unseal-stage rejection (justified, no change).** That rejection is a record that verified and cleared both floors, so a record stands at the name. Only the name-retire decision reads it. No trust verdict is dropped.

**Item 5d: `grants/accept.rs` (fixed).**
- The engine re-acks a bookmarked share only when `sequence == floor`. A replay below the floor goes back as a gate rejection. The inbox reports it and keeps the item.
- Tests: the replayed redelivery is reported and kept. A normal redelivery is retired and accuses nobody.
- Mutation: the mutant made a test fail.

**Item 5e: `walk_verdict` (justified, no change).** `Superseded` is the local copy of the root, or a root named by the index, that trails a floor a rotation raised. It converges, as ADR 0003 D2 and the blueprint "sweep" section state.

I did not touch `net/focus.rs`, `net/child.rs`, `gate/floor.rs` or `seed_for_lagging`. **Lane G23 and this PR both change `sync/drain.rs`.**

<result>The Greptile finding was real. I fixed it and pushed the fix to PR #1917. CI is green, and Greptile now rates the PR 5/5. The PR stays ready. I did not merge it, change draft state or request a bot review.

**Verdict and mechanism.** The P1 is correct. When `repair_child_scope_index` lost the CAS race, the pass did not record the failure. So `worth_another_pass()` returned false. The idle job then recorded the scope as converged at its epoch and skipped it until the epoch increased. If the writer that won the race did not repair the index, the missing child-scope entry stayed missing.

**Fix.** The fix is in `crates/engine/src/rotation/sweep.rs`, where the rule lives:
- A new field, `SweepOutcome::index_repair_lost_race`, is set when the repair loses the CAS.
- `worth_another_pass()` counts that field. The idle job therefore keeps the scope due, and a later round derives the index again and publishes it.
- The value from the last pass is kept across passes, like the other re-derived buckets.
- `run_sweep` also runs the pass again for the same reason.
- A scope that truly converged stays skipped.

**Tests.** They are in `crates/engine/src/rotation/sweep/tests.rs`. The test fake in `crates/engine/src/rotation/sweep/sim.rs` has a new setting, `index_repair_lost_race_next`, that makes the next repair lose the CAS.
- `an_index_repair_that_lost_the_cas_is_not_flagged_and_is_worth_another_pass` runs one pass, as the idle job does. It replaces the old lost-CAS test and does all its old checks.
- `the_driver_re_runs_a_lost_index_repair_until_it_lands` checks that the repair lands on the second pass.

**Mutation check.** I reverted each of the three changed lines in turn: the check in `worth_another_pass`, the flag set on a lost race, and the carry-over across passes. Each revert made at least one of the new tests fail.

**Review skills.** I ran both in my own context, with their agents in the foreground.
- `simplify`:
  - Applied: I merged the one-pass check into the existing lost-CAS test, gave the fake's setting a name that matches `lost_race_next`, and shortened the new field's doc comment.
  - Altitude: the agent confirmed that the fix is at the correct place.
  - Skipped: findings about code in earlier commits, which CodeRabbit already reviewed with no findings. These are round costs in `owned_sweep_targets` (small at a 900 s cadence), `SweepRun` against `Option`, the two related options in `SweepTarget`, the `sweep_task_factory` wrapper, and reuse of test helpers in `mount_convergence.rs`.
- `code-review`, from `origin/main`, with #1911 Part 1 and the owner decision on #1886 as the spec:
  - Standards: no rule violations. I shortened one test doc comment. The other design-smell findings are in earlier commits, and I skipped them.
  - Spec: the fix meets the convergence rules. Part 2 agrees with the owner decision that a write grantee may restore a prior version.
  - Rejected: the claim that a second owner device never sweeps the vault root. At every start, `sync/boot.rs:222` (`cold_seed_checked`) raises the root read-epoch floor from the vault pointer. Also, the device that did the cut runs the job.

**CodeRabbit.** The web review had no nitpicks and no "Outside diff range" items.

<result>All three findings on PR #1919 are fixed and pushed as one commit (5a1d7f801, 18:48 UTC). Every CI check passed.

**Setup.** I reset the worktree to c7eb783be and merged `origin/main` (#1914, #1917) with no force push. The merge had no conflicts. On the merged tree, the engine suite, clippy and the wasm32 check pass.

## Findings

**1. Greptile P1 "Floor errors skip repair": confirmed.**
- **Mechanism:** in `catch_up_vault_pointer`, a floor read error returned with no event. A test proved it on the old code.
- **Fix:** the catch-up reads the floor first. A read error now emits `RenewalFailed` at the vault-pointer name. I widened the `RenewalFailed` doc to name this case.
- **Later attempt:** there is no automatic retry. In the same session, a `RotateNow` on the vault root now completes the vouch (see finding 2). Until a vouch lands, the next cold start of this device refuses. That lockout stays in #1920.
- **Test:** `a_start_whose_floor_read_fails_surfaces_it_and_a_later_cut_vouches`. It fails the catch-up's floor read (read budget 2): the event comes, a later cut vouches the adopted epoch and mints no new root, and the device starts again.
- **Mutation:** making the floor error silent fails this test.

**2. Greptile P1 "Command retries recut roots": confirmed.**
- **Mechanism:** after a failed vouch, a second `RotateNow` published epoch 3 over the published epoch 2. A test proved it on the old code.
- **Fix:** I removed the per-command `landed` marker. `flat_root_cut` now gates the vault root, then reads the vault pointer. When the gated root epoch is above the vouched `minReadEpoch`, it vouches that epoch, raises the floor, enqueues the lazy wave, and mints no new root. If the vault pointer cannot be read, or names another root, the cut refuses before it publishes a root. There is no new durable record kind, no seam change and no wire change.
- **Tests:**
  - `a_retried_cut_of_the_vault_root_completes_the_outstanding_vouch`: the first command publishes N and fails the vouch; the retry vouches N, mints no N+1, and the device restarts.
  - `a_cut_that_cannot_read_the_anchor_publishes_no_root`.
- **Mutations:**
  - Outstanding check turned off: 4 tests fail.
  - Root-name check turned off: 1 test fails.
  - Rule-8 refusal turned off: a new facade unit test, `a_vouch_below_the_durable_floor_publishes_nothing`, fails. The old test could no longer reach that refusal, because the gate now rejects first.
- **Behaviour change you should know about:** each retry runs the gated resolve again, and that resolve raises this device's floor before the vouch lands. The resolve tick does the same in production, so the old marker only protected a harness with the loops parked. The crypto reviewer confirmed this is the #1920 class. I therefore moved the "converge" part of the old never-landed test to a second device, whose start catch-up does the vouch.

**3. CodeRabbit Minor "Bound the polling loop": fixed.** The loop is now a shared helper, `settle_on_the_clock`. It stops after 64 steps and fails with a message that gives the limit and the step size. The CodeRabbit review body has no "Nitpick" or "Outside diff range" items, so I posted no nitpick disposition comment. I read the Greptile summary; it has only the two P1 findings.

<result>Sanity check complete: 0 unresolved threads of 1 total, PR is READY and `CLEAN` (not behind main), branch unchanged at `957d7f6e` with a clean working tree, no `Co-Authored-By` or "Generated with" text on any branch commit, and all 37 CI checks on that commit are pass or skipping.

## Report

**CodeRabbit finding — verdict: rejected, with evidence.**

The finding claimed the queue head is never charged when `open_pass` rejects a grafted root. I read `drain_queue`, `pass`, `publish_queue`, `open_pass`, `apply_valve`, `resolve_scope_root`, `resolved_bytes` and `queued_ops` on the PR branch. The uncharged path is real, but the fix as asked is wrong on two counts.

1. **It is not new.** `publish_queue` propagates every `open_pass` failure with `?` at `drain.rs:1777`, above the `apply_valve` loop, and `drain_queue` discards the result at `drain.rs:1735` (`let _ = self.pass(...)`). `origin/main` holds both lines unchanged. The PR touches only the halt variant `resolved_bytes` returns, from `Halt::Unclassified` to `Halt::RecordRefused`. The grafted framing is also inaccurate: the non-grafted branch, `load_scope_root`, is uncharged in exactly the same way.
2. **The charge would destroy user work.** `ATTEMPT_BUDGET` states its own rule at `drain.rs:577` — it "is spent only by a halt the valve could attribute to these bytes". A refused scope root is not attributable to the head op. The `Halt::RecordRefused` arm of `apply_valve` calls `preserve_dead_letter` and `retire_unreferenced_name` on that op, so five passes would dead-letter a correctly authored operation because the scope owner published a root the gate refuses, then the next op, until the scope's whole queue becomes dead letters.

The stall is narrow and no longer silent: `queued_ops` partitions by `scope.enc_secret` at `drain.rs:2077`, so only the grafted scope's own ops wait, and `refuse_record` sends an `AttributableAbuse` event on every drain.

**Change made:** none. No new dead-letter reason tag was needed, so the stop-and-report rule did not trigger. Push-back posted in the inline thread (`#discussion_r4055393554`), thread resolved.

**Nitpick disposition:** the CodeRabbit review body carries no `Nitpick comments` and no `Outside diff range` section. I posted a disposition comment recording that, the one rejected finding with its reason, and the gate table (`#issuecomment-5746410793`).

**Greptile:** Confidence Score 5/5. A GraphQL `reviewThreads` query returns one thread in total, the CodeRabbit one. Greptile left no inline comment.

**Gates, all on `957d7f6e`:** `cargo fmt --all --check` pass; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` pass; `cargo clippy -p cipherbox-engine --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings` pass; `cargo test -p cipherbox-engine` pass, 2551 tests, 0 failed; `pnpm lint:tracker-refs` pass over 1042 files.

**CI:** all green on `957d7f6e`, including `Web E2E Smoke` (pass, 2m51s), so no re-run was needed.

**Push, rebase, re-review:** none of the three happened, and none were warranted. No code change means no push, and `mergeStateStatus` is `CLEAN`, so the PR is not behind main. Greptile was therefore not re-triggered; its existing 5/5 stands on the current head. No CodeRabbit review was requested and the CLI was not run.

**Left open:** the `open_pass` charge policy itself. A refused scope root stalls that scope's queue indefinitely, bounded only by the scope owner republishing a good root or the member dropping the grant. That is `main`'s behaviour and I judged it correct, but if you want a ceiling on that stall it needs its own decision and its own dead-letter semantics, not a reuse of `ATTEMPT_BUDGET`. Say the word and I will file it.
